# Irandam Tiruvandhadi

## 1. Anbe tagaliya

*anbē tagaḷiyā ārvamē neyyāga inburugu sindai idu tiriyā nanburugi jñānac cudar viḷakku ēṟṟinēn nāraṇaṟku jñānat tamiḻ purinda nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

jñānam tamiḻ – the tamiḻ work which gives knowledge; purinda – one who composed; nān – adiyēn (servitor); anbē – bhakti (devotion); tagaḷiyā – as container (lamp, for ghee); ārvamē – parabhakti (a stage of devotion wherein one gains knowledge about emperumān); neyyāga – as ghee (clarified butter); inbu urugu sindai – the mind which melts due to happiness; idu tiriyā – as the wick which is placed in the container for holding ghee; nanbu – ātmā (soul) which is jñānasvarūpi (the epitome of knowledge); urugi – being melted; jñānam – parajñāna (ability to have darśan (vision) of emperumān); sudar viḷakku – the radiant lamp; nāraṇaṟku – for nārayaṇa; ēṟṟinēn – I lit

**Перевод:**

**anbē tagaḷiyā – in mudal tiruvandādi, poygai āḻvār had mercifully said “vaiyam tagaḷiyā” (entire expanse of the world as holding vessel). bhūdattāḻvār thinks of the entire expanse of the earth through his devotion and makes that devotion itself as the container. Since he has already heard the pāsuram “vaiyam tagaḷiyā” \[sung by poygai āḻvār\] he is transforming his devotion towards emperumān as the container and is lighting it. ḍoesn’t the oil \[ghee\] need a base \[for holding it\]? \[the word snēham means both oil and devotion in samskrutam\]. Hence he is terming devotion \[anbu also means snēham\] as the base \[container\] for oil. Devotion which cannot be destroyed by anything became container. This is the base for other things which follow. The knowledge, mentioned earlier \[previous prabandam\], fructifies fully into devotion. īsn’t knowledge lending itself to mature into devotion! īsn’t that the reason why nammāḻvār mercifully said in tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1 “mayarvaṟa madinalam aruḷinan ” (madinalam is knowledge matured into bhakti).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is mercifully mentioning about his servitorśip born out of the affection and other qualities that he had towards emperumān, who is apt to be attained, and also about emperumān’s role as the Lord. Just as was mentioned in the mudal tiruvandādi, here too āḻvār is establishing emperumān through deduction. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ārvamē neyyāga** – Once that bhakti matures, keeping that bhakti as the causative factor for enjoying [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), without which āḻvār cannot sustain himself,  his parabhakti (knowledge about emperumān) became the base \[container\] for ghee. *ārvam* (parabhakti) is the second stage of devotion. Affection is taken as being involved with emperumān due to snēham. parabhakti  is that stage where one is constantly thinking of emperumān and will be unable to move away from him. Alternatively, affection is having compassion towards emperumān and parabhakti is the involvement with emperumān as a result of that compassion. Hasn’t āṇḍāḷ mercifully said in nācciyār tirumoḻi 4-8 “*āval anbudaiyār tam manattanṟi mēvalan*” (emperumān cannot tolerate anything other than the hearts of those who have compassion and involvement)!

**inburugu idu sindai idu tiriyā** – the mind which transforms because of enjoyment  of bhagavat viśayam (relating to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) on account of affection and involvement, became the wick for that lamp. Since emperumān is apt to be attained and has auspicious qualities, the mind keeps meditating on him constantly, melts and being sweet, the mind became the wick.  īsn’t mind the reason for thinking!

**nanburugi** – nanbu refers to the darmabhūta jñānam of ātmā \[dharma bhūta jñānam is knowledge of ātmā by which it is able to sense everything which happens in all parts of the body even though ātmā is infinitesimally small and is located inside the heart\]. brahma sūtram 2-3-29 says “*tadguṇasāratvāt tadvyapadēśa: prājñavat*” (for ātmā, since jñānam (knowledge) as a characteristic is the essence, it is referred to as jñānam itself, just as a person is called jñānavān (one with knowledge)). Alternatively, that which has a benefit is called *nanbu* and *nanbu* can refer to ātmā. The benefit to ātmā is that it is distinct from acētana entity which is fickle and unsteady, it is identified by knowledge and joyousness, it is always present, and it is a servitor to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). When it is said that it (ātmā) melts, it means that it cannot be cut, it cannot be burnt, it cannot be wet, yet when it comes to matter relating to emperumān it melts and becomes a liquid like material. Since ātmā, which is always present, melts, it means that it involves totally with emperumān and sinks.  Has it not been said in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-7-39 “*na vivēda ānmanō gātram tat smrutyāhlāda samstita:*” (prahlāda did not realise that his body was being bitten by snakes as he was thinking about emperumān and was joyful)! Thus, with all these, the different stages of devotion to emperumān have been mentioned. The order of these states is that initially it is parabhakti, then parajñānam and then paramabhakti.

**jñānac cudal viḷakku ēṟṟinēn** – with the mercy of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) I lit the lamp which is brightly burning parajñānam. Lamp will show itself and will also show other objects. In the same way, bhakti, which is matured knowledge, will show its śēśatvam (being a servitor) and the śēśitvam of emperumān (being the master).

**viḷakku ēṟṟinēn** – Once the servitorśip is known, since only kainkaryam (service) is to be carried out, I carried out service in this manner.

**nāraṇaṟku** – to [nārāyaṇnan](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) who is the Lord of all. Through this, it is mentioned that carrying out service to any inapt entity is avoided.

**jñānat tamiḻ purinda nān** –  I who composed the tamiḻ prabandam which is the personification of knowledge. As mentioned in vēdārta sangraham  “*bhaktiśca jñānaviśēśa:*” (bhakti is a classification of knowledge), the prabandam, created by me, gives jñanam which matures into devotion.

**jñānat tamiḻ purinda nān** –  the prabandam that I had composed, apart from being without any mistake in tamiḻ grammar, is also capable of granting mōkśam.

**jñānat tamiḻ purinda nān** **jñānac cuddar viḷakku ēṟṟinēn** – I who can be trusted, said, just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said of himself as being with lot of desire, in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-1-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruvaimozhi-2-1-11-soratha-epporutkum/) “*kādal kurugūrc catakōpan sonna*”. As said in śrī rāmāyaṇam 2-40 “*śōka: ślōkātvamāgata:*” (sorrow was brought out as ślōkam (hymn)), knowledge was brought out as knowledgeable tamiḻ. I composed this pāsuram so that bhakti will manifest itself resplendently to all the people of this world, instead of ending with me. āḻvār is divinely saying this prabandam so that the sākśātkāram (ability to see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) that he obtained, will be available to those who learn this prabandam. Here onward, he is mentioning about his bhakti, his involvement, the sweetness of emperumān which is the cause for those two, the aptness with which the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) was to think of his servitorśip, the servitorśip that he had towards emperumān etc.

We shall move on to the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 2. Jnanattal nangunarndu

*jñānattāl nanguṇarndu nāraṇan tan nāmangaḷ dānattāl maṟṟu avan pēr sāṟṟināl vānattu aṇi amarar ākkuvikkum ahdanṟē nangaḷ paṇiyamarar kōmān parisu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nāraṇan than – śrīman nārāyaṇan’s; nāmangaḷ – distinct divine names (which refer to his svarūpa guṇam (qualities of his basic nature)); maṟṟu – and; avan pēr – his divine names (which refer to his expansive wealth); jñānattāl – with knowledge (which is the epitome of bhakti); nangu uṇarndu – knowing very well; dānattāl – standing in the position (of the boundary of bhakti); sāṟṟināl – if meditated upon; nangaḷ paṇi amarar kōman parisu – the quality of emperumān who is the lord of nityasūris, who are our friends and who are servitors of emperumān; vānattu aṇi amarar ākkuvikkum ahdenṟē – will make us to be on a par with nityasūris who are like decorations to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)

**Перевод:**

**nāraṇan than nāmangaḷ maṟṟu avan pēr dānattāl sāṟṟināl – if the distinctive divine names of emperumān which refer to the qualities of his basic nature and his divine names which refer to his aspects \[wealth\] are recited with bhakti, from an exalted state**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that those who have such bhakti \[as described in the first pāsuram\], even if they recite the divine names of emperumān in a disorderly way, and even if they are permanent samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm), emperumān will make them on a par with nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**jñānattāl nangu uṇarndu** – knowing very well through bhakti (it is difficult to know without bhakti). Even though both dritarāśtra \[father of the 100 kauravas starting with duryōdana\] and sanjaya \[his charioteer\] studied \[in gurukula, school of learning from an ācāryan\] together, only sanjaya had devotion \[towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]. dritarāśtra learnt only the lines \[in the lessons\]. dritarāśtra asked of sanjaya “while both of us studied only in the same place, what is the reason that you have knowledge while I do not have it?” sanjaya responded saying “unlike you who learnt only the lines, did I not learn with bhakti!”

**nangu uṇarndu** – when reciting his divine names, we should know very well the relationship between [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and us. In other worlds, we should know his qualities and our qualities; that he is the lord and we are his servitors.

**nāraṇan than nāmangaḷ** – (nārāyaṇa is also a divine name. Why then say “*nārāṇan than nāmangaḷ?*”) the divine name nārāyaṇa is distinct from other divine names. Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s *jñāna, ānanda etc* (knowledge, bliss etc) are considered as identities of his basic nature, and other characteristics such as *jñana, śakti* etc (knowledge, power etc) as identities of his qualities, [tirumantram](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) (nārāyaṇa) is considered as an identity of his basic nature while other names are considered as divine names. Haven’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 9-3-1 “*pēr āyiram koṇḍadōr pīdudaiyan*” (the strong one with a thousand names) and [pēyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his mūnṟām tiruvandādi “*nāmam pala solli nārāyaṇa*” (reciting multitude names) divinely mentioned the same!

**nāraṇan than nāmangaḷ** – the divine names of the apt Lord, emperumān

**dānattāl** –  with bhakti. Reciting for obtaining other benefits \[than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself\]. Even if there is no need for other benefits, unless these are recited, it will not be possible to sustain oneself, hence reciting these names. That it is not possible to sustain oneself without reciting the names indicates the boundary of bhakti. The next stage to this is reciting emperumān’s divine names with ecstasy.

**maṟṟu** – this could be considered either as a metrical syllable or as reciting the divine name above the position of bhakti.

**avan pēr** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names which refer to his wealth and qualities.

**avan pēr** – since it has been mentioned as *pēr* (name) instead of mentioning as *tirunāmam* (divine name) this implies that there is no need for any specific propriety or ritual to recite these names and that they can be recited in any manner (even if it is not orderly). Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too divinely mentioned in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-5-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thiruvaimozhi-3-5-8-var-punal/) “*pēr pala sollip pidaṟṟi*” (babbling the names)! *nangu uṇarndu sāṟṟudal* refers to realising with the heart and reciting the divine names through the mouth.

**sāṟṟināl** – reciting the divine names such that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s enemies will become disgusted and his followers will become very happy on hearing these divine names.

What will [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) do if the divine names are recited?

**vānattu aṇiyamarar ākkuvikkum ahdenṟē** – emperumān will make paramapadam to feel proud due to association with such a samsāri \[who recites the divine names of emperumān\] and keeping the samsāri as an equal to nityasūris. An alternative explanation – taking such samsāris to paramapadam and making them as decorative objects for those who reside there (nityasūris). Hasn’t nammāḻvār too divinely mentioned in his [tiruvāimoḻi 4-2-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thiruvaimozhi-4-2-1-balanay-ezhulagundu/) “*malipugaḻ vānavarkkāvar naṟkōvaiyē*” (they will complement nityasūris)! Even when āḻvārs are vorśipping emperumān don’t they say as said by [kulaśēkara āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kulasekarazhwar/) in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 2-3-10 “*adiyārgaḷ kuḻāngaḷai – udan kūduvadu enṟukolō*” (when will I ever be with the group of nityasūris!).

**vānattu aṇi amarar** – the greatness of paramapadam is that it has nityasūris who are faultless. We can consider the meaning as: Reciting the divine names will take the samsāris to paramapadam which has nityasūris as decorative objects. nammāḻvār too has divinely mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 10-5-8 “*pēr Ar ōduvār ārār amararē*” (whoever recites the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will be considered as equivalent to nityasūris irrespective of their birth, clan etc).

Is emperumān giving such a high status to those who recite his divine names only because he does not have anyone who attains him?

**nangaḷ paṇiyamarar kōmān parisu** – this is the quality of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the lord of nityasūris such as ādiśēśan, garudan et al who are naturally related to us \[all are servitors of emperumān\] and who, as mentioned in mahābhāratam śanti parvam 344-45 “*nityānjaliputā hruśtā: nama ityēva vādina:*”(always vorśipping emperumān with folded hands and becoming happy on account of that, and always saying *nama:* (I am not for me \[ I am only for emperumān\])).

**paṇiyamarar** – doing service is natural to them \[nityasūris\]. It is not that they carry out service one day and refrain from doing that the next day. Being subservient to emperumān is their nature.

**paṇiyamarar** – unlike muktars (those who were samsāris once upon a time, and after being liberated, reached paramapadam) and mumūkśus (those who are desirous of reaching paramapadam) who were subservient to others for some time and then falling at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), saying  as in tirukkuṟundāṇḍagam 12 “*aḻukkudambu eccil vāyāl*” (those with dirty forms and disgusting mouths) and as in iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 10 “*kāvadiyēn patta kadai* ”(you have to rid me of all the lowliness that I suffered before becoming your servitor), these nityasūris do not have to say such things.

We shall move on to the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 3. Parisu narumalaral

*parisu naṟumalarāl pāṟkadalān pādam purivār pugappeṟuvar pōlām purivārgaḷ tollamar kēḷvit tulangoḷisēr tōṟṟattu nallamarar kōmān nagar*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāṟkadalān pādam – the divine feet of emperumān who is reclining in the milky ocean; naṟu malaral – with fragrant flowers; parisu purivār – those who worship him willingly through the means of devotion; purivārgaḷ tol amarar – the ancient dēvas (such as brahmā et al) who are sādanānuśtāna parar (those who are trying to reach emperumān through their own efforts); kēḷvi – that which can only be heard of (and not be seen); tulangu oḷi sēr tōṟṟattu – with resplendent radiance; nal amarar kōman nagar – paramapadam which is the huge city of the head of nityasūris; pugap peṟuvar pōlām – they will attain, it seems!

**Перевод:**

**naṟu malarāl – with beautiful, fragrant flowers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said “vānattaṇiyamarar ākkuvikkum” (samsāris would be made equivalent to nityasūris). In this pāsuram, he is amazed that samsāris of this world, using the flowers available in this world and vorśipping his divine feet here, would reach the śrīvaikuṇṭam of emperumān, who is the Lord of the indefatigable nityasūris. We can construe this as either āḻvār is speaking with involvement about the activity that emperumān is going to undertake for his followers or as āḻvār is speaking about emperumān’s followers reaching paramapadam even as emperumān is waiting for his followers here. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pāṟkadalān pādam** – īsn’t it to enable people like us who can go and worship his divine feet that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is reclining in a simple manner at tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) which is the origin for all the incarnations of emperumān! Among the four forms of emperumān viz.  vāsudēvan, sankarśaṇan, pradyumnan and aniruddan, he is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal as the junior-most, aniruddan. The divine feet of such emperumān who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal, who is constantly engaged in reaching samsāris to the other shore, paramapadam.

**parisu purivār** – those who are trying to attain emperumān as per the way showed by śāstras (sacred texts) – viz. those who are trying to attain emperumān through the path of *bhakti* (devotion)

**pugappeṟuvar pōlām** – it appears that such people will attain him! There is no fault in the means to attain him. Only those who do not attain him will be the losers.

**pugappeṟuvar pōlām** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is mentioning, in such a way as to show his displeasure that he is yet to attain paramapadam, of those who would attain paramapadam.

Why is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) taking those who attain him, to paramapadam? Is it that he doesn’t have anyone who is attaining him? No, there are many dēvas (celestial entities) who are trying to attain him. Who are these dēvas?

**purivārgaḷ tol amarar** – those who are trying to attain emperumān through their own efforts. The old dēvas such as brahmā et al, who are reciting his divine names, carrying out penance and growing matted hair.

**tol amarar** – very old dēvas. Those who are older than us by a few days.

**kēḷvi** – even such dēvas would have only heard of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s dwelling place of śrīvaikuṇṭam and would not have seen it. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-55 says “*yam na dēvā munaya: nacāham na ca Sankara: jānanti paramēśasya tad viṣṇō: paramam padam*” (brahmā says that “that is the supreme dwelling place of paramēśvara viṣṇu (supreme Lord) which dēvas, riśis (sages), Sankara and I have not been able to know a little bit about”).

**tuḷangu oḷi sēr tōṟṟattu** – mahābhāratam āraṇya parvam 136-18 says “*atyarkkānala dīptam tat stānam*” (that paramapadam is brighter than sun and fire). That paramapadam appears as such an immeasurable bright place. śvētāsvatara upaniśat 3-8 says “*āditya varṇam tamasa: parastāt*” (having the brightness of sun, located beyond the world of tamas (total darkness or ignorance)). nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-2 says “*mēltōnṟum sōdi vēda mudalvar*” (the supreme being who is referred to by vēdas (sacred texts) who dwells in that place which is beyond all the other places). This is how paramapadam has been referred to \[in a few examples\].

**nal amarar kōmān nagar** – the dwelling place of the Lord of nityasūris such as *periya tiruvadi* (garudan), *tiruvanantāḻvān* (ādiśēśan) who are the naturally residing, permanent dwellers there unlike muktars who have stayed in samsāram for some time and then due to the grace of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) sever their connection with samsāram and reach there in their own order.

**nal amarar kōmān** – the carateristic of emperumān is that he is the Lord of those nityasūris who enjoy their goal and who cannot sustain themselves if they are separated from emperumān, unlike dēvas who observe certain means on their own for some time. parapapadam is the dwelling place for nityasūris who give emperumān his characteristic quality. Instead of saying that brahmam (supreme being) does not have any significant quality, it says that there is a divine abode for him and both he and the divine entities \[nityasūris\] reside there! It appears that those who carry out bhakti at the divine feet of emperumān with fragrant flowers would enter such paramapadam! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that such people will initially worship [arcāvatāra emperumān](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html) (dwelling in idol form in various divine abodes in samsāram), then worship him in vibhavam (various incarnations), then vyūham (at tiruppāṟkadal) and finally in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). It appears that saying like this is not typical of [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) and they are saying this as per their engagement. It is said that if one worships the junior most form, anirudda in tiruppāṟkadal, he would certainly attain paramapadam! Hence, it is certain that if one were to worship at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in any one place, he is bound to get the goal \[of reaching paramapadam\].

Let us move on to the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 4. Nagar ilaittu

*nagariḻaittu nittilattu nāṇmalar koṇḍu āngē tigaḻumaṇi vayiram sērttu nigarillāp paingamalam ēndip paṇindēn panimalarāḷ angam valam koṇḍānadi*

**Пословный перевод:**

nagar iḻaittu – making my heart as the capital for emperumān to reside; āngē nittilattu nāḷ malar koṇḍu – making affection (devotion), also termed as pearl, as a flower that has just blossomed that day; tigaḻum maṇi vayiram sērttu – keeping carbuncle and diamond as water lily and pollen respectively; nigar illā – the incomparable; paim kamalam ēndi – donning bhakti as a cool lotus flower; panimalrāḷ angam valam koṇḍān – emperumān who keeps śrīdēvi (śrī mahālakśmi), who dwells in a lotus, on his right chest; adi – divine feet; paṇindēn – I vorśipped

**Перевод:**

**nittilattu nāḷ malar koṇḍu  āngē nagar iḻaittu – The meaning for this could be construed either as referring to flower like bhakti or creating a flower which is as cool as pearl. Just as in the case of “anbē tagaḷiyā” where āḻvār referred to affection (devotion) as a simile for lamp, here too he is referring to bhakti  (devotion) as a simile for flower and is attaining emperumān with that.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram āḻvār mentioned about the bhakti (devotion) that was created in him. ōr, it could be construed as how emperumān taught him to be subservient to him. In this pāsuram, he thinks of emperumān’s simplicity and worships him without mamakāram (being possessive). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nagar iḻaittu** – keeping his heart as a dwelling place fit for emperumān. nagar is town, referred here to as temple (it is pertinent to recall that [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) has also said *‘mārvam enbadōr kōyil amaittu*’ (setting heart as a temple)).

**nittilattu nāḷ malar koṇḍu** – thinking of a cool pearl as a petal. Making a petal out of devotion.

**nittilattu nāḷ malar** – cool, fragrant flower. As said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam “*dvidābhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyam*” (even if split into two, I will not worship \[śrī rāma, said rāvaṇa\]), being unlike this is in itself cool and comfortable to the mind.

**nāḷ malar** – cool flower

**āngē** – in that mind

**tigaḻum maṇi vayiram serttu** – taking shining carbuncles and diamonds as being involved, and keeping them as water lilies and pollens. bhakti is referred to as radiant carbuncle and diamond gems.

**nigar illāp paim kamalam ēndi** – donning the incomparable meditation as flower, attained [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**paingamalam** – well spread out lotus flower. In other words, I vorśipped with the lotus of my heart. There is a simile for other lotuses. But since there is none for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/)’s lotus like heart, he is terming it as incomparable cool lotus. In this pāsuram, āḻvār is terming love as a metaphor for pearl and devotion as a metaphor for carbuncles and diamonds. Hasn’t been divinely mentioned as in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-4-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-4-9-nadatha-malar/) “*nādāda malar nādi*” (searching for flowers which are difficult to get) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-2-pusum-sandhu/) “*pūsum sāndu en nenjumē*” (the sandalwood paste to be applied to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is my heart itself)! The reason for terming bhakti as a *bhāvapuśpam* (emotional flower) is because of the maturity of bhakti resulting from the enjoyment of emperumān who is always with pirātti ([śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)i).

Who is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) attaining?

**panimarāḷ angam valam koṇḍān adi** – I fell at the divine feet of emperumān who is embracing the boundlessly sweet pirātti on his right side. Thus, he is saying that the entity to be attained is only emperumān who is with *tirumagaḷ* ([śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)). The apt person to be subservient to like this is only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The attainment, even if it is faulty, will become faultless if he is attained when she is with him. She will not allow him to analyse the good and bad deeds of the followers! She will tell him that if compassion is shown only for those with good deeds, it will create a fault in the relationship of being father and mother to the world.

We shall take up the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 5. Adi munril

*adi mūnṟil ivvulagam anṟu aḷandāy pōlum adi mūnṟu irandu avani koṇḍāy padi ninṟa nīr ōda mēni nedumālē nin adiyai yār ōda vallār aṟindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

padi ninṟa – incarnating on earth and standing on it; nīr ōdam mēni nedumālē – Oh supreme being, with the colour of ocean!; adi mūnṟu – land covered by three steps; irandu avani koṇḍāy – begging (from māvali) you got the worlds; anṟu – during that time; ivvulagam – this world; adi mūnṟil aḷandāy pōlum – did you measure with your divine feet? No; nin adiyai – your divine feet; aṟindu – knowing very well; ōda vallār yār – who is capable of speaking? \[There is none\]

**Перевод:**

**adi mūnṟil ivvulagam anṟu aḷandāy pōlum – For the sake of those who have devotion towards you, you incarnated like vāmana. In order to manifest your bias towards them, you had already decided in your divine mind that you are going to measure the world in three steps and accordingly asked for three steps of land and measured the world. In other words, he begged as if he is not accepting his possessions but is accepting someone else’s possession.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār called bhakti as lamp and town as temple. In this pāsuram, he is talking only about bhakti. ōr, we can say that he is speaking about how emperumān remains very simple for people with bhakti. āḻvār said in the previous pāsuram “nigarillāp paingamalam ēndip paṇindēn” (he carried an incomparable lotus flower and attained \[emperumān\]). emperumān asks him “Did you attain me?” āḻvār responds “I just said a word like that. I do not know whether I attained you. Is it possible for anyone to measure you?” āḻvār is asking in this pāsuram, whether the simplicity of emperumān which he mentioned in the previous pāsuram could be measured by anyone. We can also say that āḻvār is talking about the grace showered by emperumān on those who surrendered to him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**adi mūnṟu irandu aḷandu koṇḍāy** – In order to make the earth yours, thinking of measuring it, you sought three steps of land from mahābali. You measured the worlds in just two steps. For this purpose, you showed your small feet \[as vāmana\] and said that you wanted three steps of land with those feet, ascertaining what you wanted. Since you knew what was going to happen later on, seeking three steps of land and measuring in just two steps, you kept your foot atop mahābali’s head and imprisoned him \[in pātāla lōka, the nether word\]. Did anyone know this thought process of yours?

**padi ninṟa** – *padi* is bhūmi (earth). Standing on earth to protect it. ōr, *pad*i is to resemble. You stood to resemble \[your greatness\]. Another meaning for *padi* is quality. You stood to manifest your quality of simplicity.

**nīr ōda mēni nedumālē** –  Oh [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), having a beautiful form like the ocean which is lapping with waves, you are greater than everyone else.

**nedumālē** – *māl* – greatness. Oh, the one who had a great form during your trivikrama incarnation! ōr, *māl* – affection. Having lot of affection towards your devotees.

**nīr ōda mēni nedumālē** – Your form can be described as being similar to the ocean. But it is not possible to set a limit for y our affection.

**nin adiyai yār ōda vallār aṟindu** – Who can know and talk about the simplicity of your divine feet? Even after knowing that it is not possible to speak, only a few persons without any knowledge, such as I am, can speak this much. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that there is none who can speak about your divine feet which are indeterminate. Only if someone can determine that activity of yours when you went to mahābali and measured the worlds after begging him for three feet, could one determine \[the greatness of\] your divine feet. It may be possible to set a boundary for your greatness, but no boundary can be set for your simplicity.

We shall consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 6. Arindaindum ulladakki

*aṟindaindum uḷḷadakki āymalar koṇḍu ārvam seṟinda manattarāyc cevvē – aṟindu avan tan pēr ōdi ēttum perum tavattōr kāṇbarē kār ōda vaṇṇan kaḻal*

**Пословный перевод:**

aṟindu – knowing well (the greatness of emperumān and the lowliness of worldly pursuits); aindum – the five sensory perceptions; uḷ adakki – (preventing them from engaging in other pursuits and) engaging with bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to emperumān) by anchoring firmly; āy malar koṇḍu – analysing and taking the appropriate flowers (apt for emperumān); ārvan seṟinda manattaṟāy – with the heart full of bhakti (devotion); sevvē aṟindu – knowing well (the relationship of Lord – servant between emperumān and oneself); avan than pēr – the divine names of that emperumān; ōdi – constantly reciting; ēttum – vorśipping; perum tavattōr – the great, fortunate ones; kār ōdam vaṇṇan kaḻal – the divine feet of that emperumān who has the complexion of a dark sea; kāṇbar – will see and enjoy

**Перевод:**

**aṟindu – Knowing the lowly matters and the great matters. In other words, knowing well the greatness of emperumān and the lowliness of worldly matters. Knowing well the lowliness of this physical form \[one’s body\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asked in the previous pāsuram whether it is possible to know the quality of emperumān. This is not something similar to a non-existing entity beyond one’s knowledge. He says that It is not possible to estimate emperumān’s quality. In that case, is it that no one can know about it and do we have to give it up? āḻvār says that it is not like that. Those who get rid of their desires in worldly pursuits and worship him, recite his divine names, would get to see his divine feet. Those who involve themselves with emperumān fully would be fortunate to see him. We can construe it as saying that instead of their own efforts, they will get to see him out of his mercy. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aindum uḷ adakki** – ensuring they \[one’s thoughts\] do not go towards lowly matters relating to śabdam etc (the five sensory perceptions starting with sound). Pointing out to the five sensory perceptions such as śabdam etc, the [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is residing inside \[one’s heart\]. Not letting the sensory perceptions to graze outside but controlling them and showing emperumān who is inside. Making the sensory perceptions to be immersed in the beauty of emperumān just like tying a raging bull inside the house and feeding it instead of letting it to go outside.

**āy malar koṇḍu** – carrying the apt flowers for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Taking fresh flowers.

**ārvam seṟinda manattarāy** – being with affectionate mind, with involved mind.

**sevvē aṟindu** – knowing well the relationship that he is the Lord and we are the servitors.

**avan pēr ōdi ēttum perum tavattōr** – the fortunate \[or blessed\] people who recite emperumān’s divine names and worship him. Those who have it as their nature to recite [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names.

**kāṇbarē kārōda vaṇṇan kaḻal** – they will get to see the divine feet of emperumān who is having the form of an ocean which removes the tedium in the person who sees it and comforts him.

**kāṇbarē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) expresses his desire to see emperumān’s divine feet.

**kār ōda vaṇṇan** – emperumān’s divine form is such that even nityasūris would enjoy and experience it.

**kāṇbarē** – by emphasising with the long syllable (kāṇbar**E**), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is reassuring that they will certainly see \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet\]

We shall consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 7. Kalal eduttu vay madittu

*kaḻal eduttu vāy madittuk kaṇ suḻanṟu māṟṟār aḻal edutta sindaiyarāy anja taḻal edutta pōr āḻi ēndinān pon malarc cēvadiyē ōr āḻi nenjē ugandu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻi nenjē – Oh mind, which is as deep as the ocean!; kaḻal eduttu – as one who lifted his divine feet; māṟṟār – those enemies such as namuci; aḻal edutta sindaiyarāy anja – shivering with their hearts burning like fire, out of fear; vāy madittu – puckering his lips; kaṇ suḻanṟu – rolling his eyes (such that his enemies will collapse by the mere look); taḻal edutta pōr āḻi – the sudarśana cakkaram (divine disc) which is spitting fire and is battle ready; ēndinān – emperumān who is donning; pon malar cē adiyē – the divine feet which are like beautiful flower; ugandu ōr – keep meditating, with desire

**Перевод:**

**kaḻal eduttu – lifting his divine foot when he measured the world. This implies that he blessed the dēvas (celestial persons), the dwellers of upper worlds, who are his followers. On top of this, this also implies that he destroyed asuras (demons) who are not his followers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his divine mind that this being his \[emperumān’s\] nature, keep meditating on his divine feet with affection, without any break. Only those who give up the worldly pursuits and desire only him, will get to have his darśan (see him). āḻvār tells his mind, hence you too desire him and attain him. When asked whether there are any hurdles to attaining him, āḻvār says that those hurdles will suffer the fate of the demon, namuci. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings*

**vāy madittu kaṇ suḻanṟu māṟṟār aḻal edutta sindaiyarāy anja** – two interpretations are given for this: [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) donned the divine disc such that demons such as namuci were fearful with their lips puckered and their eyes rolling, and their hearts burning with the fear of fire. ōr, these qualities (of puckering the lips and rolling the eyes) could be attributed to emperumān and it could be said that he donned the divine disc which spewed scorching fire such that his enemies such as namuci et al were grieving in fear.

**pon malarc cēvadiyē** – the divine feet which are like gold, like flower and are beautiful.

**ōr** – try and meditate.

**āḻi nenjē** – Oh heart, which is deep.

When meditating on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet,

**ugandu ōr** – meditate with desire. Do not meditate with your limitations. Meditate with desire. Instead of having to meditate out of necessity, meditate with desire.

**ōr** – implies “try and attain”

We shall move on to the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

## 8. Ugandu unnai vangi

*ugandu unnai vāngi oḷi niṟangoḷ kongai agam kuḷira  uṇ enṟāḷ āvi  ugandu mulai uṇbāy pōlē munindu uṇḍāy nīyum alai paṇbāl ānamaiyāl anṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ugandu – playacting as if she is full of affection, just like yaśōdā pirātti; unnai vāngi – lifting you (when you were sleeping); oḷi niṟam koḷ kongai – beautifully radiant bosom (since it was full of milk); agam kuḷira uṇ enṟāḷ – she said “drink” to make the infant happy in its mind; ānamaiyāl – hence; anṟu – during that time when she offered her bosom to you; nīyum – you too, as an infant; alai paṇbāl – due to your \[excessive\] childishness; mulai uṇbāy pōlē – as if you are really suckling; ugandu – (showing outwardly) your happiness (as if you have attained an impossible gift); munindu – becoming very angry (in your mind); āvi – (that pūtanā”s) life; uṇḍāy – you swallowed (along with her milk)

**Перевод:**

**ugandu – since pūtanā came in the guise of a mother, she came with the affection \[put on, in her case\] like a mother would have towards her child. She came with the same affection that yaśōdā would have towards her child, but a feigned one. Even as she had anger towards kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) she hid it and came as if she had love. If she did not have love, milk would not have secreted! If she did not have the thought of having love towards her infant, milk will not secrete!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his mind that when taking refuge under emperumān, make sure that enmity is not lurking behind. It is implied here that taking refuge with falsity will be harmful. When asked whether there are hurdles in taking refuge under him, āḻvār says that when you take refuge under him, he would destroy the enemy just as he did in the case of pūtanā \[a demonic woman sent by kamsan to kill the infant kriṣṇa\]. Since he mentioned about namuci in the previous pāsuram, āḻvār thought of pūtanā also and hence mercifully mentions about her in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**unnai vāngi** – lifting you gently even as you were sleeping, just as yaśōdā pirātti will lift you gently with love.

**oḷi niṟam koḷ kongai** – the bosom which is shining radiantly with milk, similar to yaśōdā pirātti’s milk laden bosom.

**agam kuḷira uṇ enṟāḷ** – mothers, before nursing their children, would say a few loving words which would bring out their love for the children as well as remove any tiredness that the children may have.  pūtanā said similar words \[to kriṣṇa\]. She spoke a few words to comfort kriṣṇa’s heart, making it clear that he has to drink her milk. She spoke so sweetly that his stomach would get filled up with her words themselves, even without the need for drinking her milk.

**āvi** – her life

**nīyum ugandu mulai uṇbāy pōlē munindu uṇḍāy** – you also became happy and like an infant wishing to drink milk, drank her life with anger, as if you were drinking her milk. Just as she came with feigned love to kill you, you too feigned happiness while drinking her milk and, becoming angry, killed her. He \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] too did not show his anger and killed her. pūtanā gave her milk to him as if she could not have survived had she not given her milk. He also drank her milk as if he could not have survived had he not taken that milk. The term being angry while suckling is explained this way – when infants are hungry they would like to have milk immediately. If there is any delay, they will start wailing in anger. In the same way, he too was angry. The qualities of devotion and act of surrendering to him that we have would also look feigned like the feeling that pūtanā had when she came to nurse him.

**alai paṇbāl ānamaiyāl anṟu** – the way you did when pūtanā came that day to kill you, who are the beginning of this world. a*lai paṇbal* – the simplicity which a great person has. a*lai paṇbāl* – since you are an ocean of qualities. While the feigned quality of being a friend is enough to protect, you killed pūtanā and protected yourself, Lord of the world. īsn’t this because of your great qualities! īsn’t it because of the partiality that you have towards your followers that you killed pūtanā and gave yourself! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is carrying out such deeds only to sustain them. Alternatively *alai paṇbāl* could also refer to the destructive quality of pūtanā.

We shall take up the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 9. Anradu kandanjada

*anṟadu kaṇḍanjāda āycci unakkirangi ninṟu mulai tanda innīrmaikku anṟu varan muṟaiyāl nīyaḷanda mākadal sūḻ jñālam perumuṟaiyāl eydumō pērttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – during that period (when you drank her milk and killed pūtanā such that she fell down like a corpse); adu kaṇḍu – looking at pūtanā’s corpse after she had nursed you; anjāda – not fearing whether she would die if she (yaśōdā pirātti) nursed you; āycci – yaśōdā pirātti; ninṟu – sustaining herself; unakku irangi mulai tanda – nursing you, being compassionate towards you; i nīrmaikku – for this great quality; anṟu nī varan muṟailyāl aḷanda mā kadal sūḻ jñālam – this huge world surrounded by ocean, that you measured then because of your special relationship with it; pērttu perumuṟaiyāl eydumō – is it possible to compare \[this deed of yours\] to that great quality of yaśōdā pirātti’s, even if analysed repeatedly?

**Перевод:**

**anṟu adu kaṇḍu anjāda āycci – On that day when pūtanā died after nursing you. yaśōdā did not fear whether anything untoward would happen to her, but feared whether anything untoward would happen to kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is wondering as to why talk about pūtanā. He says that we should talk about the wonderful affection of yaśōdā pirātti. Is there any comparison for yaśōdā pirātti’s affection? In order to get rid of the sorrow caused by thinking about pūtanā, āḻvār talks about the activities of yaśōdā pirātti and is happy saying that yaśōdā is unique. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**unakkirangi** – she was in grief owing to the deep love that she had for you

**ninṟu mulai tanda innīrmaikku** – without fearing for herself, she protected kaṇṇan by standing firmly on the floor \[and nursing him\]

**anṟu varan muṟaiyāl** – during that time when he received the world as a gift from mahābali and measured it in a great manner. On that day when he measured the world, being the natural lord of the world. You \[emperumān\] are the mother for this world. Hence nursing you is equivalent to nursing the world. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi  3-1-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-10-maraiyaya/) “*muṟaiyāl ivvulagu ellām padaittu … aḷandāy*” (you created and measured all the worlds properly).

**mā kadal sūḻ jñālam** – this earth which is surrounded by huge ocean.

**peru muṟaiyāl eydumō pērttu** – if they are compared for the greatness of the acts, will they be equal? Measuring the worlds is a great act. yaśōdā nursing you without any fear is also a great act. āḻvār is asking whether his measuring the worlds would equal the act of yaśōdā pirātti, even if compared repeatedly. āḻvār says that if these two acts are compared, the act of yaśōdā in nursing you is a greater act than that of emperumān measuring the worlds.

**pērttup perumuṟaiyāl eydumō** – your act of measuring the worlds will not equal the characteristic of yaśōdā pirātti.

**pērttāl** – the meaning for this world can be construed as *if thought of*.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 10. Perttanai masagadam

*pērttanai māsagadam piḷḷaiyāy maṇṇirandu kāttanai palluyirum kāvalanē ēttiya nāvudaiyēn pūvudaiyēn ninuḷḷi ninṟamaiyāl kāvadiyēn patta kadai*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāvalanē – Oh, the omni-protector!; piḷḷaiyāy – as a child; mā sagadam – a huge wheel (inside which a demon had entered); pērttanai – you kicked it such that it was destroyed completely; maṇ – the world; irandu – taking alms (from mahābali); pal uyirum – all the ātmās (souls); kāttanai – mercifully protected; nin – your (such a protector); uḷḷi ninṟamaiyāl – since thinking of (as protector); ēttiya nā udaiyēn – I am having a tongue which worships you as its nature; pū udaiyēn – I am having flowers (fit for worshiping you); adiyēn – having a great status as being your servitor; patta – having suffered (attaining other deities); kadai – lowliness; kā – you have to remove

**Перевод:**

**pērttanai māsagadam piḷḷaiyāy – even when you were a little child, you destroyed a huge wheel which could not be moved, by kicking it.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says – this being the position, I should have had the devotion that yaśōdā pirātti had towards you. Even though I did not have love towards you to that extent, I had an involvement with you. He pleads with emperumān to have mercy on him so that the lowliness of having to fall at the feet of other dēvatās for eons should not recur. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mā sagadam** – huge wheel. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is afraid as to what will happen \[to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] if this wheel comes and gives him difficulties. This was an activity carried out by emperumān even before he had become a youth.

**maṇ irandu kāttanai pal uyirum** – even as mahābali seized the worlds which were your possession, you took the role of a mendicant for the sake of indra (head of celestial persons), sought the worlds as alms and with your divine feet, caressed the heads of all cētanas (sentient entities) and uplifted them.

**kāvalanē** – the reason for carrying out the \[above-mentioned\] deed is that you are the protector.  Thus, while protecting is the quality of the protector, the quality of those protected is to carry out kainkaryam (service) to the protector. His activity is that of a protector and my activity is that of a servitor.

When asked as to what he was going to carry out, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) responds saying

**nin uḷḷi ninṟamaiyāl** – since I am involved with you, who are having such a quality \[of being a protector\]

**ēttiya nā udaiyēn pū udaiyēn** – I am having a tongue which will recite hymns about you and a body with which I can carry out bodily service to you. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he has a tongue and hence he can carry out servitorśip to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**ēhtiya nā udaiyēṇ** – I have a tongue which will praise emperumān without having to wait for me to give it orders to do so.

**pū udaiyēn** – I have flowers too with which I can carry out service to you.

**nin uḷḷi niṟamaiyāḷ ēttiya nā udaiyēn pū udaiyēn** – since I attained you, I became endowed with abundance of limbs and materials with which to worship you. Since I thought of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is fit to be attained, I do not have any shortcoming. Even then, there is something which you have to do….

**kā adiyēn patta kadai** – you have to mercifully bless me, your follower, so that the lowliness that I have been accruing because of carrying out service to other deities who are not fit for those, will not come my way hereafter.

**adiyēn patta kadai  kā** – I, who am having the pride of being your servitor.  You have to mercifully ensure that I, who now have the greatness of being your servitor, do not suffer the lowliness of being the servitor to others.

**adiyēn patta kadai** – how can I describe how I suffered by falling at the feet of other deities, when I belong only to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)?

We shall move on to the 11<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 11. Kadai ninru

*kadai ninṟu amarar kaḻal toḻudu nāḷum idai ninṟa inbattarāvar pudai ninṟa nīrōda mēni nedumālē nin adiyai yārōda vallār avar*

**Пословный перевод:**

amarar kadai ninṟu – standing at the doorstep of other deities; nāḷum – at all times; kaḻal toḻudu – falling at (their) feet; idai ninṟa inbattar āvar – they will realise the pleasures lying in-between (such as heaven etc); pudai ninṟa nīr ōdam mēni nedumālē – Oh the supreme being who is having the form of ocean which is surrounding this world!; nin adiyai – your divine feet; ōda vallār avar yār – is there anyone who can think of? (there is no one)

**Перевод:**

**kadai ninṟu – standing at the door step of various deities at the places of their dwelling. kadai  – door step.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “you are saying ‘protect me’ and putting all the responsibility on my head. ṣouldn’t there be some benefit or the other with you also?” to which āḻvār responds saying “one can attain other deities and ask for very low benefits only such as svargam (heaven); is it possible for one to know about you, who are absolutely unquantifiable, and attain you?  Who can know about your divine feet and attain you, who can remove the sorrows of having attained other deities? Who can attain you, after knowing your simplicity?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**amarar** – these deities are called as *amarar* (immortals) as they live for a few years longer \[than we\]. vēdam says “*na brahmā nēśāna:…*” (not brahmā, not īśan \[the deity for creation and destruction as ordained by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] have seen śrīvaikuṇṭam). Not realising that these deities also get destroyed one day, the samsāri \[dweller of materialistic realm\] thinks that they are immortals.

**amarar kaḻal toḻudu** – the samsāri keeps falling at the feet of such deities, one after another. These deities, unlike emperumān, are not capable of granting all wishes of a samsāri. Hence the samsāri keeps falling at the feet of each of these deities to get each wish granted.

**nāḷum toḻudu** – these deities keep asking for various things to satisfy themselves such as infant or goat or rooster etc and are difficult to worship. Thus, until they grant him his wish, the samsāri has to keep holding on to their feet, for a long time. These deities are unlike [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who had mercifully said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-26](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-26/) “*patram puśpam palam tōyam*…” (worship me with a leaf, or a flower or a fruit or water). Even after vorśipping them in such a hard way, what can these deities grant?

**idai ninṟa inbattar āvar** – they \[those who worship these deities\] will get to experience comfort in svargam (heaven) which is neither here nor there, and is more of a hurdle. They do not enjoy you as in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) like muktars and nityars. They do not stay here (on earth) like us, vorśipping you and expecting to reach śrīvaikuṇṭam soon. Since it \[heaven\] is a place full of egotists and they stay for a long time, as said in [mudal tiruvandādi 56](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-56/) “*kadikkamalattil uḷḷirundum kāṇgilān kaṇṇan adikkamalam tannai ayan*” (even though brahmā is residing in the fragrant lotus flower which grows out of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s navel, he does not get to worship the divine feet of emperumān) they do not worship you to get uplifted and merely enjoy the mediocre comforts such as svargam etc. How ignorant is this!

But why do they carry out such a foolish act? It is because they do not know your greatness, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/)…

**pudai ninṟa nīrōda mēni** – āḻvār mercifully describes about the divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which removes the ennui of those who have darśan (audience) of his divine form. His divine form has the complexion of the ocean which surrounds the earth. Is it only the divine form?

**nedumālē** – Oh one who has immeasurable greatness of *ātmasvarūpam* (nature of emperumān’s soul)!

**nin adiyai ōdavallār avar yār** – Is there anyone here who, knowing that you are greater than everyone else, attains your divine feet as the ultimate benefit through worship etc? There are many in this world who attain other deities for lowly benefits. The opinion here is that even among those who worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), there are very few who consider him as both the means and the benefit. Hasn’t emperumān himself said in bhagavat gītā 7-19 “*vāsudēvas sarvam iti sa mahātmā sudurlabha:*” (those great souls who consider emperumān as everything are very rare)!

There is another interpretation to this pāsuram.

**amarar** – other deities such as brahmā, rudra et al

**kadai ninṟu** – holding on to the door of your temple

**kaḻal toḻudu** – vorśipping your divine feet

**nāḷum idai ninṟa inbattar āvar** – instead of enjoying you as the ultimate goal, they will seek positions such as brahmā, rudra, indra etc, similar to asking for loin cloth from a wish fulfilling tree such as *kaṟpakam* (a celestial tree). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) feels sad about the ignorance of such people. Hasn’t nammāḻvār too divinely mentioned in his tiruviruttam 92 “*ninnai viṇṇōr tāḷ nilam tōyndu toḻuvar nin mūrtti pal kūṟṟil onṟum kāṇalum aṇgol enṟē vaigal mālaiyum kālaiyumē*” (the celestial entities will worship you everyday both in the morning and in the evening, with their feet firmly rooted to the ground \[so that you would get rid of their enemies and\] not because they wish to see one of your varied forms)! [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) conveyed the same meaning in [bhūdattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/)’s verse “*nin adiyai yār ōda vallār*” in his verse “*nin mūrtti pal kūṟṟil onṟum kāṇalum āngol enṟē*”

We shall take up the 12<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 12. Avar ivar enrillai

*avar ivar enṟillai aravaṇaiyāṇ pādam evar vaṇangi ēttādār eṇṇil pavarum seḻum kadirōn oṇmalarōn kaṇṇudalōn enṟē toḻum tagaiyār nāḷum todarndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

eṇṇil – if we analyse (who all get their sustenance from emperumān); avar ivar enṟu illai – there is no distinction between one person and the other when it comes to attaining him \[for getting favours\]; aravu aṇaiyāṇ pādam – the divine feet of emperumān who has ādiśēśan as his mattress; vaṇangi – worship; ēttādār – those who do not praise; evar – who? (there is none); pavarum seḻum kadirōn – sun who has well spread out rays in thousands; oṇmalarōn – brahmā who has the beautiful lotus (growing out of emperumān’s navel) as his seat; kaṇ nudalōn – rudra who has an eye in his forehead; nāḷum – every day; todarndu toḻum tagaiyār anṟē – aren’t they having the quality of seeking out the place where emperumān is dwelling and vorśipping him?

**Перевод:**

**avar ivar enṟillai – There is no difference between lowly people or great people. (emperumān is attained, without any distinction, by all entities, including the dēvars such as brahmā, rudra et al who are famous for being attained by others, and the people who attain such dēvas . Is there any child who is not liked by its mother? (thus all the entities attain emperumān).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Can’t we hold on to other deities? Why talk ill of them? āḻvār says that these other deities approach emperumān and obtain the authority that they need and the \[limited\] ability to fulfil their followers’ wishes. Hence, it is appropriate for us for the remainder of our lives to hold on to emperumān’s divine feet only (in the second explanation for the earlier pāsuram it was said that the other deities worship emperumān. In the same way, they get their authority for administrative purposes). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Who do they attain?

**aravaṇaiyān pādam** – the divine feet of emperumān who is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan in *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean). īsn’t reclining on ādiśēśan a unique identification for emperumān! ādiśēśan is there with him in order to speak on our behalf and carry out *puruśakāram* (recommend us to emperumān). Hasn’t it been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-1 “*yatra rāmassa lakśmaṇa:*” (where rāma is together with lakśmaṇa) – vibhīśaṇa came to that place where the two were together (that vibhīśaṇa came to surrender to śrī rāma who was with lakśmaṇa (an incarnation of ādiśēśan) to play the role of recommending, is mentioned here). When [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) surrendered to emperumān he did so in the presence of ādiśēśan as in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-10-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/thiruvaimozhi-5-10-11-naganai-misai/) “*nāgaṇai misai nambirān caraṇē śaraṇ*”. [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) too said the same in her nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-9 “*nāgaṇaiikē senṟu*” (going to the mattress of ādiśēśan)

**evar vaṇangi ēttādār** – who does not hold on to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? Everyone attains him. This is because one has to attain the divine feet of one who does not have to hold on to someone else (that particular quality is present only with emperumān)

**eṇṇil** – if one were to analyse; or, if one were to think.

**pavarum seḻum kadirōn oṇ malarōn kan nudalōn anṟē** – these people are famous for being attained by others. But even these people attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**pavarum seḻum kadirōn** – sun with spread out, infinite number of rays.

**oṇ malarōn** – brahmā who is in the lotus shooting out of emperumān’s navel.

**kaṇ nudalōn** – rudra who has an eye in his forehead.

Don’t these people…

**toḻum tagaiyār nāḷum todarndu** – have the quality of following [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) wherever he goes, compel him and attain him.

**nāḷum toḻum tagaiyār** – every day they are vorśipping emperumān. Just as a rope will lose its strength if it is untwisted, these people also think that they will lose all their power if they do not worship emperumān and hence keep following him. Instead of vorśipping him because this is what has been ordained, they worship him in order to get some benefits \[and lose the more important benefit of enjoying him\]. How unjustified is this that they are treating their vorśipping of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as a wage for getting some benefits instead of treating the act of vorśipping itself as the benefit!

We shall move on to the 13<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 13. Todar edutta

*todar edutta māl yānai sūḻkayam pukku anji padar edutta paim kamalam koṇḍu anṟu idaradukka āḻiyān pādam paṇindanrē vānavar kōn pāḻidān eydiṟṟu paṇḍu*

**Пословный перевод:**

todar edutta māl yānai – an elephant in exultation, breaking free from its chains and running with the chain in its trunk; sūḻkayam pukku – entered an expansive pond; padar edutta paim kamalam koṇḍu – taking lotus flowers which have blossomed well (in order to lay them at the divine feet of emperumān); anṟu idar adukka – after getting into a deep hurdle (getting caught by a crocodile and unable to free itself from the crocodile); anji – trembling with fear in its mind (whether it would be able to offer the flowers to emperumān before they wither away); āḻiyān pādam – at the divine feet of emperumān who is holding in his hand the divine disc (the implement for killing the crocodile); paṇindanṟē – because of vorśipping (after surrendering); tān – that gajēndra āḻvān \[in our sampradāyam certain people (and other entities) are referred to as āḻvān to indicate their exalted status\]; paṇḍu – in ancient time; vānavar kōn pāḻi – the dwelling place of the Lord of nityasūris (permanent dweller of śrivaikuṇṭam), paramapadam; eydiṟṟu – attained

**Перевод:**

**todar edutta māl yānai – an elephant which, in exultation, pulling aside the creepers bearing flowers, went about as it pleased. It could also be construed as a huge elephant which removed its chain (used for tying it) and went about as it pleased.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Could only those with authority attain emperumān? Could only dēvas, with limitations, attain him and should others lose out being unable to attain him? āḻvār says that there is no such compulsion. Even an animal, with a lowly birth and lowly activities, such as gajēndran \[an elephant\] was able to attain his divine feet and also had his enemy removed by emperumān, and ultimately reached paramapadam. Wasn’t he \[emperumān\] such a simple entity even for an elephant! Thus, anyone can attain him, says āḻvār. In the earlier pāsuram, it was mentioned that emperumān gives what had been desired by those who attain him for other benefits. In this pāsuram, it says that emperumān gives himself to those who worship him and desire to see him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māl yānai** – elephant in exultation. Alternative meaning: one which cannot be controlled.

**sūḻ kayam pukku** – entering an expansive pond whose bank could not be seen with one’s eyes \[so huge\]

**sūḻ kayam** – a pond which could drive away those who entered it, with the help of wild creatures such as crocodile etc.

**kayam pukku** – entering an area which is not its \[elephant’s\].

**anji** – fearful of the hurdle caused by crocodile. The elephant would have felt stronger on land than in water. It would have been with huge ego similar to that of entities such as brahmā et al. viṣṇu darmam 69-47 says “*paramāpadamāpanna:*” (got into a huge difficulty). A difficulty which was huger than such an elephant. It was such a difficulty that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had to come hurriedly to overcome it.

**padar edutta paingamalam koṇḍu** – *padar* – being an expansive lotus flower which blossomed. e*dutta* – a lotus which has grown tall. *paingamalam* – a beautiful lotus. Alternatively, a cool lotus. Plucking that lotus with lot of desire.

**anṟu idar adukka** – a difficulty which occurred there due to crocodile. *idar* – it was such a difficulty that there was no remedy for it other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself coming there.

**anji** – fearing whether he \[gajēndra\] would be able to offer that lotus to the divine feet of emperumān before the flower withered.

As a remedy to it…

**āḻiyān pādam paṇindanṟē** – is it not by vorśipping at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is donning the *cakrāyudam* (divine disc as a weapon)!

**āḻiyān** – one who is donning the divine disc. Hasn’t viṣṇu darmam 69-81 said “*graham cakrēṇa mādava:*” (mādavan (emperumān) killed the crocodile with his weapon disc). It is mentioned that emperumān offers himself to those who desire only himself and no other benefit from him.

**paingamalam** – it is conveyed here that gajēndran plucked a very soft lotus in an easy manner.

**āḻiyān** – it is conveyed here that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has weapon in his hand to remove the difficulties of his followers.

**vānavar kōn pāḻidān eydiṟṟup paṇḍu** – once upon a time, the elephant ascended the mattress of emperumān, who is the lord of tireless nityasūris, at his dwelling place of paramapadam (this takes into consideration that the meaning for the word *pāḻi* could be either place or bed). When such is the quality of emperumān, is there any boundary for saying that so and so is dear to emperumān and so and so is not dear to emperumān? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that everyone is qualified to attain him.

We shall move on to the 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 14. Pandip perum padiyai

*paṇḍip perum padiyai ākkip paḻipāvam koṇḍingu vāḻvāraik kūṟādē eṇḍhisaiyum pērtta karam nāngudaiyān pērōdip pēdaigāḷ tīrtta kararāmin tirindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pēdaigāḷ – Oh ignorant people!; paṇḍiyai – stomach; perum padi ākki – filling it up \[stomach\] with everything as if it were a huge town; paḻi pāvam koṇḍu – going on accumulating sins through faulty activities carried out knowingly and unknowingly; ingu – in this world; vāḻvārai – those who dwell in this materialistic realm; kūṟādē – instead of praising them; eṇ disaiyum pērtta karam nāngu udaiyān – trivikrama’s puffed up divine shoulders which blew to smithereens the eight directions (when he measured the worlds); pēr – divine names; ōdi – reciting continuously without expecting any gain in return; tirindu – taking a sojourn all over the world; tīrttakarar āmin – purify all the lands (through your connection)

**Перевод:**

**paṇḍu ip perum padiyai ākki – nurturing this old materialistic realm. Alternatively, paṇḍi also means stomach. Hence, nurturing the stomach to make it like a huge town. Earlier one person (nanjīyar?) mercifully said that “this body is a mobile celestial vehicle” (since emperumān has pervaded the physical body, this body becomes a celestial vehicle for emperumān). āḻvār feels sad that this body is being nurtured without knowing emperumān and ātmā (his śarīram) are being destroyed.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that once it is known that emperumān can be attained by everyone, don’t praise lowly persons who nurture only the life in this materialistic realm but praise emperumān and make the entire world a purified one through your sojourns. Once knowing the nature of emperumān, worship at the divine feet of emperumān, recite his names and through your connection, purify the world. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**paḻi pāvam koṇḍu** – Gathering ridicules and sins in order to nurture the body. People of this world, without realising that they are the repository for the ridicules which come without a reason and the sins which come with a reason, will gather the results of those \[activities\] which they do unknowingly and those which they do knowingly. Ridicule is something which is thrust on us by others and sin is the result of lowly acts which we carry out ourselves. Ridicule results  because we are beholden to someone else and sin, because of our own independence and thinking that “I am my Lord”.

**ingu vāḻvāraik kūṟādē** – instead of praising the people of this world who are like this \[those who gather ridicule and sins\]. Unless one thinks that there is something called as soul which is different from the body, he will not know that there is a world \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\] above and that one has to enjoy the happiness there. Since those who live here are people who think that there is nothing other than what is seen in this world, they will think that there is nothing other than living their lives for sometime here. It is better not to praise such people. It would be better not to praise them saying “can anyone ridicule you! can you accumulate any sin!”

**ingu vāḻvārai** – those who think that they are living here, while there is nothing called prosperity. One should not praise someone who thinks that sorrow is pleasure.

**pēdaigāḷ** – Oh ignorant people! Those who think that there is nothing other than what is being seen with eyes. Those ignorant people who think that wealth which is seen by the eyes, is also a benefit!

**eṇḍhisaiyum pērtta karam nāngu udaiyān pēr ōdi** – with the divine hands puffing up and growing in all the eight directions. The hands puffed up such that they wanted to destroy the existing directions and make new, larger directions. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) tells us to recite the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who measured the worlds to protect them.

**tīrttakarar āmin tirindu** – go all over the world to make them auspicious. Has it not been divinely mentioned in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 10-5 “*tiridalāl tavam udaittu ittaraṇidānē*” (this world is fortunate because of those who go all over the place, with their hands folded and head bowed) and in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 2-6 “*tīdil nanneṟi kātti engum tirundu*” (going to all places, manifesting their activities which are free from any faults)! Correct this world which cannot be set right by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself, with his divine names. Just as people of this world spoil the others apart from spoiling themselves, those who recite emperumān’s divine names will have noble activities themselves and will also make others to have noble activities. Has it not been mentioned in manu smiriti 8-92 “*yamō vaivasvatō rājā yastavaiva hruti stita: tēna cēda vivādastē mā gangām mā kurūn gama:*” (there is no need for you to go to gangā or kurukśētram if emperumān, who is the King residing amidst the solar galaxy, controlling everyone, stays in your heart and you do not have any argument with him \[in other words, if you accept him and are well disposed towards him\]).

**tirindu tīrttakarar āmin** – make the world sanctimonious by your sojourns (according to tradition, there is an anecdote mentioned about people sanctifying the world – once dēvappiḷḷai, elder (son?) to [piḷḷān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/pillan/) had to go to a different town. He was very sad about it and told [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) (elder son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/)) “while relatives like all of you are here, only I have to go outside. After reaching there, whose face shall I see when I wake up (meaning that they are all strangers)? bhattar responded by divinely saying “rāmapirān (śrī rāma) gave mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) even to grass. Yet, he left behind his sons lava and kuśa even though he had no difficulty in granting them mōkśam, so that they could protect this world. In the same way, aren’t you going outside only to enable those who live there to get uplifted?”

We shall move on to the 15<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 15. Tirindadu venjamattu

*tirindadu venjamattut tēr kadavi anṟu pirindadu sītaiyai mān pin pōy purindaduvum kaṇ paḷḷi koḷḷa aḻagiyadē nāgattin taṇpaḷḷi koḷvān tanakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

vem samattu – in the deadly (bhārata) war (when he incarnated as kriṣṇa); tēr kadavi – (as pārtasāraty, the charioteer) conducting the chariot, fore and back; tirindadu – wandered; anṟu – when incarnated as śrī rāma; mān pin pōy – went behind the demon mārīcan who came as a deceptive deer; sītaiyai pirindadu – suffered after being separated from pirātti (sītā pirātti, mahālakśmi); kaṇ – on the ground; paḷḷi koḷḷa – to sleep; purindaduvum – desired; nāgattin taṇ paḷḷi koḷvān tanakku – for emperumān who is reclining on the cool mattress of ādiśēśan; aḻagiyadē – are these apt?

**Перевод:**

**tirindadu venjamattut tēr kadavi – \[emperumān during his incarnation as kriṣṇa \] wandered in the cruel mahābhārata war as charioteer, clashing with armies. Even there, was he the chief, getting jobs done through others? He was a mere charioteer for arjuna, crediting arjuna for all the victories and taking the arrows directed at arjuna on himself, by being in the forefront. In battles, usually, the warriors on chariots will first direct their anger on the charioteer \[of enemy\] and incapacitate him so that it would be easier to tackle the enemy on his chariot. kriṣṇa sat as charioteer under the feet of arjuna, without any armour to protect him when the opponents came at him with their weapons.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Even as he thought of emperumān’s vāmana avatāram, āḻvār thinks of how he \[emperumān\] changed his identity to incarnate as śrī rāma and as kriṣṇa, in order to protect such followers, and had to put up with much suffering. He is disgusted that such a gentle and soft entity as emperumān had to suffer so much while people of this world protect only their physical forms and ensure that no harm comes to their forms. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**venjamattut tēr kadavi tirindadu** – \[emperumān\] made the armies clash with each other. Did not the arrows shot by one bhagadattan fall on kriṣṇa! Has it not been said in mahābhāratam drōṇa parvam 29-18 “*urasā prati jagrāha pārtam sancādya mādava:* ” (kaṇṇan hid arjuna and took the arrows on his chest)! It appears that while kaṇṇan thrust his chest to take the arrows which were directed at arjuna, pirātti (sitting on the chest of emperumān) took the arrows on herself. Did not pirātti say in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 27-7 “*agratas tē gamiśyāmi*” (I shall precede you, in protecting our followers)!

**anṟu pirindadu sītaiyai mān pin pōy** – going behind the deceptive deer, did he not get separated from sītai! Hasn’t [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) mercifully mentioned in his periya tirumoḻi 2-5-6 “*kiḷar poṟiya maṟitiriya adanin pinnē padarndānai* ” (he went behind the deceptive deer which had spots all over its body and which was wandering in their presence)! The deceptive deer had spots on its body to attract sītāppirātti. As it was moving about in their presence, sītāppirātti requested śrī rāma to secure it for her. śrī rāma went chasing it. However since it was a cruel deer, it did not yield itself to him and tried to escape from him and he also kept chasing it. This chase went so far that ultimately it led to his separation from pirātti.

**mān pin pōy pirindadu sītaiyai** – were we fortunate to have emperumān and pirātti together when they came to protect us? (Did they not separate from each other to protect dēvas (celestial entities))! They are together, to protect others. They get separated, also to protect others!

**sītaiyai** – śrī rāma had mentioned about sītāppirātti in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāndam 10-21 “*prāṇēbhyō garīyasī*” (you are greater than my life). He had to get separated from such pirātti.

**purindaduvum kaṇ paḷḷi koḷḷa** – what he did or what he desired. *kaṇ paḷḷi koḷḷa* – *kaṇ* refers to place. Here it refers to the hard ground. In his younger days, śrī rāma had slept on grass as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 22-23 “*truṇaśayanē anucitē*” (it was not apt for him to sleep on grass). While he \[daśarata\] wept uncontrollably at this, śrī rāma had to sleep on the hard ground, without even grass. He had to wander in the forests, without even a village in sight. How cruel it was! Did not daśarata cry out, as mentioned in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 9-3 “*kallaṇaimēl kaṇ tuyilak kaṟṟanaiyō kāguttā*” (Oh kāguttā! \[another name for śrī rāma\] did you learn how to sleep on stone-bed? I, who had sent him to the forest, did not train him to sleep on stone-bed too, cries daśarata).

**kaṇ paḷḷi koḷḷa** – while lakśmaṇa spread out a bed of leaves for śrī rāma to sleep on, because of his separation from sītāppirātti, without knowing that, he slept on hard ground.

**aḻagiyadē nāgattin taṇ paḷḷi koḷvān tanakku** – for one who, when he sleeps on the mattress of ādiśēśan, his followers will fear as to what will happen to his soft divine form, is sleeping like this \[on hard ground\] apt? Hasn’t [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) mercifully said in [tiruppallāṇḍu 9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-9-uduththuk-kalaintha/) “*padutta painnāgaṇaip paḷḷi koṇḍānukku*” (to emperumān who sleeps on the mattress of ādiśēśan who has well spread hood)! [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) too has mercifully said in nānmugan tiruvandādi 35 “*tāḷāl ulagam aḷanda asaivē kol vāḷā kidandu aruḷum vāy tiṟavān nīḷōdam vandalaikkum māmayilai māvallikkēṇiyān aindalai vāy nāgattaṇai*” (Is it that emperumān is tired of measuring all the worlds with his divine feet that he is silently lying on the bed of ādiśēśan in [mayilaittiruvallikkēṇi](http://thiruvallikkeni.koyil.org/) which is lapped by the waves from ocean!). He has thought that the reason for lying in such a cool place is because he is probably tired.

**aḻagiyadē** – does it not appear that protecting the world like this (wandering in the forest in search of sītāppirātti and driving the chariot for arjuna, taking all the arrows aimed at arjuna on himself\] is not apt for his divine form!

**aḻagiyadē nāgattin taṇ paḷḷi koḷvān tanakku** – āḻvār feels very sad that he had to lie down on hard ground, when he has such a soft divine form with which he normally reclines on ādiśēśan; that he had to wander around in search of sītāppirātti; that he had to wander in the battle field being ridiculed by the others. He asks whether it is apt for him to protect the world after doing all these? Does he have to protect his followers only by doing all these? āḻvār mercifully asks with sadness whether he, the primary entity, has to destroy himself like this and protect his followers.

We shall take up the 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
