## 16. Tanakku adimaippattadu

*tanakkadimai pattadu tānaṟiyānēlum manattadaiya vaippadām mālai vanattidarai ēriyām vaṇṇam iyaṟṟum iduvallāl māri yār peygiṟpār maṟṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tanakku – for himself (who is a cētana, sentient entity); adimai – being a servitor; pattadu – that it is being apt; tān aṟiyān ēlum – even if he is not able to know; manattu – in the mind; adaiya – as soon as he reaches; mālai – that emperumān; vaippadu – to keep him, by providing place; Am – is apt; vanam tidarai – the high ground on which the forest has sprung up; ēri Am vaṇṇam iyaṟṟum idu allāl – other than the activity of digging a lake in that area so that rain water will not flow elsewhere but into that lake; maṟṟu – other than; māri – rain; peygiṟpār – to make it fall; yār – who is there? (isn’t rainfall due to emperumān’s sankalpam (vow))

**Перевод:**

**tanakkadimai pattadu than aṟiyānēlum – servitorśip is always ordained for jīvātmā (soul). This has been brought out by a few illustrations such as in (1) śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāndam 15-7 “paravānasmi” (I am fully dependent \[on emperumān\]), (2) stōtra ratnam-60 “tavāsmi” (I belong to you), (3) viṣṇutatvam “svatvam ātmani sanjātam svāmitvam brahmaṇi stitam” (it is ātmā’s characteristic to be a possession and brahmam (emperumān)’s characteristic to be the possessor). Even if this is not known. Hasn’t it been said in viṣṇutatvam “ātmadāsyam harē: svāmyam” (ātmā’s characteristic is to be servitor and hari’s is to be the Lord).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks whether we have to take efforts to attain him instead of attaining him through his efforts, when his actions are like these, toiling hard to protect his followers, as seen in the previous pāsuram. When he is like this for his followers, should it not be that the reason for our having knowledge is to make us attain him? When he is trying to attain us, should we try to attain him? āḻvār asks whether there is any shortcoming for us when emperumān, who has no results for his deeds (in other words no pāpa or puṇya), undergoes hardships which even we, who suffer from pāpa and puṇya, do not undergo, for protecting us. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tān aṟiyānēlum** – even if he ([cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) does not know emperumān’s and his qualities. Even if he does not know that his quality is being a servitor. Even if he is without good knowledge.

**tān aṟiyānēlum** – despite pramāṇams (authentic texts) saying the above mentioned;  not carrying out service to his ācāryan and learning from him about these.

**manattadaiya vaippadām** – even unknown to the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) enters his heart  in order to claim ownership of his possession, what is required is not to shun him at that time. Is emperumān not referred to as *māl*? *māl* means having affection. All that the cētana needs to do is to provide emperumān space when he comes head over heels to the cētana. It is emperumān’s responsibility to do whatever else needs to be done after that. [vaduga nambi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vaduga-nambi/) (a disciple of [bhagavat śrī rāmānujar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) once said “we need not carry out any means to attain emperumān.  When the neighbour dons tirumaṇ kāppu on his forehead, it is enough to tolerate that instead of making fun of him”. Hasn’t emperumān said in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-67 “*na ca mām abyasūyati*” (do not share this secret with one who is jealous of me)! [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) (ācāryan of the vyākyātā of this prabandam śrī nampiḷḷai) once told tiruvarangapperumāḷ dāsar “when someone says ‘emeprumān’ be happy that he has given us the quality of not being jealous towards him”. It is opined here that we can only develop a desire towards emperumān and cannot create the benefit of attaining him. An example is illustrated for this, now.

**vanattidarai ēriyām vaṇṇam iyaṟṟum idu allāl** – one can only dig a high ground in the forest and convert it into a lake so that (when rain falls) water can be prevented from going out and can be contained within the lake.

Apart from this…

**māri yār peygiṟpār maṟṟu** – is making rain to fall in a specific place, within the control of [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)?  īsn’t it varuṇa dēva  who is responsible for this! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that when [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/),  appropriate to his being with knowledge, does not show hatred, vouldn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) offer himself to such jīvātmā as a benefit!

Instead of this, some persons will offer an alternative interpretation for this pāsuram.

**\[tanakkadimai pattadu than aṟiyānēlum\]** As mentioned in muṇḍaka upaniśat 1-1-10 “*ya: sarvajña: sarvavit*” (whoever knows the natural character and natural disposition of all entities) and in śvētaśvatara upaniśat 6-8 “*svābhāvikī jñānabalakriyā ca* ” (it is mentioned that the paramātmā has natural knowledge, power, deeds etc), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) knows everything as his nature. It used to be said derisively that the meaning for this term is: since the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) does not know that he is a servitor to such emperumān and that emperumān controls everyone, even if he does not have such knowledge, is it possible for such a jīvātmā to keep emperumān in his heart? While emperumān has affection towards his followers, can the jīvātmā keep him in his heart? Is there anything known that the jīvātmā can, with his own efforts, know about emperumān and attain him? While emperumān himself will create the desire in the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and offer himself, is there any meaning in the jīvātmā trying anything on his own?

**vanattidarai ēriyām vaṇṇam iyaṟṟum idu** – it is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who ordains the jīvātmā to dig the lake.

**allāl māri yār peygiṟpār maṟṟu** – isn’t it he who also makes indra (lord of celestial entities) to create downpour!

**vanattidarai ēriyām vaṇṇam iyaṟṟum idu** – emperumān makes us avoid avidyā (ignorance), karmam (deeds resulting from puṇyam and pāpam), vāsanā (impressions from innumerable births) and ruci (taste for repeating a particular deed) in our hearts.

That too ….

**māri yār peygiṟpār maṟṟu** – isn’t it again he who makes his qualities to strike in the heart of the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)! If the words are slightly rearranged to read as *vanattidarai ēriyām vaṇṇam iyaṟṟum iduvallāl peygiṟpār maṟṟu yār* this will not appear to be an illustration. Instead of this, if it is taken to refer to those who have deep involvement with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), we should consider that emperumān is only to be attained and is not an object to be spoken of in any way that one thinks of in his heart. ōr, even if the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) does not know that he is a servitor to emperumān, he should keep emperumān in his heart. In other words, carrying out this as one’s appropriate deed. It can also be taken to mean that he would know that cētana and [acētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) form the body of emperumān since he knows the meanings of vēdāntams (the end portions of vēdams, dealing with emperumān). For the term *manattadaiya vaippadām*, there are three interpretations: attaining emperumān after seeing how others attain him; having only the desire to attain him; and, emperumān only will create that desire too. Of these, the first interpretation is credited to [\[parāśara\] bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) \[son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/)\], the second to [tirumalai nambi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periya-thirumalai-nambi/) \[one of the ācāryas of [śrī rāmānujar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) and the third to [āḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) \[kūrattāḻvān, one of the most important disciples of śrī rāmānujar\].

We shall consider the 17<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 17. Marrar iyalavar

*maṟṟār iyalāvār vānavarkōn māmalarōn suṟṟum vaṇangum toḻilānai oṟṟaip piṟaiyirunda senjadaiyān pin senṟu mālaik kuṟai irundu tān mudittān koṇḍu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vānavar kōn – indra, the head of all celestial entities; mā malarōn – brahmā, who dwells on the lotus flower, shooting out of emperumān’s navel; suṟṟum vaṇangum toḻilānai – one who has activities such that he is surrounded on the sides and vorśipped \[by the above-mentioned entities\]; mālai – emperumān; koṇḍu – keeping (in the heart); oṟṟai piṟai irunda senjadaiyān – rudra who is having reddish matted hair and who dons moon with single kalā (phase of the moon); pin senṟu – following \[emperumān\]; irandu – begging him; kuṟai – his shortcoming (of brahmahatti dōśam, the fault of removing one of brahmā’s heads); tān mudittān – he completed his task; iyal āvār – apt to be attained; maṟṟu Ar – who else is there, apart from sarvēśvaran (supreme being)?

**Перевод:**

**maṟṟār iyalāvār – is there anyone other than sarvēśvaran (supreme being) emperumān who is known? There is no one other than emperumān and only he is apt to be attained.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that apart from emperumān who is like this (as mentioned in the previous pāsurams) there is no one who is apt to be attained. Those deities who are well known in samsāram (materialistic realm) as being apt for being attained such as brahmā et al, themselves attain emperumān to fulfil their desires. This being the case, the only entity for us to attain is emperumān says the āḻvār. As mentioned in mahā nārāyaṇa upaniśat 11-13 “sa brahmā sa Siva: sēndra:” (he himself is brahmā, he himself is Siva and he himself is indra), deities such as brahmā, Siva and indra are part of his physical form, attain him and realise their svarūpam (basic nature). In the 12th pāsuram, āḻvār had mentioned “pavarum seḻum kadirōn oṇ malarōn kaṇ nudalōn anṟē” (sun, brahmā and Siva follow emperumān and keep vorśipping him everyday). In that pāsuram it was mentioned that apart from emperumān there is no other śēśi (possessor).  In this pāsuram, it is mentioned that apart from emperumān there is none apt to be attained. In that pāsuram, mention was made of deities in general such as sun. Here, mention is made of deities in high positions such as brahmā. Even those great entities who are occupying great positions, show their emptiness and obtain what they desire from emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vānavarkōn māmalarōn** – indra, the lord of dēvas (celestial entities) and brahmā, who has as his dwelling place the lotus flower growing out of emperumān’s navel

**suṟṟum vaṇangum toḻilānai** – indra and brahmā instead of going directly to emperumān, will go a little bit away on the side and attain him. emperumān, who has such a quality (of being vorśipped). ōr, one who carries out favourable activities. ōr, we can interpret it as one whose activities are vorśipped by entities such as indra, brahmā et al. Don’t even such deities attain emperumān and fulfil their needs!

**toḻilānai** – one who is attained in this way.

While brahmā, indra et al strive hard to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)

**maṟṟar iyalāvār** – who are the others striving? [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-8 conveys the same meaning “*nōlādāṟṟēn una pādam kāṇa ennum nuṇṇuṇarvin nīlār kaṇḍattu ammānum niṟai nānmuganum indiranum* ” (one with fine emotions such as Siva, one with supreme knowledge such as brahmā and indra – these entities come and worship emperumān saying that they will not adopt any means to attain his divine feet).

**oṟṟaippiṟai irunda sem sadaiyān** – *oṟṟaippiṟai* here refers to single phase of the moon. In other words, it refers to a unique phase. Siva, who has reddish matted hair, who dons moon with such a unique phase, and is comfortable.

**pin senṟu mālaik koṇḍu** – having affection (*māl*) in his heart; having [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (*māl*) in his heart.

**pin senṟu** – following emperumān

**kuṟai irandu** – and then humbly appealing about his fault

**tān mudittān** – completed that task and fulfilled his desires. Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that even entities in high positions such as brahmā, Siva and indra attain emperumān and have their desires fulfilled. This being the case, āḻvār asks whether there is anyone other than emperumān who is fit to be attained.

We shall take up the 18<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 18. Kondadu ulagam

*koṇḍadu ulagam kuṟaḷ uruvāyk kōḷ ariyāy oṇḍiṟalōn mārvattu ugir vaittadu uṇḍaduvum tān kadanda ēḻulagē tāmaraikkaṇ māl orunāḷ vān kadandān seyda vaḻakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

kuṟaḷ uruvāy – in the form of vāmana (dwarf); koṇḍadu – seized; kōḷ ariyāy – became narasimha with strength; oṇ tiṟalōn mārvattu – on the chest of the might iraṇiyan (hiraṇyakaśyap); ugir vaittadu – pressed his fingernails and tore him, killing him; oru nāḷ – at one point of time; tān kadanda – that which he measured; ēḻ ulagē – all the worlds; uṇḍaduvum – keeping in his divine stomach (so that deluge will not swallow it); vān kadandān – having the greatness that even if one were to measure the sky, he cannot be measured; tāmaraikkaṇ – having eyes like reddish lotus; māl – emperumān; seyda – mercifully carried out; vaḻakku – honest activities

**Перевод:**

**koṇḍadu – this mentions about emperumān protecting the world. Even if he carries out very difficult tasks, he assumes that “I did what I am supposed to do”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that though emperumān is īśvaran (controller) for all and is attained by all, when it comes to protecting his followers, he will even transform his identity to protect them. When others protect, they will do so without any harm to their identity.  But emperumān will be even willing to change his identity if he has to protect them. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**koṇḍadu ulagam kuṟaḷ uruvāy** – for the sake of his followers, he assumed the form of vāmana (a dwarf) to obtain the worlds. For getting back his possession, was it required to go begging? If he had to ask for alms, could he not have gone in his natural form? Did he have to take the form of a dwarf?

**kōḷ ariyāy oṇḍiṟalōn mārvattu ugir vaittadu** – In order to keep his finger nails on the chest of the mighty iraṇiyan (hiraṇyakaśyap) did he not assume the form of narasinga (lion face and human torso) which no one else in the world assumed! *kōḷ ari* – very powerful narasimha. Could he not have destroyed the enemy iraṇiyan with one form? Did he have to combine lion and man?

**oṇ tiṟalōn** – very powerful iraṇiyan.

**mārvattu ugi vaittadu** – it is implied here that he, with his own hands, touched and destroyed the enemy of his followers.

**ugir vaittadu** – Could he not have killed him with his divine disc? If he had to do that \[kill iraṇiyan with his nails\] since iraṇiyan had obtained the boon that he should not die of any weapon, could he not have killed him with his *sankalpam* (mere thought), if he had so much of ingenuity!

**uṇḍaduvum tān kadanda ēḻulagē** – Was it not enough that he had to assist one follower, prahlāda? Did he have to protect the entire world?

**uṇḍaduvum tān oru nāḷ kadanda ēḻulagē** – did he not protect the world which he had one day (during his incarnation as trivikrama) kept under his divine feet! Alternatively, we have to construe the meaning for *oru nāḷ* as once upon a time when he ate up the world.

**tāmaraikkaṇ māḷ** – sarvēśvaran (supreme being) who is lotus-eyed (it is implied that only the one with lotus eyes can be the īśvaran (controller) for all the worlds). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has divinely mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-5-10 “*anaittulagum udaiya aravinda lōcananai*” (only the one with lotus eyes is the possessor of all the worlds).

Who begged as śrī vāmana for the earth, who tore the chest of iraṇiyan as śrī narasimha, who ate up the world that he had measured one day?

**vān kadandān** – one who cannot be estimated (measured). As said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-10-thuyakkaru-mathiyil/) “*vānilum periyana vallan*”, one can measure even the sky but not emperumān. Alternatively, the word *vān* can be taken to imply those in the sky, and the meaning will change to: he is greater than nityasūris in paramapadam. ōr it can mean *vānai aḷandavan* – one who measured heaven.

**seyda vaḻakku** – he is the protector of the world. Hence in order to protect the worlds which are his possession, he carried out certain tasks and was contented that “I did what I was supposed to do”. He thought that he had done all these for himself and not for others. Since he is the Lord, he is responsible for protecting his possession and hence did those. īsn’t the protector protecting his possession, an apt act!

We shall take up the 19<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 19. Valakkanru kanday

*(īnstead of stopping vit tis daring act) kuḻa kanṟu – a young calf (vho is actually a demon) tī viḷavin kāykku eṟinda – \[vhic you\] trev to destroy anoter demon vho had taken te form of a vood apple tīmai – tis miscievous act pār viḷanga – to śine brightly on tis eart paḻi seydāy – you acted vrongly*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vaḻakku anṟu kaṇḍāy vali sagadam seṟṟāy – Even though protection is an appropriate act, taking the form of a dwarf \[vāmanāvatāram\], breaking and destroying a very powerful wheel even though you are gentle, are activities which you should not have indulged in. As an infant, your destroying a wheel is not appropriate for your age,**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān thinks that these activities \[mentioned in the previous pāsurams\] are apt to be carried out. But āḻvār, thinking of his gentle nature, says that he should not have carried out these. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: vaḻakkanṟu kaṇḍāy vali sagadam seṟṟāy vaḻakkenṟu nī madikka vēṇḍā kuḻakkanṟu tīviḷavin kāykkeṟinda tīmai tirumālē pār viḷangac ceydāy paḻi tirumālē – you (when you incarnated as kaṇṇan, \[kriṣṇa\]) vali sagadam – the powerful wheel seṟṟāy – kicked it aside and broke it vaḻakkanṟu kaṇḍāy – this is not appropriate for you, please see vaḻakku enṟu nī madikka vēṇḍā – you should not think that this is appropriate for you*

**vali sagadam seṟṟāy** – it could also be construed that you destroyed the wheel with your strength. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is wondering whether it was appropriate for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to act that way with his strength.

**seṟṟāy** – It is our fortune that the wheel was destroyed and you were victorious (if something had happened to you, what would we have done!)

**vaḻakku enṟu nī madika vēṇḍā** – accepted that you had destroyed the wheel. If you say that it was to be carried out, I say that this should not have been done at all. Though the wheel had been destroyed, you should not think that this activity was correct.

**nī madikka vēṇḍā** – you did not have a feeling of remorse that you have carried out an activity which was not appropriate for your age and your gentleness! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is saying so because if he had also accepted what [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did, he would have carried out similar mischievous acts. When a worm crawls in sand, it would appear that it had left behind some writing \[patterns in sand\]. In a similar way, when you stretched your leg, the wheel, per chance, broke. Since this incident happened once, should you keep doing the same type of activity repeatedly?

**kuḻakkanṟu tīviḷavin kāykku eṟinda tīmai** – More than the activity carried out earlier, was it appropriate for you to throw  a calf on wood apple? Both of them \[the demons inside the calf and the fruit\] had the same intention of destroying you! Using a demon in the form of a young calf to kill another demon in the form of wood apple was an inappropriate act. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is worried \[much after the event\] as to what would have happened if both the demons had fallen together on kaṇṇan.

**tirumālē** – if something had happened to you, the world would have been without a Lord. As said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 16-21 “*mangaḷānyabhidadyuśī*” (she carried benediction on śrī rāma), didn’t sītāppirātti , with affection, carry out benediction on you! Could such a person carry out these activities?

**pār viḷangacceydāy paḻi** – you carried out a blame that will last forever on earth.

**paḻi** – thinking that you are going to protect something on earth, you carried out an activity which would have made both the vibhūtis (his properties, material realm and spiritual realm) lose you. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/), out of his affection for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is terming this as blame. But emperumān thinks that it is fame for him. This is *anyatājñānam* (mistaking one for another; i.e. considering blame as fame), feels āḻvār.

We shall take up the 20<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 20. 20 -Pali pavam kaiyagarri

*(tus te tvo types of people) vāḻavarām – tey vill live vit happiness mādō – is tis any surprise!*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**paḻi pāvam kaiyagaṟṟi – avoiding the blame which the people of the world heap on them and the sins which result out of that**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that without any distinction between those who have already praised emperumān and those who have just begun to praise him, they have all carried out what they were expected to do. As mentioned in the earlier pāsuram, vouldn’t only those who meditate on the activities that he carried out to protect the world and attain him, flourish! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: paḻi pāvam kaiyagaṟṟi pal kālum ninnai vaḻi vāḻvār vāḻvarām mādo – vaḻuvinṟi nāraṇan than nāmāngaḷ nanguṇarndu nangēttum kāraṇangam tām udaiyar tām paḻi – blame; disrepute pāvam – bad deeds carried out knowingly kaiyagaṟṟi – removing palgālum – always ninnai – you vaḻi  vāḻvār – those who attain you through the ways ordained in śāstram (sacred text) nāraṇan than – nārāyaṇa, your nāmangaḷ – divine names nangu uṇarndu – knowing well nangu ēttum kāraṇangaḷ tām udaiyār tām – those who have the means to worship you well*

**paḻi pāvam** – *paḻi* is that which comes because of others heaping blame when one does not do anything on his own. *pāvam* results when one knowingly causes harm to others. Removing these two. In other words, removing the two inimical forces: blame which comes without a cause and sin which comes with a cause. Should one suffer the result of an action which he has not carried out at all \[blame\]?  This is also due the result of a sin \[committed earlier\]. Does he have to spoil the apt position for himself due to these?

**palgālum ninnai vaḻi vāḻvār vāḻvarām** – Those who worship you as ordained in śāstrams  (sacred texts) and attain you, will live without any trouble. The means adopted to attain him and the benefit of attaining him would be cause for happiness. Even though it is said that “*na sukhāt sukham labhyatē*” (one cannot attain happiness through happiness), this does not apply to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) related matter. Here, both the means (the effort that one takes) and the benefit that one attains will both be happy, without any difference.

**mādō** – this word is like a conjunction. “Is it a surprise?” asks [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/).

**vaḻuvinṟi nāraṇan than nāmangaḷ nangu ēttum** – meditating well upon the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) without any break. Meditating upon the divine names of emperumān which are faultless and hence is the reason for meditating.

**nāraṇan than nāmangaḷ** – it is like speaking about one’s mother.

**nangu uṇarndu** – knowing through devotion

**nangu ēttum** – vorśipping such that one cannot sustain otherwise

**kāraṇangaḷ** – the reason for reciting \[his divine names\]. That is, devotion; singing about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which is the cause for devotion; the desire which is the cause for singing; the puṇya (virtue) which is the cause for desire; emperumān’s mercy which is the cause for puṇya – those who have these will live happily (those who attain puṇya due to emperumān’s mercy, due to which they have desire towards emperumān, due to which they sing about him, due to which they develop devotion towards emperumān, will prosper). Thus, those who worship you after removing their blame and sins and worship you for a long time and those who recite your divine names and worship you without any break, will both prosper. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that only those who receive his mercy will have affection towards him. If the pātam (text) is **tāraṇangaḷ tāmudaiyār** \[instead of **kāraṇangaḷ tammudaiyār**\],  *tāraṇam* will refer to sustenance. In other words it refers to having sustenance through engaging with emperumān, not being swayed by other \[worldly\] pursuits.

Let us take up the 21<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 21. Tam ulare

*(tus, te cētanas (sentient beings) are tere in tis vorld vitout any śortfall) sevvē – directly arunaragam – te cruel hell sērvadu – to reac aridu – very difficult task to carry out*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tām uḷarē… – emperumān has already created cētanas, giving them physical forms and sensory perceptions. viṣṇu tatvam says “vicitrā dēha sampatti: īśvarāya nevēditum, pūrvamēva krutā brahman hastapādādi samyutā” (Oh brāhmaṇa! The physical body, with hands and legs, has been created in the beginning to be offered to emperumān). Thus, emperumān has already given the physical form to cētanas. They do not have to create themselves.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Looking at their fullness \[cētanas\], there is no way that they would reach hell. āḻvār wonders as to why they seek exception and fall into hell. In the earlier pāsuram he advised that we should worship emperumān well. For that to happen, we should be there \[alive\] in the first instance. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: tāmuḷarē tam uḷḷam uḷḷuḷadē tāmaraiyin pūvuḷadē ēttum poḻuduṇḍē vāman tirumaruvu tāḷ maruvu senniyarē sevvē arunaragam sērvadaridu tām uḷarē – cētanas are already there (in order to worship emperumān with their physical bodies and senses) tam uḷḷam uḷ uḷadē – (to think about emperumān) their minds are within themselves (without the need for having to search outside) tāmariyin pū uḷadē – lotus flower (in order to offer to the divine feet of emperumān) is available in plenty (in all water bodies) ēttum poḻudu uṇḍē – the time required to praise emperumān is available in abundance vāman – sarvēśvaran (supreme being) vāmana mūrtti (who kept his divine feet on the heads of all) tirumaruvu tāḷ – beautiful divine feet maruvu – apt to be bowed to, in obeisance senniyarē – people with heads are available*

**tam uḷḷam uḷ uḷadē** – once their existence has been granted, without the need for searching out for a heart, he has given that also, and has made it, his. Heart is not something that is outside of oneself. In order to hold on to the readily accessible, righteous [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the readily available heart is sufficient.

Despite these, is it difficult to worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)?

**tāmariyin pū uḷadē** – The lotus flower is available! Even if he does not want, these have been created already so that he does not have to seek anything outside. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has removed all his short falls, so that the cētana does not have to search out for the flower. The lotus flower is not outside his possessions!

**ēttum poḻudu uṇḍē** – The time required to hold on to emerpumān is also there without any shortfall. Even if one feels lazy to pluck a flower, emperumān has created sufficient time for worship!

**vāman tirumaruvu tāḷ maruvu senniyarē** – \[for the sake of poetic metrics\] vāmanan has been shortened to vāman. īsn’t vāmanan willing to come even as a mendicant in order to accept whatever the cētana gives! He has created the head \[for the cētana\] so that the cētana can bow his head at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The divine feet manifest the opulence of emperumān.

**vāman** – isn’t he one who is elated, keeping his divine feet on everyone’s head and is willing to come even as a mendicant in order to reclaim his possession! All that is required of us is to hold on to him (he doesn’t expect anything else).

**tirumaruvu tāḷ** – As mentioned in [stōtra ratnam 31](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-31/) “*Sanka chakra ratānga kalpaka dvajāravindānkuśa vajra lāncanam*” (divine feet which have as their identity the following: conch, disc, kaṟpaka tree (wish fulfilling tree), lotus flag, goad, vajrāyudam (a weapon like a mace, made of diamond)) his divine feet are such that they manifest clearly that he is the Lord. Hasn‘t [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) too divinely mentioned the same in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-7 “*tiruppolinda sēvadi*” (his divine feet manifest opulence)!

**maruvu senniyarē** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had already created the head which manifests the cētana’s servitorśip! āren’t they (cētanas) those with heads which will accept when emperumān keeps his divine feet on them! “*nān*” (I) denotes egoistic state like that of rāvaṇa. “*aham*” (servitor) denotes the humble state like that of vibhīśaṇa.

When this is the position…

**sevvē arunaragam sērvadu aridu** – to go directly to the cruel samsāram (life in the materialistic realm) is very difficult. When such is the difficulty, how do these people go, searching for samsāram? When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has given so many implements \[physical form, senses, lotus flower, time, head etc\] there is no scope for going to hell (Yet these people waste all these implements and take births in samsāram repeatedly, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says mercifully, with sadness).

**arunaragam** – those who do not have knowledge will consider the dwelling place of yama as hell. Those with knowledge will consider taking birth in this word itself as hell. sītāppirātti says in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 30-18 “*nirayō yastvayā vinā*” (being separated from you is hell). Hasn’t śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 17-63 said “*dēhē cēt prītimān mūda: bavitā narakēpi sa:*” (that ignorant person will have desire for his physical form even in hell)! Instead, to one who does not have it (desire for his body), there is no scope for going to hell.

We shall move on to the 22<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 22. Ariyadu elidagum

*ariyadu eḷidāgum āṟṟalāl māṟṟip peruga muyalvāraip peṟṟāl kariyadōr veṇkōttu māl yānai venṟi mudittanṟē taṇkōttu māmalarāl tāḻndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āṟṟalāl – with his power; māṟṟi – removing the hurdle (of cētana’s efforts); peruga muyalvārai – the supreme entity, emperumān, who is taking all the efforts; peṟṟāl – if we are able to attain \[him\]; ariyadu – mōkśam \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\] which is very difficult to attain; eḷidu āgum – will become easy; veṇ kōdu – having white tusks; māl – huge; kariyadu ōr yānai – black complexioned, unique, gajēndrāḻvān \[name of the elephant\]; venṟi mudittu – victorious (over crocodile) and destroying; taṇ – cool; kōdu – on the bank of the pond; mā – great; malarāl – with lotus flower; tāḻndu anṟē – did it not bow down and carry out service \[to emperumān\]?

**Перевод:**

**ariyadu eḷidāgum – attaining the difficult to attain emperumān and vorśipping him are very difficult. What should be done for that?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*While going to hell is rare, āḻvār mercifully mentioned about the activities carried out by cētanas on their own, in the earlier pāsuram. āḻvār says that even if they do not have any implements \[to attain emperumān\], if they leave their responsibility with emperumān and remain that way, that emperumān will carry out all the tasks will be evident by merely looking at his greatness. Is it easy for samsāris to attain emperumān? āḻvār mentions an easy way. If we are able to attain one who takes care of everything, including our existence, everything will become easy, he says. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āṟṟalāl māṟṟīp peruga muyalvāraip peṟṟāl** – *āṟṟalāl* could mean either with power or with patience. If we consider the first meaning, this would mean: since emperumān has no shortcoming in his power, transferring the responsibility from [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) to himself, and utilising that power for removing the hurdle which stands in the way of the cētana attaining emperumān and offering himself to the cētana. If we attain one who says “why should I expect that this entity (cētana) should do something? Let me take that responsibility on myself and carry out the task for him”, the difficult-to-get entity will become easy-to-get. emperumān, with his strength, will protect the cētana such that the cētana’s powerlessness will get negated. If the second meaning is considered (with patience), this would mean:  this patience will be useful for emperumān to put up with all the inimical deeds carried out by the cētana which would cause hurdles for him in attaining emperumān.

**māṟṟip peruga muyalvāraip peṟṟāl** – Even the difficult tasks will become easy to achieve if he (a cētana) can get one who will take the cētana’s responsibility on his shoulders so that the cētana doesn’t have to do anything. That person who takes the responsibility will give strength to the cētana and make that also as an act that gives himself strength. He would feel elated that the cētana has been able to achieve so much (while actually the achievement is the other person’s) without any knowledge or strength \[who can be such a person other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)!\]. It is implied here that he \[emperumān\] can be attained only through his grace and if we take any effort that would become the cause for even losing him. As an example, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is citing the case of gajēndran, the elephant.

**kariyadōr veṇ kōttu māl yānai** – the elephant which is black in colour, has white coloured tusks and is huge.

**venri mudittanṟē taṇ kōttu māmalarāl tāḻndu** – \[gajēndran\] the elephant entered the pond, plucked a lotus flower and was harassed by a crocodile. To remove the sorrow of the elephant that it may not be able to offer the flower to the divine feet of emperumān before the flower withers away, emperumān came running to the bank of the pond with his dress and crown diśevelled and took  the elephant to safety, on the bank. Once he reached the bank, didn’t gajēndaran offer the flower at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and remove his enemy! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) called the elephant, which was troubled by the crocodile, as *kariyadōr veṇkōttu māl yānai* (black coloured, huge elephant with white tusks). The purpose of describing the colour of the elephant and the tusks when the elephant was in such a condition \[being troubled by crocodile\] is: just as a child falls into a well and the mother thinks “what a beautiful child” and starts thinking about the beauty and the various parts of the child, āḻvār also thinks of the beauty of the elephant in the same way. The world *kōttu* refers to bank (of the pond) and the world *taṇ* means being cool. Did the elephant become victorious after it reached the cool bank and offer the flower? Did it become victorious after submitting the flower at the divine feet? Did it not become victorious with emperumān’s mercy! If the text is *taṇ tōttu māmalarāl* the meaning will be “huge flower with cool petals”.  When emperumān had not taken on himself the protection of gajēndran, the elephant was under the crocodile’s control. As soon as emperumān took over its protection, the hurdle for elephant disappeared. The one who is protected with emperumān’s mercy will be strong. When emperumān is angry and does not protect, the enemies will be strong. Just as emperumān himself  has mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 12-7](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/12-7/) “*tēśām aham samutdārtā mrutyu samsāra sāgarāt*” (I save them from the ocean of samsāram), he will protect. When he is angry, he will push us into difficulties, as mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 16-19 “*kśipāmi ajasramaśubān āsurīśvēva yōniśu*” (I will push them into demonic births). Thus \[if we leave out protection to emperumān\] the difficult task will become easy. All that is required of [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) for this to happen is not to obstruct when emperumān wants to shower his mercy on him.

We shall move on to the 23<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram, next.

## 23. Talndu varam kondu

*tāḻndu varam koṇḍu takka vagaigaḷāl vāḻndu kaḻivārai vāḻvikkum tāḻnda viḷanganikkuk kanṟeṟindu vēṟṟuruvāy jñālam aḷandu adikkīḻ koṇḍa avan*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāḻnda – (due to the weight of its fruits, the branches of the wood apple tree remained) lowered; kanikku – for plucking the fruits; kanṟu eṟindu – throwing a calf (as a throwing stick); vēṟu uru āy – inapt form of a mendicant as vāmana (for one who has long hands due to the habit of always giving to others); jñālam – the earth; aḷandu – measuring; adi kīḻ – under his divine feet; koṇḍa – had under his control; avan – that emperumān; tāḻndu – vorśipping at his divine feet; varam koṇḍu – attaining his divine feet; takka vagaigaḷāl – appropriate manner (to their states); vāḻndu – obtaining wealth or kaivalyam (ātmā enjoying itself) or bhagavat prāpti (attaining emperumān himself); kaḻivārai – those entities who wish to increase their comforts more and more; vāḻvikkum – emperumān will grant them

**Перевод:**

**tāḻndu – unlike rāvaṇa who said, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “dvidā bhajyēyamapi na namēyam” (even if I were split into two, I will not worship him), giving up one’s ego and falling at the divine feet of emperumān**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān, who is attained like this, will remove the hurdles which are like enemies of his follower, will be very simple, and will fulfil all the wishes of his follower. He will give wealth to those who ask for it, kaivalyānubhavam (ātmā experiencing itself) to those who ask for it and himself to those who want to attain him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings\|:*

**varam koṇḍu** – attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and getting with his mercy instead of trying on one’s own efforts to get the desired benefits

**varam koṇḍu** – implies that instead of being humble just for one day, having humility permanently

**takka vagaigaḷāl** – attaining as per the characteristics of the ātmā. ōr attaining, following the ways mentioned in śāstras (sacred texts). ōr, as per the worthiness of ātmā. One who desires wealth should think of emperumān to be always with wealth, worship him and think of emperumān at the time of death. For one who desires *kaivalyam,* he should think of the purity of emperumān. One who desires [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself should think of emperumān with his auspicious qualities. This is why [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) used the term *tāḻndu varam koṇḍu*.

**vāḻndu kaḻivārai vāḻvikkum** – He will let live those who desire to live life fully. He will protect those who spend their time thinking only of emperumān and considering that as their life.

**takka vagaigaḷāl vāḻndu kaḻivārai** – He will grant the benefits to those who seek them and let them live. He will call those who come to him for alms, as said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-18](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-18/) “*udārā:*” (magnanimous persons). However, there are some hurdles in obtaining from him and these do not go easily. Even these will be removed by himself.

**tāḻnda viḷanganikkuk kanṟeṟindu** – throwing a calf to fell the fruits which had grown from top to bottom in the wood apple tree. Thus, he caused harm to the two demons, vatsāsuran and kapittāsuran who wanted to harm him \[these two demons had entered the calf and wood apple fruit, respectively, with the intention of killing kriṣṇa\].

**vēṟṟuruvāy** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the most magnanimous as brought out in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 21-7 “*gosahasrapradātāram*” (one who gives away a thousand cows). Yet he made himself as a mendicant \[to take his possessions from mahābali\]. ōr, one who is the consort of *tiru* (śrī mahālakśmi) went asking for alms. ōr, initially he asked for three steps of land, showing his small feet \[as vāmana\] and once it was granted, transformed himself \[into trivikrama\] and measured with huge feet.

**jñālam aḷandu adikkīḻk koṇḍa avan** – after measuring earth, he kept it under his divine foot. Will such an entity give up on one who desires [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself?

**jñālam aḷandu adikkīḻk koṇḍa avan** – just as a mother will embrace her sleeping child, emperumān also kept his divine feet on the earth, unknown to the earth, and measured it. Hence he will let his followers prosper.

We shall move on to the 24<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 24. Avan kanday nanneje

*avan kaṇḍāy nannenjē āraruḷum kēdum avan kaṇḍāy aimpulanāy ninṟān avan kaṇḍāy kāṟṟuttīnīrvān karuvarai maṇ kārōda sīṟṟattīyāvānum senṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nenjē – Oh (my) good heart!; Ar aruḷum – mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which results due to complete mercy; kēdum – samsāram (materialistic realm) which results due to aversion; avan kaṇḍāy – it is that emperumān himself, see for yourself; aimpulan – the five senses; kāṟṟu, tī, nīr, vān, maṇ āy ninṟan avan kaṇḍāy – it is the sarvēśvaran (supreme being) himself who is existing as the body composed of the five elements; karu varai – the dark mountains; (avan kaṇḍāy) – it is he; senṟu – contemplating (to bring to an end this universe); kār ōdam sīṟṟam tī āvānum – the creator of the fire which will burn the ocean with a rage; avan kaṇḍāy – it is that emperumān only

**Перевод:**

**avan kaṇḍāy nannenjē āraruḷum kēdum – it is not the cētana’s (sentient entity) efforts which are the reason for his uplifting. It is emperumān’s mercy alone which is the reason for the cētana getting various benefits. It is not merely the activities of the cētana, but the causeless grace of emperumān which leads to benefits for the cētana. It is emperumān’s anger which again is the reason for the difficulties experienced by the cētana. Thus, for whatever benefits or difficulties that the cētana gets, it is emperumān’s grace and anger which are the reasons. There is no puṇya or pāpa beyond this. We can also say that it is emperumān’s mercy which is the reason for the cētana getting mōkśam and his anger, for the cētana getting samsāram (repeated births in materialistic realm).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that after the jīvātmā was created, emperumān has given him everything. Is it something great? He says, right from the beginning, did not emperumān carry out everything for him, including his existence! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nannenjē** – Oh heart who agrees with humility when told that emperumān is the cause for both puṇya and pāpa. Oh heart who is apt to listen about emperumān.

**avan kaṇḍāy aimpulanāy ninṟān** – It is he who creates a thought in us that he is needed for us. It is he who exists as our sensory perceptions which determine whether we do or do not attain him. The senses will be under his control whether we say that we exist and emperumān doesn’t or if we accept that emperumān exists. It is he who kindles the sensory perceptions to engage with worldly pursuits and leads us astray or makes the sensory perceptions to engage with him and protects us. Both the chain (with which to tie us down) and the crown (with which to take us to glory) are in his hands. He has mercifully said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 10-10](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/10-10/) “*dadāmi bhuddiyōgam tam yēna māmupayānti tē*” (I give you intellect which is the means for attaining me). He has also mercifully mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 16-19 “*tānaham dviśada: krūrān samsārēśu narādamān kśipāmi ajasram aśubān āsurīśvēva yōniśu*” (I always push those who hate me, who are cruel, who are the worst among the men and who are inauspicious, into samsāram (materialistic realm) and that too in demonic birth).

**avan kaṇḍāy kāṟṟut tī nīr vān karuvarai maṇ** – it is he who creates these with the help of the five elements, which are responsible for the cētana losing emperumān.

**kāṟṟut tī nīr vān** – since it is the physical body which is a hurdle for attaining him, it is he who gives the body and nurtures samsāram. As said in [śrī bhagavat gīta 7-14](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-14/) “*davīhyēśā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā, māmēva yē prapadyantē māyāmētām tarantitē*” (this prakruti (matter) which is composed of the three qualities \[sātvika or good, rājasa or passionate and tāmasa or ignorant) has been created by me. This cannot be overcome by anyone on his own efforts. Only those who surrender to me will be able to overcome it), we have to surrender to him and sever our connection with this worldly life.

**kāṟṟut tī nīr vān karuvarai maṇ** – It is he who creates the five elements and the huge mountains which are responsible for creating the body with which one can attain either life in this samsāram or mōkśam.

**kārōdac cīṟṟat tī āvānum senṟu** – at the time of total annihilation \[deluge\] at the end of the yuga (age), it is he who manifests as the huge fire which destroys all these entities. *kārōdac cīṟṟattī* is the fire called as *patapāgni* which forms in the middle of the ocean. In other words it is the fire which burns water (which would normally put out the fire)! Thus, it is he who controls those which are to be given up and which are to be attained.

Let us move on to the 25<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 25. Senradu ilangai mel

*senṟadu ilangai mēl sevvē tan sīṟṟattāl konṟadu irāvaṇananik kūṟungāl ninṟaduvum vēyōngu taṇ sāral vēngadamē viṇṇavar tam vāyōngu tol pugaḻān vandu*

**Пословный перевод:**

viṇṇavar tam vāy – apt to be praised by the mouth of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); ōngu tol pugaḻān – emperumān with great, long established qualities; sevvē – directly; senṟadu – went with a rage; ilangai mēl – on lankā; than sīṟṟattāl – with his anger; konṟadu – destroyed; irāvaṇanai – it was rāvaṇa; kūṟungāl – when mentioned; vandu ninṟaduvum – the place where he stood (so that all cētanas (sentient entities) would get uplifted at all times); vēy ōngu taṇ sāral vēngadamē – at tiruvēngadamalai with cool, mountainous sides and tall bamboo shoots

**Перевод:**

**senṟadu ilangai mēl – this says that unlike rāvaṇa who sent \[his uncle\] mārīca as a deceptive deer and made rāma to travel a long distance behind it in order to capture sītāppirātti in a cunning way, \[śrīrāma\] in keeping with his valour, went directly to lankā, waged a war and destroyed rāvaṇa.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram it was mentioned that emperumān stands for all. In this pāsuram his bias towards his followers is mentioned. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**senṟadu ialangai mēl sevvē** – instead of through his sankalpam (vow), he went directly to lankā walking with his divine feet. As mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 1-29 “*abhiyātā*” (one who fights in the forefront), śrī rāma went in the forefront of his army and fought.

**ilangai mēl** – he invaded fearlessly on lankā, a place which entities such as indra et al would be scared even to mention by name.

**than sīṟṟattāl konṟadu irāvaṇanai** – with his anger, he destroyed the heads of rāvana, who, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam “*prahartā ca*” (one who attacks) was protected by various boons.

**than sīṟṟattāl** – one who has anger under his control. He sought anger and destroyed rāvaṇa. He could carry out tasks with his sankalpam (vow) only if he were with the nature of *paramporuḷ* (supreme entity). In rāmāvatāram (incarnation as śrī rāma), since he had truly assumed the characteristics of a human being, he destroyed rāvaṇa with his anger alone.

**kūṟungāl** – if one were to mention the characteristics of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s

**ninṟaduvum vēy ōngu taṇ sāral vēngadmē** – in order to help those who had missed out during his various ages of incarnation so that they do not lose out, the place where he came down to is tirumalai (tiruvēngadam) with its cool, hilly sides which remove the tiredness of those who climb the hills, and with well grown tall bamboo shoots.

**ninṟaduvum vēngadamē** – He has come to tirumalai so that he can remove the enemies of his followers who had come after the times of his incarnations. Just as he redeemed sītāppirātti who was captured by rāvaṇa, he is remaining at tirumalali in order to redeem the ātmās (souls) which have been stolen by [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who claimed them as their own.

**viṇṇavar tam tol pugaḻān**  – this mentions the place that he had dvelled in, before coming to tirumalai. He is one who has natural greatness, and is praised by the nityasūris in śrīvaikuṇṭam. We can also construe this as one who has long established qualities, praised by nityasūris. ōr, since the context is śrī rāmāyaṇam, we can consider this as the praise of brahmā et al who said, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 117-27 “sītā lakshmi: bhavān viṣṇu:” (sītā is [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) and you are [viṣṇu](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)).

**vandu** – he came as the one who seeks to offer himself, to tirumalai.

We shall take up the 26<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 26. Vandittavanai

*vandittavanai vaḻi ninṟa aim bhūtam aindum agattadakki ārvamāy undip padiyamarar vēlaiyān paṇḍu amararkkīnda padiyamarar vāḻum padi*

**Пословный перевод:**

padi amarar – śrīvaiṣṇavas; vāḻum – living permanently; padi – the holy place of tirumalai \[tiruvēngadam\]; vaḻi ninṟa – standing as a hurdle (to attain emperumān); aimbhūtam aindum – the five elements and the five sensory perceptions; agattadakki – controlling well inside so that they do not wander out; avanai – that emperumān; vandittu – vorśipping; ārvam āy – as epitome of affection; undi – pushing and falling over one another; padi – coming and vorśipping; amarar – for dēvas \[celestial entities\]; vēlaiyān – emperumān who is in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); paṇḍu amararkku īnda – the place which he gave to the ancient dēvas, nityasūris

**Перевод:**

**vandittu – praising emperumān. Without any ego, attaining emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since āḻvār spoke about tirumalai in the previous pāsuram, the tirumalai hill attracted him more. His divine mind goes to it and he speaks about the wealth in tirumalai. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vaḻi ninṟa aimbhūtam aindum agattu adakki** – controlling inside the evanescent body those matters which are hurdles in attaining emperumān, without letting them go through the senses such as ear etc.

**aimb hūtam aindum** – being victorious over the five elements and the five sensory perceptions.

**ārvamāy** – being engaged fully with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)

**undip padi amarar vēlaiyān** – emperumān, who is reclining in *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean), who is attained by dēvas such as brahmā et al, with each pushing the others \[as if in a competition, to attain emperumān first\] and vorśipping him.

**paṇḍu amararkku īnda padi** – this could be construed in two ways: (1) given to the long standing nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); (2) given earlier to nityasūris.

What was given to the nityasūris?

**padiyamarar vāḻum padi** – tirumalai where the dēvas of  bhūlōkam, śrīvaiṣṇavas (followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who dwell on the earth); tirumalai where they live in total experience.

[\[parāśara\] bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) (son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/), one of the primary disciples of [bhagavad śrī rāmānujar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) told piḷḷai amudanār that this pāsuram should be read as: *vaḻi ninṟa aimbhūtam aindum agattadakki ārvamāy vandittu undippadiyamarar vēlaiyān padiyamarar vāḻum padiyānadu paṇḍu amararkku īndadu*.

We shall take up the 27<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 27. Padi amaindu nadi

*padi amaindu nādip parutteḻunda sindai madiyurinji  vān mugadu nōkki gadi miguttu angōl tēdiyōdum koḻundadē pōnṟadē māl tediyōdum manam*

**Пословный перевод:**

padi – in the pious place of tirumalai; amaindu – standing aptly; nādi – analysing; paruttu eḻunda sindai – having mind which grows in a rousing manner; madi urinji – going past the world of moon; vān mugadu nōkki – going (beyond) past the wall of the universe; māl tēdi – going in search of paramapadanādan (lord of śrīvaikuṇṭam); gadi miguttu ōdum – going very rapidly; manam – my mind; am kōl tēdi ōdum – going in search of a beautiful supporting pole; koḻundu adu pōnṟadu – it resembled a creeper

**Перевод:**

**padi amaindu nādi – standing aptly in tirumalai. Ensuring that this is the place to be attained. padi could also be construed to mean ‘heart’ and as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-25 “aham sarvam kariśyāmi” (lakśmaṇa told śrī rāma that he would carry out all services), the heart also affirmed.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that once he reached the dwelling place of emperumān \[tirumalai\] his divine mind went running towards emperumān just as a creeper would go in search of a supporting pole. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about how emperumān dwells in tirumalai and mercifully showers his grace. Since that abode is in arcāvatāram (emperumān in deity form), he cannot interact with emperumān fully. He says that his heart runs searching for emperumān in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) so that he can interact with emperumān in all the ways he liked. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nādi** – analysing. Searching everywhere \[for emerpumān\]

**paruttu eḻunda sindai** – the mind, going up rousingly, as a result of that

**parutteḻunda** – rising rapidly.

**madi urinji** – going beyond the world where moon traverses

**vān mugadu nōkki** – going up to the wall of the universe

**gadi miguttu** – increasing its speed. If the text is taken to be **kadir miguttu**  it will mean increasing its radiance.

**angōl tēdi ōdum koḻundadē pōnṟadē** –  it is similar to a creeper going in search of a beautiful supporting pole.

**māl tēdi ōdum manam** – it is the way the mind is, going head over heels, looking for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**māl** – one with affection. Like the river kāvēri breaching its banks and running wildly, my mind is running in search of emperumān who is full in all aspects and who is greater than everyone else.

**māl tēdi ōdum manam** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in his tiruvāimoḻi 9-3-7, has mercifully said “*māga vaikundam kāṇbadaṟku en manam ēkam eṇṇum*” (to see the paramākāśam (the supreme sky) or śrīvaikuṇṭam), my mind will think in a focussed way).

We shall go on to the 28<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

## 28. Manattullan

*manattuḷḷān vēngadattān mākadalān maṟṟum ninaippariya nīḷarangattuḷḷān enaippalarum dēvādi dēvan enappaduvān munnorunāḷ māvāy piḷanda magan*

**Пословный перевод:**

enai palarum – countless vaidika puruśas (those who follow vēdam, the sacred text) and vēda puruśa (vēdam itself); thus by all entities; dēvādi dēvan enap paduvān – he is famously called as the lord of all dēvas (celestial entities); mā kadalān – one who is reclining on the expansive tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); mun oru nāḷ – once upon a time (when he incarnated as śrī kriṣṇa); mā vāy piḷanda – one who tore the mouth of a demon who came in the form of a horse, kēśi; magan – a small child; maṟṟum – more than that; vēngadattān – one who, as simplicity personified, stands in tirumalai; ninaippariya nīḷ arangatu uḷḷān – one who is reclining in the temple which is sweet beyond anyone’s thoughts; manattu uḷḷān – he is permanently residing in my mind

**Перевод:**

**manattuḷḷān – Just as periyāḻvār had mercifully mentioned in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-10 “idavagaigaḷ igaḻndittu enpāl idavagai koṇḍanaiyē” (leaving aside all other dwelling places, you decided to stay in my heart), emperumān left all other places and entered āḻvār‘s mind.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As āḻvār’s divine mind started engaging with emperumān this way, emperumān, instead of dwelling at places that he had seen, even though they were all suitable, chose to keep āḻvār’s divine mind as his dwelling place, and entered it. He did not go to any other place, deciding to remain in āḻvār’s divine mind. In other words, this pāsuram says that as soon as āḻvār desired emeprumān’s inseparable place of śrīvaikuṇṭam, emperumān desired āḻvār’s divine mind. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vēngadattān mākadalān** – just before entering [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/)’s divine mind, the places where he had taken residence – tiruppāṟkadal and tiruvēngadam (it is implied that he had taken residence in those places only to attract āḻvār and through those places he entered āḻvār’s divine mind). Just as he had taken residence at tiruppāṟkadal before standing in tirumalai, in order to enter āḻvār’s mind, he stood in tirumalai.

**maṟṟum ninaippariya nīḷ arangatt uḷḷān** – unlike tiruppāṟkadal or tirumalai, one cannot say tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) in one word. It is very sweet and very huge and suited [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s reclining. He reclines in this place as if to tell everyone that he will not leave this place until he attains everyone and seems to be staging a protest! Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “*aprāpya manasā saha*” (the words returned with the mind, being unable to measure emperumān), it may be possible to measure emperumān’s auspicious qualities, but it is impossible to measure the sweetness of tiruvarangam.

**nīḷ arangam** – very expansive temple.

**enaip palarum dēvādi dēvan enappaduvān** – vēdas (sacred texts) and vaidika puruśas (those who practice vēdam) say, as in śvētāsvatara upaniśat “*tamīśvarāṇām paramam mahēśvaram*” (you are the Lord of all the Lords) and as in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/) “*ayarvaṟum amarargaḷ adipati*” (he is the Lord of nityasūris who do not forget anything).

**paṇḍorunāḷ māvāy piḷanda magan** – earlier, when he incarnated as kriṣṇa, he, as a child, had torn the mouth of a demon by name kēśi. ōr, one who incarnated as a human being. Since he destroyed kēśi, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) removes the enemy who is a hurdle in emperumān entering his heart. One who is called the Lord of all Lords, one who is in tiruppāṟkadal, one who tore the mouth of a demon, one who reclines in tiruvarangam which is beyond anyone’s thoughts, one who resides in tiruvēngadam, entered my heart. One who is the Lord of nityasūris, one who reclined in tiruppāṟkadal in order to protect the worlds, who incarnated as kaṇṇan, who destroyed kēśi, the demon, who is the Lord of all Lords, who is reclining in the immeasurable tiruvarangam, one who is staying put at tiruvēngadam instead of moving here and there, desired my heart and instead of going to any other place, took permanent residence there itself, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/).

We shall take up the 29<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 29. Maganagak kondu

*maganāgak koṇḍu eduttāḷ māṇbāya kongai aganāra uṇban enṟu uṇḍu maganait tāy tēṟāda vaṇṇam tiruttināy tennilangai nīṟāga eydu aḻittaāy nī*

**Пословный перевод:**

tennilangai – the beautiful lankā; nīṟu āga – to be converted into ashes; eydu – shooting arrows; aḻittāy – Oh one who destroyed; nī – you; magan āga koṇḍu eduttāḷ – (in order to suckle you with affection) the one who took a form to make you as her child, such pūtanā’s; māṇbu āya kongai – beautiful bosom; agan Ara uṇban enṟu uṇḍu – saying that you would drink to your stomach’s content, thus drinking (her life along with her milk); maganai – in your matter, as her child; tāy – yaśōdā, your mother; tēṟāda vaṇṇam – to be without trust; tiruttināy – you carried out

**Перевод:**

**maganāgak koṇḍu eduttāḷ – pūtanā, carried you skilfully just as a real mother would lift her infant, without displaying any difference. While you were born in śrī maturā, as the son of vasudēvar, and grew up in tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam) as \[nandagōpar’s\] son, putanā also acted as if you were her child.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since āḻvār spoke about kriṣṇāvatāram \[incarnation as kriṣṇa\] through mā vāy piḷanda in the previous pāsuram, he becomes bewildered, being involved with that avatāram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māṇbāya kongai** – her bosom looked beautiful, being filled up with milk, which was saturated with poison

**agan Ara uṇban enṟu uṇḍu** – Just as pūtanā lifted you \[as her son\] without displaying any difference, kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) also drank milk as if he were nursing from his mother’s bosom, without displaying any difference. His position was very much similar to pūtanā’s. Just like she could not sustain herself without nursing him, he too drank her milk as if he could not sustain himself without that. He drank such that his stomach would feel contented.

**maganait tāy tēṟāda vaṇṇam tiruttināy** – you acted in such a way that your mother, yaśōdā, will not have any trust in you. If someone says “son”, it will refer only to you, just as it would refer only to yaśōdā if someone were to say “mother”. Just like it would refer to daśarata cakravarty if someone says “affection towards son”, this is similar to that. You acted in such a way that your mother would start fretting as to what would happen to you, seeing you drinking poisoned milk from a demonic woman. You acted such that she would fret thinking about this, even after you had grown up. Alternatively, you acted such that no mother would believe her son henceforth. In other words, you acted in such a way that after giving birth to you, your mother yaśōdā was scared that she had lost you, since you were in the hold of pūtanā.

**tiruttināy** – you touched her.

**tennilangai nīṟāga eydu aḻittāy nī** – The act of burning lankā to ashes was carried out when \[[śrī rāma](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had\] matured into a man, displaying his valour. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is worried that suckling pūtanā was done when he was still an infant, before maturing of age. Did he carry out that act when a parent would feel proud that his/her son had gone to the battlefield and would return victorious (like śrī rāma)? Did he not do that act of suckling pūtana, as an infant! If one were to think about rāmāvatāram, one could keep his hand on the chest (and be without any fear) just as it is mentioned in nānmugan tiruvandādi “*kūrambanallāl*” (no one other than the one with sharp arrows (śrī rāma)). You \[referring to [kriṣṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] who had the fame as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 44-7 “*kīrtibhūtām patākām yō lōkē bhramayati prabhū: kinnāma durlabhām tasya*” (what is it which cannot be attained by śri rāma who let fly his flag called fame!), you who had carried out deeds with a bow in your hands, blowing lankā to smithereens, carried out an act which made your mother not trust her child.

We shall take up the 30<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 30. Niyanru ulagalanday

*nīyanṟu ulagaḷandāy nīṇḍa tirumālē nīyanṟu ulagidandāy enbarāl nīyanṟu kārōdam mun kadaindu pin adaittāy mākadalai pēroda mēnip pirān*

**Пословный перевод:**

pērōdam mēnip pirān – Oh benefactor who has the divine form like a huge ocean!; nīṇḍa tirumālē – Oh tirumāl, who has fame beyond one’s thoughts!; nī – you, who are like these; anṟu – once upon a time; ulagu – all the worlds; aḷandāy – measured (as trivikrama); nī anṟu – you, at another point of time; ulagu – the earth; idandāy – (as the great varāha) dug out the earth (from the walls of the universe); nī anṟu – you, at another point of time; kār ōdam mun kadaindu – churned the dark ocean initially; mā kadalai – a huge ocean (like that); pin – later (during rāmāvatāram); adaittāy – built a bridge and blocked; enbar – so say (the great sages); Al – these are also some of the amazing activities

**Перевод:**

**nī anṟu ulagu aḷandāy – sages say that you made yourself as a mendicant, approached mahābali, who becomes joyous on donating anything, got the worlds as alms from him and measured them with your divine feet. Since āḻvār spoke in the previous pāsuram about kriṣṇāvatāram in which he moved with everyone without bothering about whether they were high or low in status, mentions in this pāsuram about trivikrama, who touched the heads of everyone with his divine feet \[without any distinction\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram āḻvār mentioned about the victories of kaṇṇan and śri Rama. In this pāsuram he celebrates \[emperumān’s \] other incarnations and his activities. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nīṇḍa tirumālē** – Oh the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), and who is beyond any measurement.

**nīnda tirumālē** – you measured the earth which you got as alms and which appeared as if it was something which you could never have got otherwise. You, who are immeasurable, made yourself to be measured by others through this act! In other words, you, the consort of mahālakśmi, who is the owner of all wealth, made yourself into a mendicant and a bachelor!

**nī anṟu ulagu idandāy enbarāl** – sages say that since there was none from whom you can ask for alms when the world was being submerged during *praḷayam* (deluge), you took the form of a boar which roams around in water and marshy lands, to release the earth which had got stuck to the walls of the universe and to dig out.

**nī anṟu kārōdam mun kadaindu pin adaittāy** – mention is made of his activities for *pirātti* (śrī mahālakśmi). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) reminisces about the activities which he carried out for others and for mahālakśmi without any difference. When indra (head of celestial entities) and others surrendered to you after losing all their possessions due to the curse of sage durvāsa, you churned the great, black coloured ocean, first to get śrī mahālakśmi and then blocked it (by building a bridge over it during rāmāvatāram).

**pin adaittāy mā kadalai** – you blocked the ocean, which you churned \[initially\] for the sake of pirātti, again for the sake of pirātti.

**pēr ōda mēnip pirān** – the one who has the complexion of ocean, churned the ocean. During the time of churning as well as during the time of blocking, it appeared as if one ocean was shaking another.

**pērōda mēni** – the ocean would appear like a pond when viewed against emperumān.

**pirān** – benefactor. emperumān appeared to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) as nectar, without having to churn the ocean to obtain nectar. emperumān gave the nectar which he obtained by churning the ocean, to dēvas (celestial entities) while he offered himself, who is ārāvamudan (insatiable nectar) to āḻvār. *mā kadal* refers to huge ocean. One can construe the meaning for this term as “benefactor who has a form matching huge flood”

**pērōda mēnippirān nī anṟu ulagaḷandāy** – Should one measure the world which are composed of forests and craters, with a beautiful form matching ocean!

We shall take up the 31<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 31. Piran enru nalum

*pirān enṟu nāḷum perum pulari enṟum kurā naṟseḻum pōdu koṇḍu varāgattu aṇiyuruvan pādam paṇiyum avar kaṇḍīr maṇi uruvam kāṇbār magiḻndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

piṟan enṟum – Oh one who carried out great benefits!; peru pulari – today is the dawn of happiness; nāḷum – every day; nal – fragrant; seḻu – beautiful; kurā pōdu koṇḍu – with kurā flowers (a type of flower from forest trees); varāgattu aṇi uruvan pādam – divine feet of emperumān in the form of beautiful wild boar; paṇiyum avar kaṇḍir – only those who worship; maṇi uruvam – the sweet, auspicious form (of that emperumān); magiḻndu kānbar – will worship with happiness

**Перевод:**

**pirān enṟum – he made others worship himself, being the destined leader for the ātmā (soul) and benefactor . Even while understanding this, one would feel “what did my leader do!”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that those who attain the divine feet of emperumān who always helps in times of danger, would enjoy his aprākruta (not made of the five elements that we are made of) divine form with happiness. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nāḷum perum pulari enṟum** – In this useless, unfruitful materialistic life, during hard times, a day would come when one can enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! It would be the dawn of a good day! Celebrating such a day just like akrūrar (a minister with the demon kamsan) did when he went to see kaṇṇan, as mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-3 “*adya mē saphālam janma suprabhātā ca mē niśā*” (Only today my birth became useful! Only today, my night dawned in to a great day!).

**pirān enṟum** – it has been mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-7-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-7-6-piran-perunilam/) “*pirān perunilam kīṇḍavan*” (the benefactor dug out the huge land mass (earth))

**perum pulari** – it has been mercifully mentioned in [tiruppallāṇḍu 12](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-12-pallandu-enru/) “*nallāṇḍu enṟu navinṟu uraippar*” (they will celebrate all the time saying that good times have come). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) celebrates saying that even for him, who spends his time thinking of lowly creatures, a day has dawned when he could think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), vorśipping his good time.

**kurā nal seḻum pōdu koṇḍu** – emperumān considers whatever his followers keep at this divine feet as flowers. Don’t only those who worship emperumān with flowers and attain him get to see his divine form!

**kurā nal seḻum pōdu koṇḍu** – since this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) had constantly travelled only in forests, he says that it is enough if one worships emperumān with the flowers of *kurā* tree which is seen only in forests.

**varāgattu aṇi uruvan pādam** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) assumed the form of *varāha* (wild boar) which roams in the forest, so that people can worship him with flowers which blossom in the forest.

**aṇi uruvan** – since he assumed the form of a great boar, there was no necessity to decorate him with ornaments. His form itself appeared as if it had been decorated with ornaments. If emperumān can assume any form, it is enough if he can be decorated with any flower.

**paṇiyum avar kaṇḍīr** – [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-2](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-2/) says “*susukham kartum avyayam*” (*bhaktiyōgam* (the practice of devotion) is comfortable to carry out; it does not get destroyed even after yielding the result). In the same way, the path adopted to attain emperumān is sweetest. The path to get the fruit of enjoying emperumān is also a benefit.

**maṇi uruvam kāṇbār magiḻndu** – thus, those who offer flowers to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will happily get to have vision of his divine form which is cool like a bluish gem.

We shall take up the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 32. Magilndadu sindai

*magiḻndadu sindai tirumālē maṟṟum magiḻndadu nin pādamē pōṟṟi magiḻndadu aḻal āḻi sangam avai pādiyādum toḻil āgam sūḻndu tuṇindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālē – Oh the consort of mahālakśmi!; sindai – my heart; sūḻndu – engaged; tuṇindu – with firmness; magiḻndadu – became happy; maṟṟum – my faculty of speech, after my heart; un pādamē pōṟṟi – vorśipping only your divine feet; magiḻndadu – became happy; āgam – my body; aḻal – one that spits fire on enemies; āḻi – the divine cakkara (disc); sangam – śrī pāncajanyam (conch); avai – and other divine weapons; pādi ādum – singing and dancing; toḻil – in the activity; magiḻndadu – became happy

**Перевод:**

**magiḻndadu sindai – the mind, which had never known what happiness is, became happy after meditating on emperumān. āḻvār says that due to this, there is no need for him to go to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and become happy. He says that he will be happy, meditating on emperumān, here itself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully says how his sensory perceptions, without waiting for his upadēśam (spiritual instructions) engaged with emperumān. He says that his mind, speech and bodily faculties engaged with emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tirumālē** – this is saying about the cause of happiness. īsn’t it a matter of happiness when emerpumān is with pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)!

**maṟṟum magiḻndadu un pādamē pōṟṟi** – after my mind, my faculty of speech praised your divine feet and became happy. Here too, we can add the word *tirumālē* and say that speech praised tirumāl (consort of śrī mahālakśmi) and became happy.

**magiḻndadu aḻal āḻi sangam avai pādi ādum toḻil āgam sūḻndu tuṇindu** – the body engaged in the activity of singing and dancing about *cakrāyudam* (divine disc) which becomes angry and spits fire on enemies, and *pāncajanyam* (divine conch) and became firmly happy. We can also construe the meaning for this as: the body which (vorśipped) firmly became happy; the mind, which vorśipped firmly, became happy; the speech, which vorśipped firmly, became happy. Other parts of the body also became happy after vorśipping firmly. Alternatively, we can say that the mind praised [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet and became happy; the speech praised his divine feet and became happy; the body praised his divine feet and became happy.

We shall move on to the 33<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.
