## 33. Tunindadu sindai

*tuṇindadu sindai tuḻāy alangal angam aṇindavan pēr uḷḷattup palgāl paṇindaduvum vēypiṟangu sāral viṟal vēngadavanaiyē vāy tiṟanaḷ sollum vagai*

**Пословный перевод:**

sindai – my mind; tuḻāy alangal (angam) aṇindavan pēr – the divine names of emperumān who is donning the tuḷasi garland on his divine form; palgāl – many times; uḷḷattu – in thinking; tuṇindadu – had firm, deep faith; angam – my body too; palgāl – always; paṇindadu – worships; vēy piṟangu sāral – having foothills with plenty of bamboo; viral vēngadavanaiyē – only emperumān who is residing in tirumalai and is strong; vāy – mouth \[speech\] too; tiṟangaḷ – (emperumān’s) qualities; sollum vagai – in the way it says; tuṇindadu – became confident

**Перевод:**

**tuṇindadu sindai tuḻāy alangal angam aṇindavan pēr uḷḷattu – my heart became firm in meditating on the divine names of emperumān who is decorated with tuḷasi garland. Here the word uḷḷam is used figuratively to refer to meditating. Alternatively, we can take the two terms uḷḷattu sindai together, which would mean the thoughts in the mind, and say that the thoughts in the mind were firmly engaged in meditating on the divine names of emperumān who has donned tuḷasi garland on his body.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The previous pāsuram mentioned about the happiness arising out of enjoying emperumān with all three faculties (of mind, speech and body). In this pāsuram, āḻvār mercifully says how the three faculties engaged with emperumān to enjoy him. Was it only for enjoying him? āḻvār says that apart from him, these three faculties would not go to any other place. Thus this pāsuram says that they exist only for him and none else. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tuḻāy alangal angam** – this mentions the beauty of the entity which made the mind to become firm \[in meditating on his divine names\]

**angam alangal** – my body, always

**paṇindaduvum vēy piṟangaḷ sāral vēngadavanaiyē** – only tiruvēngadamudaiyān, who resides in tirumalai which has bamboo in abundance on its foothills, and who engages us with his beauty to be fully involved with him.

**vāy tiṟangaḷ sollum vagai tuṇindadu** – my mouth (faculty of speech) became confident in talking about his \[auspicious\] qualities. It became confident in talking about matters connected to him.

**vēngadavanaiyē** – it will not be confident about anyone else other than him, who does not know anything about anyone other than us.

The sequence to be followed to get the meanings correctly is: *tuṇindadu sindai tuḻāy alangal angam aṇindavan pēr uḷḷattu; angam palagāl paṇindaduvum vēy piṟangu sāral viral vēngadavanaiyē; avan tiṟangaḷai vāy sollum vagai tuṇindadu*.

We shall move on to the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 34. Vagaiyal avani

*vagaiyāl avani irandaḷandāy pādam pugaiyāḷ naṟumalarāl munnē migavāynda anbākki ētti adimaippattēn unakku en bākkiyattāl ini*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnē – in earlier time; vagaiyāl – through a strategy; avani – earth; irandu – (from mahābali) taking as alms; aḷandāy – measured with your; pādam – divine feet; pugaiyāḷ – with smoke; naṟu malarāl – with good, fragrant flowers; miga vāynda anbu ākki – as one with lot of affection; ētti – praising; ini – henceforth; en bākkiyattāl – the fortune through the special mercy that you showered on me; unakku adimai pattēn – I became your servitor

**Перевод:**

**You carried out activities which made me lose out to your beauty and become your servitor**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells emperumān that he obtained all the great fortune due to his mercy. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vagaiyāḷ avani irandu aḷandāy pādam** – your divine feet with which you measured the earth which you obtained through a strategy by which mahābali, who has demonic qualities, would give willingly donate with compassion.

**vagaiyāl** – even though mahābali was fit to be killed like rāvaṇa, since he had the quality of magnanimity, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), instead of killing him, imprisoned him in the nether world after getting the worlds through alms. He subordinated mahābali in such a way that when one tried to beat a snake with a stick, the snake should not be killed and the stick used should not be broken. emperumān strategised and carried out activities such that mahābali would give away the earth, which is not his, thinking that it is his, and that he \[emperumān\] would accept it as if it were not his. Let indra, who has attained me, have his purpose fulfilled.

**irandu aḷandāy** – you carried out the activity even before I had any affection towards you.

**pugaiyāl naṟumalarāl miga vāynda anbākki ētti adimaippattēn unakku** – with the help of implements such as flowers, and with lot of affection towards you, I praised you after losing out to your beauty and surrendered to your divine feet.

What is the cause for this?

**en bākkiyattāl** – with the fortune that I received through your mercy or through the mercy which I did not shun when you came to shower it on me.

We shall move on to the 35<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 35. Inidenbar kamam

*inidenbar kāmam adanilum āṟṟa  inidenbar taṇṇīrum endāy – inidenṟu kāmanīr vēḷādu nin perumai vētparēl sēmanīrāgum siṟidu*

**Пословный перевод:**

endāy – Oh my Lord!; kāmam – lowly pursuits such as śabdam etc (five sensory perceptions such as sound etc); inidu enbar – will consider as fit to be enjoyed; taṇṇīr adanilum āṟṟa inidu enbar – will say that water is sweeter than those pursuits such as śabdam etc; kāmam nīr – the lowly pursuits and water, mentioned above; inidu enṟu vēḷādu – without desiring them as being enjoyable; nin perumai siṟidu vētpar ēl – if they desire even a little bit, you as being enjoyable; sēmam nīr āgum – (in all situations) will have the characteristic of protecting \[them\]

**Перевод:**

**inidu enbar kāmam – worldly people \[those in this samsāram, materialistic realm\] will consider lust as being sweet. āḻvār has heard this from such people. Since he has used the term inidu enbar (will say that it is sweet), it implies that he has had no connection with such pursuits. Just as lowly people will say that the food that they eat is very delicious, people who are after lust will say that it is sweet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that if people of the world do not shun emperumān when he repeatedly comes, even as a mendicant, falling head over heels, and if they do not engage with worldly pursuits, suffering the consequences and are thus a bit well disposed towards him, that would protect them.  He detests that the world thinks \[people of the world think\] that worldly pursuits are sweet when emperumān is like this. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**adanilum āṟṟa inidu enbar taṇṇīrum** – they will say that water is even sweeter than that \[lust\]. Even those who are experts in lust need water to sustain them. Even though the food that people take may differ, isn’t water the same for everyone! Just as it is said “*āpa Eva hi sumana*” (water is liked by all) those who have renounced the world as well as those who haven’t, will be happy about water, without any distinction. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) doesn’t desire even that. Those who are engaged with worldly pursuits will say that lust is sweet and those who are interested in nurturing their bodies will say that water is sweet.  āḻvār has no connection with either. Just as the people of the world cannot know his qualities, he is also unable to know the qualities of worldly people.

**enbar** – people of the world will say that these are sweet. Since he does not exist in this world \[mentally\], he says *will say*.

**endāy** – For him, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the object of lust as well as water. For him, emperumān is the only matter which counts. īsn’t the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) one who considers that emperumān is everything as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-19/) “*vāsudēvas sarvamiti sa mahātmā*” (vāsudēvan is everything to me) and in tiruvāimoḻi 7-1-1 “*uṇṇum sōṟu parugum nīr tinnum veṟṟilai ellām kaṇṇan*” (kaṇṇan is everything to me – the food that nurtures, the water that sustains and the betel leaf that is enjoyed).

**inidu enṟu** – it implies that something which is not sweet is being considered as sweet. It is similar to imagining fire to be water and entering it to remove one’s ennui.  One gets engaged with worldly pursuits only because of lack of good fortune.

**kāma nīr vēḷādu** – not desiring lust and water as sweet, since they are really not sweet.

**nin perumai vētparēl** – if they desire your qualities. Since enjoyment in this world has limitation and is nonsensical, it will not remain unchanged for ever. But in the case of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), since he is very sweet, during the time of attaining him by adopting suitable means as well as enjoying the result of attaining him, it would be very sweet without any limitation and it will not be possible for one to get fulfilment after enjoying him. It would be similar to what has been mentioned in taittiriya upaniśat ānandavalli 7 “*ēśa hyēvānandayāti*” (isn’t it only the [paramātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] making the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) to be happy).

**nin perumal vētparēl** – if one were to ask “if it cannot be avoided, should it not be somehow  enjoyed?”, instead of doing that ….

**nin perumal vētparēl** – one need not desire to enjoy all his greatness. If one were to meditate on his auspicious qualities, that itself would provide protection. One need not lose a hand or leg, unlike what could happen if one were to pursue lust and water mentioned earlier. It is enough if one has the feeling of being friendly towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**sēma nīr āgum** – that will have the quality of protecting that person. emperumān is one who says, just as he said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “*mitrabhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana*” (there is no place for letting go of this person who has come in the guise of a friend). Hence it is enough if we give up the posture of being unfavourable to him. In the other matters \[worldly pursuits\] being favourable too will destroy us. If one were to get involved with worldly matters, it will lead to a situation as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 2-63](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-63/) “*bhuddi nāśāt praṇacyati* ” (due to destruction of intelligence, he submerges in samsāram and is destroyed). Even if one feigns desire in emperumān, that would protect him as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-33 “*abhayam sarvabhūtēbhyō dadami ētat vratam mama*”(I protect from all creatures; this is my vow). Hasn’t kriṣṇa mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-31](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-31/) “*kauntēya pratijānīhi na mē bhakti: praṇaśyati*” (Oh arjuna! Take a pledge yourself that my devotee will not be destroyed)! Hasn’t he said “arjuna! For this meaning, you take the pledge”! Should one, who desires to get the fame of leading a great life, attain one such as rudra who gives us up at the hands of his enemies? Should he not attain one who took the arrows directed at his follower, on his own chest!

We shall take up the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 36. Siriyar perumai

*siṟiyār perumai siṟidin kaṇ eydum aṟiyārum tām aṟiyār āvār aṟiyāmai maṇ koṇḍu maṇ uṇḍu maṇ umiḻnda māyan enṟu eṇ koṇḍu en nenjē iru*

**Пословный перевод:**

siṟiyār – (without any greatness in them, by nature) lowly samsāris (dwellers of this materialistic realm; perumai – greatness (coming out of ego thinking that there is none their equal ); siṟidin kaṇ eydum – will take them towards lowliness only (repeatedly); aṟiyārum – people who cannot discriminate between good and bad (though they think that they are omniscient); tām aṟiyār āvār – they will become more and more ignorant; en nenjē – Oh my heart!; aṟiyāmai – unknown to others; maṇ koṇḍu – obtaining earth (from mahābali); maṇ uṇḍu – (during deluge) keeping that earth inside his divine stomach; maṇ umiḻnda – (later) spitting out that earth; māyan enṟu – emperumān who is an amazing entity; eṇ koṇḍu – thinking constantly; iru – be without fear (that for us who have attained him, there is no shortcoming)

**Перевод:**

**siṟiyār perumai siṟidin kaṇ eydum – for lowly people, even if they think that they are great persons, that thought too will end up as lowly only.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “let the people of this world do whatever they want. You think about the wonderful activities of emperumān and be happy”. Let the others do anything. You think about this entity, have faith in emperumān and do what is needed to be done, tells āḻvār. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aṟiyārum tām aṟiyār āvār** – while they remain ignorant, even if they think that they know everything, it will take them only to their earlier state of ignorance.

**aṟiyārum tām aṟiyār āvār** – we do not have to establish that they are ignorant. They would themselves, with this act, establish that.

**aṟiyāmai maṇ koṇḍu maṇ uṇḍu maṇ umiḻnda māyan enṟu** – Let the ignorant people go their ways. We shall go behind the people with knowledge \[about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\], says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/). We shall go in the path mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 7-4-4 “*pērāḷan pēr ōdum periyōrai oru kālum pirigilēn* ” (I will never separate from the great people who keep reciting the divine names of emperumān). In other words, with the grace of emperumān, we shall know about him and only with his grace, attain him.

**aṟiyāmai** – The supreme being, emperumān, came in the form of a dwarf, without anyone knowing about it, to beg for alms.

**maṇ koṇḍu** – measuring the earth

**man uṇḍu** – keeping the earth in his divine earth during the time of deluge and protecting it.

**man umiḻndu** – later on, spitting that earth. These activities are examples for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) giving himself to his devotees and protecting us from the deluge called samsāram (materialistic realm). Hasn’t tiruvāimoḻi celebrated these two events together in [1-10-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-5-kandaye-nenje/) “*uṇḍānai ulagēḻum ōr mūvadi koṇḍānai*” (one who swallowed the worlds (during deluge) and who measured them with three steps)!

**māyan** – one cannot see the boundary for not only these activities, but also for his activities in protecting his followers. He has such amazing power!

**eṇ koṇḍu** – thinking of him continuously. Alternatively, focussing only on attaining him.

**en nenjē iru** – Just as arjuna was, after hearing from kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) as mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*mā suca:*” (be without any worry), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) tells his heart not to feel sad. Hasn’t it been divinely mentioned in tiruccanda viruttam 115 “*ettināḷ idarkkadal kidatti ēḻai nenjamē*” (Oh my poor heart! Why do are you lie submerged in the ocean of sorrow?)! We have to worry only if we have to protect ourselves with our power or if we are not aware of emperumān’s power. Since we are not like that, we do not have to worry.

We shall move on to the 37<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 37. Irundan kamalattu

*irundaṇ kamalattu irumalarin uḷḷē tirundum tisaimugainait tandāy porundiya nin pādangaḷ ēttip paṇiyāvēl pal piṟappum ēdangaḷ ellām emakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

īru – large; taṇ – cool; kamalattu – the navel which is like lotus flower; iru malarin uḷḷē – inside the great flower; tirundu – capable; nintisaimuganai – nānmugan (brahmā); tandāy – Oh emperumān, the benefactor, gave us!; porundiya – fitting with all; nin – your; pādangaḷ – divine feet; ētti – praising handsomely; paṇiyā ēl – if not utilised in vorśipping with bowed head; emakku – for us; pal piṟappum ellām – the various births that we take; ēdangaḷ – totally useless

**Перевод:**

**irundaṇ kamalattin iru malarin uḷḷē – inside the large flower shooting from  very cool lotus-like navel of emperumān. irumalar – large flower. This implies that the dwelling place for brahmā is expansive. If the word irumai is taken to mean greatness, the meaning will change to: inside the expansive, cool and unique flower. If the text itself is taken as tirumalar the world tiru will refer to the radiance of the flower.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that all those births in which we do not attain emperumān are wasted. If emperumān who is to be attained, is not attained, then it is wasted. When emperumān asks “if I am not attained, will the births be wasted?”, āḻvār responds saying “it is my opinion”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tirundu tisaimuganait tandāy** – emperumān gave brahmā the capability so that while creating the worlds, brahmā need not come every time to seek guidance. He gave brahmā both jñānam (knowledge) and vēdam (spiritual texts) so that there would not be a need to ask emperumān at every step \[of creating the worlds\].  Hasn’t śvētāsvatara upaniśat 6-18 mentioned “*yō brahmāṇam vidadāti pūrvam yō vai vēdāmśca prahiṇōti tasmai . . . . .  mumukśūr vai śaraṇamaham prapadyē*” (I, desiring  mōkśam, surrender to him  . . . . who created brahmā, who gave him vēdam)! Thus it mentions that the one who created brahmā is the apt entity to be surrendered to. **tandāy** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says *tandāy* as if emperumān did a favour to āḻvār by creating brahmā. Probably he says so since this creation helped him to attain emperumān.

**porundiya nin pādangaḷ ēttip paṇiyāvēl** – the others will not fit with each other \[*fit* here refers to being amiable\]. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the only one who fits with all. The entire world fits him. Being humble towards him is the only requirement. If one does not surrender to his divine feet praising him, it would lead to many types of births.

**nin pādangaḷ ēttip paṇiyāvēl** – Everything would become wasted if we do not fulfil your expectations and do not become your servitors. Just as sumitrā told lakśmaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 40-5 “*sruśtatvam vanavāsāya*” (you were sired by me only to follow śrī rāma to the forest), all the days would be good days if we continue to serve emperumān.  The purpose of taking a birth will be served only if we attain emperumān.

**palpiṟappum** – all the births that we take due to our *karma* (deeds). As mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-9-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thiruvaimozhi-3-9-10-ninru-ninru/) “*senṟu senṟāgilum kaṇḍu*” (even if many births pass by, emperumān hopes that the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) will realise in one birth and approach him), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) creates many births for this jīvātmā so that some birth, out of the innumerable births, will bear fruit. The reason for using the plural number in **ēdangaḷ** is because of the varied types of difficulties undergone by the jīvātmā in each birth. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that all those difficulties are there only for him.

**paṇiyāvēl** – if I do not say that I, desiring liberation, surrender as mentioned in śvētāsvatara upaniśat 6-18 “*mumukśurvai aham prapadyē*”

**emakku ēdangaḷ** – those births will become wasted. The purpose of life is only to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). If it is anything other than this, it is useless, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/). Hasn’t gāruda pūrvabhāgam 222-22 said “*yanmuhūrtam kśaṇam vāpi vāsudēvō na cintayatē sā hāni: tanmahaccidram sā bhrāntis sā ca vikriyā*” (if one doesn’t think of vāsudēvan (emperumān) in any moment in a muhūrtam (approximately one and a half hours), that will be destruction; that will be disaster; that will be bewilderment; that will be evil disposition).

We shall move on to the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 38. Emekkenru irunidiyam

*emakkenṟu irunidiyam ēmāndirādē tamakkenṟum sārvam aṟindu namakkenṟum mādavanē ennum manam padaittu maṟṟavan pēr ōduvadē nāvināl ōttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

irunidiyam – great wealth; emakku enṟu – thinking that it is for oneself; ēmāndu irādē – instead of feeling arrogant; tamakku enṟum sārvam aṟindu – knowing the entity which will always protect us; namakku enṟum mādavanē ennum manam padaittu – having the heart with conviction that “it is only emperumān who is everything for us at all times”; maṟṟu – on top of that; avan pēr – his divine names; ōduvadē – to recite with tune; nāvināl ōttu – apt for our tongue to recite like vēdam

**Перевод:**

**emakkenṟu irunidiyam ēmāndirādē – one who thinks that wealth is for him instead of knowing about those who do not think of wealth as important**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked as to what we should do to fulfil the purpose of taking this birth, āḻvār says that only reciting the divine names of the consort of tirumagaḷ (mahālakśmi) instead of claiming ownership of others’ possessions will be the purpose of this birth and the activity that we should carry out. He says that instead of treating wealth as puruśārtam (goal), enjoying emperumān is the only apt activity for everyone. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**irunidiyam emakku enṟu ēmāndirādē** – Considering others’ huge wealth as one’s own. Later, praising [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and getting emboldened to keep one’s legs on the head of yama (lord of righteousness). Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 9 “*ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān na bibhēti kutaścana*” (one who knows the happiness of emperumān will not be afraid of anything), one who is without any fear. Is it apt to consider emperumān’s wealth as one’s own? When emperumān who is the epitome of happiness is there, would one desire a little bit of gold? The mere thought of wealth will bring in sadness. But in the case of one who thinks of emperumān, he becomes fearless as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 “*atasō bhayangatō bhavati*” (he becomes fearless).

**tamakku enṟum sārvam aṟindu** – think that it is only emperumān who is the refuge. Thinking that the only permanent refuge for us is emperumān. emperumān thinks of the jīvātmā as being always for him, even when the jīvātmā does not know what is apt for him.

**namakku enṟum mādavanē ennum manam padaittu** – having the heart considering that it is only tirumāl (consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)) who is our refuge. Having the heart, considering that it only tirumāḷ who is apt to be enjoyed. Hasn’t it been mercifully mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 12-1 “*mādavan enbadōr anbu tannai uṟṟirundēn*” (I had strong desire for mādavan)!

**mādavan** – it is said that it is only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is with pirātti ([mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)), who is apt to be attained.

**manam padaittu** – It has been prayed for in jitantē ślōkam 1-11 “*vijñānam yadidam prāptam yaditam stānamārjitam, janmāntarēpi mē dēva mābhūt tasya parikśaya:*” (Oh emperumān! There should be no shortcoming in any birth to the knowledge and the deep belief that I have that you are the means for attaining you and you are the fruit of my attaining you). There is no need for emperumān to create them (jīvātmās) now. āren’t they existing already!

**maṟṟu avan pēr ōduvadē nāvināl ōttu** – Only reciting his divine names is the activity with the tongue. It is the activity for the tongue to keep reciting about the twin entities of emperumān and pirātti. It is said that this is the mantram (vēdic hymn) to be pronounced. In harivamśam 183-8, rudra says “*harirēkas sadādyēya:*” (hari ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) is the only one who is apt to be meditated upon always). In [tirunedundāṇḍagam 4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-4/), [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says “*mandirattai mandirattāl maṟavādu enṟum vāḻudiyēl vāḻalām mada nenjamē*” (Oh humble heart! If we constantly remember mantram (emperumān) with the mantram (hymn) without forgetting, we can live happily).

We shall take up the 39<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 39. Ottin porul

*ōttin poruḷ modivum ittanaiyē uttaman pēr ēttum tiṟam aṟīmin ēḻigāḷ ōttadnai valleerēl nanṟu adanai māttīrēl mādavan pēr solluvadē ōttin surukku*

**Пословный перевод:**

uttaman pēr – with the divine names of emperumān, who is the most supreme among all entities; ēttum tiṟam – the activity of praising; ittanaiyē – just this much; ōttin poruḷ mudivum – is the essence of meaning of vēdams; ōttu adanai valleerēl – if you are capable of knowing the meaning of vēdam; aṟimin – do know; ēḻaigāḷ – Oh people, impoverished by ignorance!; nanṟu adanai māttirēl – if you do not have the capability to know that meaning; mādavan pēr solluvadē – reciting the divine names of śrīman nārāyaṇan is; ōttin surukku – the gist of vēdam; aṟimin – know, at least, this

**Перевод:**

**ōttin poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē – āḻvār says “what I am going to say now is the essence of all inner meanings of vēdam”. Hasn’t emperumān himself declared in śrī bhagavat gītā 15-15 “vēdaiśca sarvai: ahamēva vēdya:” (through all vēdams, only I am known)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*How to praise through the mouth \[as mentioned in the previous pāsuram\]? Through reciting tirumantram (aśtākśaram) which is the gist of all vēdams (sacred texts), without diminishing its dignity as mentioned in upaniśats, and attaining \[emperumān\]. This is ōK for those who are able to attain him. āḻvār says that for those who cannot attain him this way, it is enough if they can recite the divine names of tirumāl  (consort of śrī mahālakśmi) which would be gist of vēdāntams (upaniśats). When asked “vēdātams say that indra (lord of celestial entities) et al are fit to be attained  but you say that tirumāḷ is fit to be attained” āḻvār responds saying  “you people are talking ignorantly about the superficial meaning of vēdāntams, without understanding the inner meaning. Please know the inner meaning of those statements and understand that in those places where vēdāntam says that indra et al are fit to be attained, it says that it is only tirumāl, who has those entities as his body and is their inner residing soul who is fit to be attained” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**poruḷ mudivum** – īsn’t this the limit of the benefit that we would attain!

**uttaman pēr ēttum tiṟam** – knowing the way of reciting the divine names of the supreme being who can take this confused [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), who had  thought of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) when he was in good physical and mental state, from this world to śrīvaikuṇṭam. emperumān is such a supreme being that he can protect in a way as mentioned in varāha carama ślōkam “*stitē manasi susvastē śarīrē sati yō nara: dātu sāmyē stitē smartā viśvarūpanca māmajam*” (whoever thinks of me, who has the universe as my body and who is without any birth, in a state of good health and balanced state of mind, with all the three *dātus* (the elements of kapam, pittam and vātam which are related to breathing, digestion and bone joints) in balance) “*tatastam mriyamāṇantu kāśta pāśāṇa sannibham aham smarāmi madbhaktam nayāmi paramām gatim*” (in later years, when he is like a log of wood or stone, in his last stages of life, I think of him, my devotee, and take him to a glorious state). Thus emperumān is capable of protecting even when the jīvātmā is not in a position to think of him. Reciting the divine names of such an emperumān alone is proper.

**uttaman pēr ēttum tiṟam** – reciting the divine names of the entity who has everything else as his attributes. **uttaman**  – one who provides all protections. mukundamālā 34 says “*uttartumarhasi harē puruśōttamōsi* ” (Oh hari! You, the supreme being, are fit to protect)! kriṣṇa says in śrī bhagavat gītā 15-19 “*yō māmēvamasammūdō jānāti puruśōttamam sa sarvavidbhajati mām sarvabhāvēna bhārata*” (one who knows me, the supreme being, without any bewilderment, knows all the ways by which he can attain me and is one who worships me in all aspects ). One should know that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has everything as his attribute.

**pēr** – the word which refers to the quality mentioned earlier. In other words, tirumantram (aśtākśaram). This tirumantram also contains all the words in it, just like him. hārītasmruti 3-45 says “*sarvam aśtākśarāntasttam*” (everything present in the four vēdams is present in the eight lettered tirumantram). āren’t there sayings such as “*ōmitīdam sarvam*” (everything is praṇavam), “*akārō vai sarvā vāk*” (for all words, the letter *akāram* ‘a’ is foremost)!

**aṟimin ēḻaigāḷ** – Oh fickle minded people who think that there is something else to be known! Please know this.

**ēḻaigāḷ** – How sad it is that I have to tell you all that there is nothing else to be known other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! How sad that when the apt entity emperumān is there you are all involved with worldly matters! It implies that these are ignorant people who take in only the words and not the inner meanings.

**ōttadanai valleerēl** – if you are capable of carrying out everything mentioned in the vēdams without any shortcomings

**aṟimin** – then know. Practising vēdams with their ancillaries. If you care capable of understanding the inner meaning by listening about vēdāntam, then know you ignorant people, that reciting the divine name of the supreme being is the essence of all vēdams. Keep this in your mind.

**nanṟadanai māttīrēl** – if you are not capable of carrying out what has been said, viz., carrying out in an orderly way samskārams (rituals) such as upanayanam (wearing yajñōpavīdam, the sacred thread), practising vēdams with ancillaries, knowing the meanings of vēdams, carrying out activities as mentioned in vēdams without any shortcoming and finally seeing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**mādavan pēr solluvadē ōttin surukku** – reciting the divine names of emperumān, the consort of śri mahālakśmi, is the essence of all that has been said in vēdams.

**mādavan** – is it not [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who identifies emperumān himself! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says “Consider that the sayings of one who attained knowledge through [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s mercy \[āḻvār himself\] are the true meanings. Believe me who has seen emperumān with his mercy” Since it has been mentioned that reciting emperumān’s divine names is the essence, it should be considered that in the context of not carrying out what has been mentioned in vēdams (**nanṟadanai māttirēl**), everything has been thought of such as practising vēdam etc.

We shall move on to the 40<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 40. Surukkaga vangi

*surukkāga vāngi sulāvi ninṟu aiyār nerukkāmun nīr ninaimin kaṇḍīr tiruppolinda āgattān pādam aṟindum aṟiyāda bōgattāl illai poruḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

aiyār – phlegm (a mucous material); surukkāga vāngi – contracting the body; sulāvi ninṟu – surrounding the body fully \[spreading to all parts of the body\]; nerukkā mun – before it starts troubling; tirup polinda āgattān pādam – the divine feet of emperumān who is decorated with śrī mahālakśmi on his chest; nīr ninaimin kaṇḍīr – please think of \[his divine feet\]; aṟindum – even if you are conversant with matters relating to emperumān; aṟiyāda – those which will make you ignorant; bōgattāl – enjoying worldly pursuits starting with śabdam (the five sensory perceptions); poruḷ illai – there is no purpose

**Перевод:**

**surukkāga vāngi sulāvi ninṟu aiyār nerukkā mun nīr ninaimin kaṇḍīr – phlegm, which is present everywhere in the body, will start giving trouble during the last days. At that time, the sensory perceptions of the cētana (sentient entity) will not be under his control and will not allow him the desire to think of emperumān. Thus, before such a situation arises, think of emperumān immediately.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that the essence of all vēdams is reciting emeprumān’s divine names. Instead of saying something for namesake, he tells people in this pāsuram, to think of the meaning of those divine names, emperumān, soon. Any entity other than him does not have the characteristic of an entity. Hence, only he is apt to be attained. He says that instead of attaining at one’s convenience, attain him immediately. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**surukkāga vāngi** – phlegm will start contracting the body, made of five elements, to look like a purse with a double string \[so that it can be contracted fully at the neck\].

**sulāvi ninṟu** – it will spread all over the body and kill the person

**aiyār nerukkā mun** – before phlegm starts troubling

**nīr ninaimin** – please keep this in your mind. It is not enough if you say that you will do it. *You have to do it.* Has it not been mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-8-nalum-ninradu/) “*māḷumōr idattilum vaṇakkodu māḷvadu valamē*” (even at the time of leaving this body (at the time of death) it is better to bow down \[to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] and leave)! For those who are trying to attain emperumān through *bhakti yōgam* (the path of devotion) it is compulsory to think of emperumān at the time of death. For those who are trying to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) through emperumān himself, there is no such compulsion (as mentioned in varāha caramaślōkam). If one were to think of *dvaya mantram* \[emperumān\] in the last stage, irrespective of these two states, it is welcome.

But should one have to think \[of emperumān\], to the point of hurting oneself, at that stage?

**tiruppolinda āgattān**  – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is asking whether emperumān’s physical beauty is such that it can be forgotten. One need not fear that one has not attempted to attain emperumān in a long time and feel guilty. This is because as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116-44 “*na kaścinnāprādyati*” (there is none who has not committed a mistake), pirātti is always there with emperumān to plead on our behalf. Hence think of the divine feet of emperumān who is made to look radiant by pirātti.

**aṟindum aṟiyāda bōgattāl illai poruḷ** – despite being knowledgeable about emperumān there is no purpose in going after worldly pursuits just like ignorant people do. There is no purpose in going after worldly pursuits which will make even a knowledgeable person to appear as ignorant. Even though sugrīva maharājar had total devotion towards śrī rāma, since he was immersed in worldly pursuits for some time, he had to be reminded \[about his responsibility\] like others. *aṟindum aṟiyāda bōgam* can also refer to wealth which does not allow one to give up worldly enjoyments despite knowing that they are harmful. Another interpretation – even if one enjoys worldly pursuits to a large extent, he cannot clearly understand what he had enjoyed and thus even such a person is made to look ignorant. Thus there is no benefit out of enjoying worldly pursuits. Has it not been mercifully mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-5-87 “*sandruśyatē vāpyavagamyatē vā tatjñāṇamajñāna matōnya dukkam*” (except seeing and knowing about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), knowledge about all other things is ignorance)! The knowledge which does not lead to attaining emperumān is not knowledge at all. It will be termed only as ignorance. If a person knows about everything other than emperumān, he will be called as ignorant only. Even if he does not know about anything other than emperumān, he will be referred to us a knowledgeable person only. In the pāsuram, while referring to emperumān, it says *nīr ninaimin* (you think) while referring to worldly pursuits it says *aṟindum*, meaning one should understand fully about those worldly things. If one were to ask whether it is comfort that one gets through other entities, in reality since they prevent one from attaining the real benefit that one should go after, they create only discomfort. Other entities, other than creating no purpose, also become hurdles to attaining the real benefit. This is brought out well in this pāsuram.

We shall take up the 41<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 41. Porulal amarulagam

*poruḷāl amarulagam pukkiyal āgādu aruḷāl aṟam aruḷum anṟē aruḷālē māmaṟaiyōrkkīnda maṇivaṇṇan pādamē nī maṟavēl nenjē ninai*

**Пословный перевод:**

amarulagam – the world of dēvas \[heaven\]; poruḷāl – with wealth; pukku iyal āgādu – is not proper to enter; aruḷāl – with mercy; aṟam – through the fruits of puṇya (good deeds); aruḷum anṟē – will grant with his mercy; aruḷālē – through his mercy; mā maṟaiyuōrkku – for the parama vaidikas (those who stand by vēdas, the sacred texts); īnda – one who offered himself; maṇivaṇṇan pādamē – the divine feet of emperumān who has blue complexion; nenjē – Oh my heart!; nī maṟavēl – do not forget; ninai – keep meditating

**Перевод:**

**poruḷāl amar ulagam pukkiyal āgādu – there are two interpretations: (1) with wealth as puruśārtam (end goal), not entering  the apt place for one’s svarūpam (basic nature), mōkśalōkam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); (2) not considering places such as brahmalōkam etc as a goal and not attaining them. Just as śrī guṇaratna kōśam 21 says “surapuri yadgaccatō durgati:” (you made those who wanted to attain you to think of dēvalōkam too as hell), reaching dēvalōkam is considered as lowly. Just as brāhmaṇas would not go anywhere near places inhabited by those born in lowly clans, one should not even go near svargam (heaven).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, did not āḻvār say “think of the divine feet of emperumān who is decorated with śrī mahālakśmi on his chest?”. He takes that instruction for himself and tells his divine mind not to forget emperumān, who has a beautiful form which removes all the ennui and confers happiness, in the first place. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aruḷāl aṟam aruḷum anṟē** – why so? Why is it that it is considered as an inappropriate place? The [cētana (sentient entity)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) likes only materialistic possessions. If he does not get them, he becomes sorrowful. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has immeasurable mercy, unable to tolerate his sorrow, may give him what he desires, saying “why should the cētana lose out on wealth? Let him have it”. cētana has sensory perceptions which are active. Hence when he desires wealth, he may even say “do what is righteous for you”. This is similar to a mother yielding to a child which desires to eat sand. He may even say, just as he had told a brāhmaṇa by name gōvindasvāmy, mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 5-8-5 “*bōgam nī eydip pinnum nammidaikkē pōduvāy*” (stay in the world for some time, enjoy all the worldly pleasures and then reach my śrīvaikuṇṭam). *aṟam* refers to the benefit obtained through righteousness. If *svargam* is obtained, it will be inimical to the svarūpam (basic nature) of cētana. Even for obtaining lowly benefits, one needs the mercy of emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says, that being the case, why not attain emperumān himself with the help of that mercy.

**aruḷālē māmaṟaiyōrkkīnda maṇivaṇṇan pādamē** – the divine feet of the one who gives himself through his mercy. He gave mōkśam to great sages such as sukar (son of vēda vyāsar), vāmadēvar et al who detested the materialistic life and prayed to him to grant his divine feet. Just as it is said “*sukhō mukta: vāmadēvō mukta:*” (sukar attained mōkśam, vāmadēvar attained mōkśam), he granted them śrīvaikuṇṭam through his mercy. If the text is taken as *māmaṟaiyōṟku* (singular number) it will refer to sage mārkaṇḍēya who was granted nityatvam (permanency). If the text is taken as *māmaṟaiyōrkku* (plural number) it can be taken to refer to the sages in dandakāraṇyam, to whom he gave his beautiful form itself to enjoy. Hasn’t it been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 1-13 “*rūpasamhananam lakśmīm saukumāryam suvēśatām dadruśu: vismitākārā: rāmasya vanavāsina:*” (the sages, who dwell in forests, looked with amazement at śrī rāma’s beautiful form, gentleness and the aptly fitting guise of sage). *rūpasamhananam* – natural beauty; *lakśmīm* – radiance over the beauty; *saukumāryam* – the gentle form of his divine body, which, even by looking at it, will become red \[it is so gentle and soft\]; *suvēśatām* – such was his guise as a sage that even the dress made of bark and the matted hair looked like ornaments on him; *vanavāsina: dadruśu:* sages who dwell in forests saw him. Who beheld the sight which is normally held by whom? rig aśtakam 1-2-7 says “*sadā paśyanti sūraya:*” (nityasūris always behold the sight of viṣṇu). Thus, the sight, normally seen by nityasūris in śrīvaikuṇṭam, was seen in the forest by the sages!

**maṇivaṇṇan pādamē** – vasn’t it his beautiful form that he gave the sages! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) tells his heart to think constantly, without forgetting, of the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has bluish complexion which will remove the fatigue of those who see him. Oh heart which manifests knowledge, do not forget this matter which has been exclusively created for you! Oh heart, you need not be lectured upon! Do not consider these words as having been uttered in an assembly for the sake of utterance! Forgetting emperumān is equivalent to keeping desire in worldly matters. āḻvār tells his heart not to forget, so that his heart will not contemplate on worldly matters.

We shall take up the 42nd pāsuram next.

## 42. Ninaippan tirumalai

*ninaippan tirumālai nīṇḍa tōḷ kāṇa ninaippār piṟapponṟum nērār manaippāl piṟandār pirāndu eydum pērinbam ellām tuṟandār toḻudārat tōḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālai – the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; nīṇḍa tōḷ kāṇa ninaippan – to enjoy (his) divine shoulders, I think of him; ninaippār – those who think (like this); piṟappu onṟum – any type of birth \[in any of the classes of dēvas, humans, animals and plants\]; nērār – will not attain; at tōḷ – those divine shoulders; toḻuvār – those who worship; manaippāl pirāndār piṟandu eydum inbam ellām – the small \[worldly\] pleasures which those who attain since they are born in samsāram (materialistic realm); tuṟandār – they detest \[them\]

**Перевод:**

**ninaippan tirumalai – isn’t the consort of tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi) apt to be meditated upon! It is similar to thinking of one’s mother and father. āḻvār thinks of emperumān just like how a person who has gone to another country, thinks of his parents. He says that he thinks of the way śrī rāma and sītāppirātti were together seen in the āśram (hermitage) of sage śrī atri bhagavān. He says that it is similar to the way he saw perumāḷ and pirātti in the corridor of mrigaṇḍu maharishi in tirukkōvalūr. Just as lakśmaṇa said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 8-31 “bhrātā bhartā ca banduśca pitā ca mama rāghava” (śrī rāma is my elder brother, lord, relative and father), the purpose of thinking of emperumān is not for begging him to obtain any benefit.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, getting dejected with the worldly pursuits, says that only those who think of emperumān will not enter samsāram again. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

If not, then for what?

**nīṇḍa tōḷ kāṇa** – Instead of seeing the shoulders which have wizened due to separation from pirātti, I want to see his divine shoulders which have become larger due to his being together with pirātti. To see the shoulders which appear sweet because of his being together with her. It is not obtaining benefits such as attaining heaven etc.

What should be done with the births which present a hurdle to think of him?

**ninaippār piṟappu enṟum nērār** – Those who think of emperumān this way will not attain any birth, ever. If the text is taken to be *piṟappu onṟum nērār*, the entity which goes by the name birth will not come anywhere near such a person.

**manaippāl** – in the  house

**piṟandār piṟandu eydum** – those who are born in various houses \[various births\]. The benefit that they attain, after being born like this . . . .

**pērinbam ellām** – all the pleasures. āḻvār terms the worldly pursuits as *pērinbam* (great pleasures).

The heaviness of these pleasures which prevents one from approaching emperumān all these days ..

**tuṟandār toḻudār at tōḷ** – āren’t they the ones who had renounced such petty pleasures! They are the ones who worship the divine shoulders of emperumān.  Alternative interpretation – who can renounce these petty pleasures and worship emperumān unless emperumān himself shows his shoulders to the one who thinks of him?

We shall take up the 43<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 43. Tolirandettelum

*tōḷ iraṇḍu ettu ēḻum mūnṟum mudi anaittum tāḷiraṇḍum vīḻac caram turandān tāḷ iraṇḍum ār toḻuvār pādam avai toḻuvadu anṟē en sīr keḻu tōḷ seyyum siṟappu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḷ iraṇḍu ettu ēḻum mūnṟum – twenty shoulders; mudi anaittum – all the heads (which sprouted); tāḷ iraṇḍum – the two legs; vīḻa – to be destroyed; saram turandān – emperumān who shot arrows; tāḷ iraṇḍum – two divine feet; toḻuvār Ar – whoever worships (their); pādam avai – divine feet; toḻuvadu anṟē – isn’t vorśipping them; en sīrkeḻu tōḷ seyyum siṟappu – the help provided by my beneficial shoulders (to me)

**Перевод:**

**tōḷ iraṇḍu ettu ēḻum mūnṟumB – rāvaṇa’s twenty shoulders (2+8+7+3)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not possible for us to give up worldly pursuits; we cannot think of emperumān too; it would be better to hold on to the divine feet of his followers who are firmly engaged with him with the belief that he will remove the enemies of his followers. He says that he need not worship emperumān’s shoulders; it is apt to worship those who worship him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mudiyanaittum** – his heads which could not be kept count of, as they kept on sprouting even while they were being severed

**tāḷ iraṇḍum vīḻa** – making the two feet, which are the support for his shoulders and heads, to fall down

**saram turandān** – the purpose of saying individually the shoulders, heads and legs is to provide some pastime for śrī rāma’s arrows when they were shot. āḻvār mercifully gives the sequence in which the arrows played. This implies that even though rāvaṇa was also created by emperumān only, since he became an enemy to his followers, emperumān had to destroy him.

**saram turandān tāḷ iraṇḍum** – the two divine feet of *cakravarty tirumagan* (son of daśarata cakravarty). āren’t they the target to be attained by us! (implying that only the one who removes our enemy is to be attained).

Such two divine feet

**Ar toḻuvār** – in whatever species we are born, with whatever knowledge that we have, vorśipping emperumān’s divine feet is what is required. The greatness or lowliness for a person comes out of vorśipping or not vorśipping his divine feet. It is always beneficial for one, whoever he may be, to worship emperumān’s divine feet. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully stated this in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thiruvaimozhi-3-7-1-payilum-sudaroli/) “*pāṟkadal sērnda paramanaip payilum tiruvudaiyār yavarēlum avar kaṇḍīr*” (whoever worships emperumān who is reclining in tiruppāṟkdal (milky ocean), in whichever clan he is born or whatever qualities he has, he is a great person).

**Ar** – whoever he may be. That person can be a rākśasa such as vibhīśaṇa, or a monkey such as sugrīva or hanuman, or a regal personality such as arjuna or even a devil such as ghaṇṭākarṇan.

**pādam avai toḻuvadu anṟē** – the person who worships emperumān can be anybody. Even with such a person, his divine feet are apt to be attained. Attaining emperumān is similar to holding on to someone’s hands for getting a work carried out. Attaining him through his follower is similar to holding on to his divine feet for the same work. In the latter case, it will be impossible to refuse help.

**pādam avai toḻuvadanṟē** – what is being sought is not equality with followers, but attaining their feet \[being a servitor to emperumān’s followers\].

**en sīrkeḻu tōḷ seyyum siṟappu** – the assistance which my shoulders can provide to me while desiring a benefit. The eminence which my beautiful shoulders can provide for me.

**sīr keḻu tōḷ** – āḻvār gives the difference between our shoulders and those of emperumān’s follower (the shoulders of emperumān’s follower worship the divine feet of emperumān while my shoulders worship the divine feet of the follower, which is more apt). Hence it is enough if one worships such shoulders. The purpose of one’s body is to attain puruśārtam (goal). āḻvār is terming his shoulders as *sīr keḻu tōḷ* since they have provided the benefit of attaining the followers of emperumān.

We shall take up the 44<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 44. Sirandarkku

*siṟandārkkeḻu tuṇaiyām sengaṇmāl nāmam maṟandārai mānidamā vaiyēn – aṟandāngum mādavanē ennum manam padaittu maṟṟavan pēr ōduvadē nāvināl uḷḷu*

**Пословный перевод:**

siṟandārkku – the great śrīvaiṣṇavas (followers of emperumān); sem kaṇ māl – emperumān’s, who is having reddish eyes; nāmam – divine names; maṟandārai – those who forget; mānidam A – \[considered as having been\] born in the species of humans; vaiyēn – I will not keep in my heart; aṟam tāngum mādavanē ennum manam padaittu – having the heart which says “Oh mādava who establishes rightesouness!”; maṟṟu – and; avan pēr – his divine names; nāvināl ōduvadē – reciting with tongue; uḷḷu – keep meditating (that it is apt for us)

**Перевод:**

**siṟandārkku – those who have attained emperumān, considering his divine feet as most appropriate. Just as kriṣṇa has said in śrī bhagavat gītā 7-18 “jñānī tu ātmaiva mē matam” (it is my opinion that the knowledgeable person who desires only me, is my ātmā), those who have been referred to as jñāni (knowledgeable) by emperumān himself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that those who do not attain emperumān like this do not have any knowledge. Just as emperumān’s followers are apt for us, those who are not his followers are most inapt and are to be given up! Only those who are related to emperumān are to be respected while others are not to be respected. āḻvār says that he does not have any connection with such people. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eḻu tuṇaiyām** – he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] will be the companion (guide) on the way for such people so that they do not forget him. For those who attain his divine feet, he will remove their involvement in worldly pursuits, create involvement for them in himself, and at the time of their death, he will precede the *ādivāhikars* (those who guide the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) in its journey towards paramapadam, after it crosses the world of vidyut (lightning) ) towards śrīvaikuṇṭam). He will hand-hold the ātmā and take it to paramapadam just as he had mentioned in varāha caramaślōkam “*nayāMi paraām gatim*” (I take them to exalted status).

**sengaṇmāl** – due to his motherly affection, which is the reason for being a companion, he has reddish eyes. This implies how he will act towards those who do not forget him and the reason why we should not forget him.

**nāmam maṟandārai** – those who forget his divine names

**mānidamā vaiyēn** – will not consider them to be human beings. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he will not think lowly of those who do not know his divine names. I may even consider them to be omniscient since they have the qualification to know him henceforth. But the one who has forgotten him, after being born as a human being, fit to follow sacred texts and after knowing his sweetness, is not a human at all. kriṣṇa called arjuna, who had forgotten the gītā that he had discoursed to him, in mahābhāratam aśvamēdikha parvam 17-19 as “*durmēdāhyasi pāṇḍava*” (Oh ignorant pāṇḍava!).

**mānidamā vaiyēn** – Can one,  who [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) considers in his divine mind as “not a human”, be a human! If one looks at a pebble and thinks it is a gem, it will not become a gem, will it! Whatever an expert in gems calls a gem, will be a gem. In the same way, only the person, who āḻvār considers in his divine heart as human, will be a human. Following his footsteps, [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) has mercifully mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 11-7-9 “*ānvidaiyēḻ anṟadattārkku āḷānār allādār mānidavar allar enṟu en manattē vaittēnē*” (I firmly kept in my mind that the one who does not attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who killed the seven raging bulls, is not a human at all). Hasn’t it been said in narasimha purāṇam 16-13 “*jñānēna hīna: paśubhissamāna:*” (one who does not have knowledge is equivalent to an animal!). Knowledge is that which is mentioned by [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) in [mudal tiruvandādi 67](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-67/) “*oṇḍāmaraiyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu*” (knowledge will attain only the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)).

**mānidamā vaiyēn** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that it can be engraved on stone or copper plate that those who are not like that are not  humans (implying that this opinion is very firm). In other words, those who are given up by āḻvār, who in turn are those who have been given up emperumān himself, have no further refuge. Hence, those who have been given up by āḻvār will not be considered by anyone as humans.

**aṟam tāngum mādavanē ennum manam padaittu** – Having the intelligence that it is only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, with his mercy, who protects everyone. Alternatively, having the knowledge that emperumān, the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, wears the righteousness of protecting the world. ḍoesn’t emperumān protect the world, being kindled by pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) and to make her happy! (this is the reason why [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is referring to emperumān here as *mādavan*,  the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)).

**mādavanē ennum manam padaittu** – having the knowledge that it is only the consort of śrī mahālakśmi is like this. We can also construe this as āḻvār calling his heart and telling it “Oh heart! Try to recite the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who protects the world, with your tongue. Meditate on him”.

The thoughts in the heart went towards

**aṟam tāngum mādavanē** – the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who sustains righteousness.

**nāvināl maṟṟu avan pēr ōduvadē** – reciting the divine name which refers to the union of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with pirātti, viz. [dvayam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) (the couplet which is mentioned at the time of surrendering to emperumān in the presence of pirātti).

**nāvināl** – the purpose of having the tongue

**uḷḷu** – recite (meditate).

We shall move on to the 45<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 46. Payinradu arangam

*payinṟadu arangam tirukkōtti pannāḷ payinṟaduvum vēngadamē pannāḷ payinṟadu aṇi tigaḻum sōlai aṇi nīrmalaiyē maṇi tigaḻum vaṇ tadakkai māl*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇi tigaḻum – shining like a blue gem; vaṇ tadakkai – being magnanimous, having rounded divine hands; māl – emperumān; payinṟadu – residing permanently; arangam tirukkōtti – at tiruvarangam and at tirukkōttiyūr; pal nāḷ – for a very long time; payinṟaduvum – also residing permanently; vēngadamē – at tirumalai; pal nāḷ payinṟaduvum – living permanently for a very long time; aṇi tigaḻum sōlai – having beautiful gardens; aṇi – being a jewel-piece for the world; nīrmalai – at tirunīrmalai

**Перевод:**

**payinṟadu arangam tirukkōtti – unlike incarnations when emperumān had to go back to his paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) after fulfilling the purpose for which he had taken the incarnation, at tiruvarangam periya kōyil (the big temple at śrirangam) emperumān resides permanently, with happiness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned that since emperumān’s followers are attached to him, they gave up worldly pursuits. In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that emperumān, leaving aside the pleasures in śrīvaikuṇṭam, comes to samsāram and attaches himself to his followers. The pāsuram says that apart from his followers, emperumān has no profit or loss. If the follower tells emperumān that he has given up everything, as mentioned by śrī vibhīśaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-5 “parityaktā mayā lankā mitrāṇi ca danāni ca” (kingdom, friends and wealth have been given up by me), emperumān tells him, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 10-19 “apyaham jīvitam jahyām tvām vā sītē sa lakśmaṇām, na tu pratijñām samśrutya brāhmaṇēbhyō viśēśata:” (Oh sītā! I may give up my life, lakśmaṇa as well as you, but will never give up my vow, especially that which had been made to brāhmaṇas) and considers his follower as everything. It is this act of emperumān which makes the follower also to learn from him. In śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17-18 “sarvēśām hi sa darmātmā varṇānām kurutē dayām caturṇām vayas tānām tēna tē tamanuvratā” (that śri rāma, who is the darmasvarūpi (epitome of all rightesouness) showers mercy on the people in the four varṇams (classifications based on their profession and qualities) and in the four age-groups; hence, they are also very affectionate towards him). Once a sage by name saubari took fifty forms to marry fifty damsels. In the same way emperumān also takes different forms to enjoy his follower. If through emperumān’s mercy someone like saubari can take multiple forms, should one say anything about the number of forms that emperumān himself would take, of his own freewill and desire! Even for carrying this out, it is emperumān who had to struggle and not these people, says the āḻvār. He says that there is no limitation to the number of divine places (divyadēśams) where he has taken residence. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tirukkōtti** – it is the same at tirukkōśtiyūr too. When he is residing here, there is no need to exclude another place!

**pannāḷ payinṟaduvum vēngadamē** – residing for a long time at tirumalai. tirumalai was the divine place where he set his divine golden feet initially, with a view to residing at many divine places in [arcāvatāram (incarnations in divine deity form)](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html).

**pannāḷ payinṟadu aṇi tigaḻum sōlai aṇi nīrmalaiyē** – in the same way he has been residing for a long time at tirunīrmalai, which is having beautiful gardens.

Who resides like this?

**maṇi tigaḻum vaṇ tadakkai māl** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with a form which removes ennui like a blue gemstone, being magnanimous with his rounded divine hands. Since he gave himself to the people of this world for enjoying him, and since he is beautiful, he is referred to as being magnanimous. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in his [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-11-parpanaban/) says “*enakkē tannaittanda kaṟpagam*” (he is like the celestial wish-fulfilling tree, but gave himself to me) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-5-vittilangu/) “*vittilangu senjōdi*” (emperumān displays his eyes which are like reddish lotus, shining brightly).

**māl** – it could be construed as ‘*greatness*’ in which case it would refer to his being greater than everyone and everything else. Alternatively, it could be construed as ‘*being infatuated*’ in which case it would refer to his being infatuated with his followers.

We shall take up the 47<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 47. Malai ariyuruvan

*mālai ariyuruvan pādamalar aṇindu kālai toḻudeḻumin kaikōli jñālam aḷandu idandu uṇḍu umiḻnda aṇṇalai maṟṟallāl uḷam kidandavāṟṟāl uṇarndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

jñālam – the entire world; aḷandu – (playfully) measured; idandu – (as varāha) dug out; uṇḍu – (during deluge) kept inside the stomach; umiḻnda – later spat it out; aṇṇalai – emperumān, my supreme svāmy; maṟṟu aḷḷāl – apart from the protective activities mentioned earlier; uḷḷam kidanda āṟṟāl – in different ways residing inside the hearts of followers (melting their hearts); uṇarndu – meditating; ari uruvan – that emperumān who incarnated as narasinga mūrty; pāda malar – in the divine lotus feet; mālai aṇindu – decorating with garlands; kālai – in the early hours of the morning; kai kōli toḻudu – vorśipping with folded hands; eḻumin – uplift yourselves

**Перевод:**

**mālai ari uruvan pādamalar aṇindu – gathering beautiful garlands and offering them at the divine feet of emperumān who keeps the form that he takes for the sake of his followers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells the people that once he likes you, you also try and like him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ari uruvan** – divine feet of narasingan who is beautiful and gentle

**kālai toḻudu eḻumin kai kōli** – worship emperumān at the appropriate period of satvam (tranquil), which is also called as brahma muhūrtam \[4 am to 6 am\] and uplift yourselves. *kaikōli* would refer to the involvement with which he is attained. Alternatively it would refer to folding the hands and performing anjali (vorśipping with folded hands). emperumān has created various methods to attain, to worship and to decorate.

**jñālam aḷandu idandu uṇḍu umiḻnda aṇṇalai** – protecting in all the ways. emperumān who has such qualities and activities.

What are those activities?

**jñālam aḷandu** – measuring the entire earth. This implies that he keeps his divine feet on the heads of even those who do not fold their hands in worship.

**idandu** – incarnating as mahā varāha (great boar) to dig out the earth during deluge. This implies that he protects when there is danger.

**uṇḍu umiḻndu** – keeping in the stomach due to deluge and protecting, and later spitting so that it world can go on an outing. This implies that he is accustomed to protecting during times of deluge.

**aṇṇalai** – indicating to the world due to his aforesaid activities that he is the head of the world. The head who has qualities and activities enjoyed by [cētanas (sentient entities)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/).

**aṇṇal** – chief. This indicates the relationship for carrying out the aforesaid activities. It indicates the unbreakable relationship even if he is not the one to protect.

**maṟṟu allāl uḷam kidandavāṟṟāl uṇarndu** – apart from these \[activities\], mediating on all the others which were to be thought of. Other than the general activities that he had carried out for protecting the world, there will be many other activities to which he would have undertaken for the sake of his followers, making them feel “how simple is he” and losing their hearts out. āḻvār thinks that since he melts on thinking of activities of emperumān other then whatever had been mentioned in this pāsuram, others will also melt like he did.

The sequence to get the correct flow of meaning is: *jñālam aḷandu idandu uṇḍu umiḻnda aṇṇalai maṟṟallāl uḷam kidandavāṟṟāl uṇarndu ariyuruvan pādamalar mālai aṇindu kaikōli kālai toḻudu eḻumin.*

We shall take up the 48<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 48. Unarnday marai nangum

*uṇarndāy maṟai nāngum ōdināy nīdi maṇandāy malarmagaḷ tōḷ mālē maṇandāy pōy vēyirumsāral viyaliru jñālam sūḻ māyirum sōlai malai*

**Пословный перевод:**

mālē – Oh one who has lot of affection! \[towards his followers\]; maṟai nāngum – the four vēdams (sacred texts); uṇarndāy – (during every deluge) kept in your mind and let them out; nīdi – texts such as smrutis which explain the concepts in vēdams \[similar to an auxiliary text\]; ōdināy – you mercifully gave through sages such as manu et al; malar magaḷ – periya pirātti, who was born in a flower; tōḷ – with her divine shoulders; maṇandāy – you are together, always; vēy irum sāral – having foothills abounding with bamboo shoots; viyal – wondrous; iru jñālam sūḻ – circum-ambulated by people in this expansive world; māyirum sōlai malai pōy – coming to tirumālirum sōlai \[a divine abode near present day madurai\]; maṇandāy – are residing with happiness in your divine mind

**Перевод:**

**uṇarndāy maṟai nāngum – you remembered the four vēdas (sacred texts). You released the vēdas during creation of worlds after deluge, just as a person wakes up after sleeping and just as a person remembers after forgetting something. You had remembered to keep the vēdas safely when the sentient and insentient entities were without a form or name \[during deluge\] and released them at the start of creation, the next time that it occurs. Since you remembered the vēdas again, properly, it has affirmed the permanency of the vēdas as well as the fact that they were not written by any one person. Instead of saying vēdam seydān (he created the vēdams), āḻvār is saying vēdam uṇarndān (he remembered the vēdams). This is similar to the saying in kātakam 3-9-50 “pūrvē pūrvēbhyō vaca ētadūcu:” (the forefathers repeated the words of their forefathers). nammāḻvār has divinely mentioned this in tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-7 “uḷan sudarmigu surudiyuḷ” (he resides in the radiant vēdas) and in tiruvāimoḻi 1-6-2 “mudu vēda mudalvan”(he is termed as the first, complete entity by the ancient vēdas). He gave vēdas, in a way similar to giving a copper inscription which gives the details of where a treasure is hidden.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “are there any activities which we have carried out for followers such that you are able to think of without forgetting?”. āḻvār responds saying “Are the benefits carried out by dēvarīr (honorific  way of saying ‘you’) one or two? āren’t they so many!” āḻvār expands the term maṟṟallāl used in the previous pāsuram, in this. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ōdināy nīdi** – you created the upabruhmaṇams (auxiliary texts) such as smruti (darmaśāstras or texts about righteousness), itihāsa (famous epics śrī rāmāyaṇam and mahābhāratam) and purāṇas (ancient texts giving the details of various incarnations). Just as the face reflects what is running in the mind, these upabrahmaṇams reflect what is mentioned in vēdas.

**maṇandāy malarmagaḷ tōḷ** – you hugged the shoulders of [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) (śrī mahālakśmi) who is established in the vēdas. Not only is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) established in the vēdas, but the fact that he lives permanently with periya pirātti in śrīvaikuṇṭam is also established in the vēdas. (subsequently several examples confirming that emperumān is always with periya pirātti are quoted). yajus samhitai 4-4-12 says “*asyēśānā jagatō viṣṇu patnī*” (she is the mother for the world and consort for viṣṇu); puruśa sūktam “*hṟīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau*” (bhūmidēvi and lakśmidēvi are your consorts); “īśānā dēvī bhuvanasya patnī” (she controls the world and is consort of viṣṇu); yajur 3-3 “*śraddayā dēvō dēvatvamaśnutē*” (because of pirātti, emerpumān gets the inherent quality of being the lord); śrī sūktam 9 “*īśvarīm sarvabhūtānām*” (she controls all the living beings); lakshmi sahasranāmam 1 “*pumpradānēśvara īśvarīm*” (she is the consort of emperumān who controls jīvātmā and primordial matter); śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 37-18 “*aprayamēyamhi tattēja: yasya sā janakātmajā*” (isn’t the radiance of that śrī ramā with whom sītā, the daughter of janaka, is together, immeasurable!); śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāndam 4-7 “*kāvyam rāmāyaṇam krutsnam sītāyā: caritam*” (the entire śri ṟamāyaṇam epic is about the story of sītā). Thus, the greatness of [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) is mentioned in vēdas and upabruhmaṇas. vēdam affirms that emperumān and pirātti are always together. Being established by vēdas is symbolic of being the supreme entity, just as being the consort of pirātti is.

**maṇandāy pōy vēy irum sāral viyal iru jñālam sūḻ māyirum sōlai malai** – you desired to dwell in tirumālirum sōlai so that people of the world can see you and enjoy you with their eyes instead of remaining only in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) as mentioned by vēdas, which will remain just that way – on paper \[where people of this world could not have seen you\]. You were happy with the huge tirumālirum sōlai which has abundance of bamboo shoots on its foot hills, which is amazing and which is attained by people of the world. If the meaning for the term *mālē* is taken as ‘one with affection’, the meaning for this term will be that this affection becomes the reason for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to dwell in tirumālirum sōlai.

We shall take up the 49<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 49. Malai elum

*malai ēḻum mānilangal ēḻum adira kulaisūḻ kuraikadalgaḷ ēḻum mulai sūḻnda nanjurattup peṇṇai navinṟu uṇḍa nāvan enṟu anjādu en nenjē aḻai*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjē – Oh my heart!; m alai ēḻum – the seven kulaparvatams (major mountains); mā nilangaḷ ēḻum – the seven dvīpams (islands) such as jambhūdvīpam etc; kulai sūḻ kurai kadalgaḷ ēḻum – the seven oceans which are surrounded by shores and which raise continuous noise; adira – to vibrate; mulai sūḻnda nanju urattup peṇṇai – the cruel demonic person pūtanā, who had poison all over her bosom; navinṟu – speaking (gibberish words even as he was drinking her milk); uṇḍa – one who drank; nāvan – Oh one who has the divine tongue!; anjādu – without any fear; aḻai – call out

**Перевод:**

**malai ēḻum – the seven kulaparvatams which are like huge support for earth**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that after emperumān took residence at tirumalai, there is no need to fear that there is any hurdle for us. Hence, call out his name in such a way that the entire world vibrates with that. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mā nilangaḷ ēḻum** – the seven dvīpams such as jambhūdvīpam etc

**kulai sūḻ kurai kadalgaḷ ēḻum** – the seven oceans, without crossing their shores, remaining within them and making noise

**adira** – making these to vibrate

**mulai sūḻnda nanjurattup peṇṇai** – pūtanā, who was strong, after applying poison all over her bosom

**navinṟu uṇḍa nāvan enṟu** – having a tongue which was speaking some unintelligible words as if he were expressing his thankfulness to her for nursing him

**anjādu en nenjē aḻai** – call out to him without fearing about hurdles.

What will happen to the hurdles? They will face the same fate as pūtanā did.

**anjādu en nenjē aḻai** – there is nothing that we need to do for removing our enemies. It is enough if we recite his divine names and enjoy them. Alternatively, we can construe the meaning for *anjādu* as “not being afraid that we are not qualified to recite his names”. Even a demonic person such as pūtanā was able to approach him and hence we need not fear to approach him. āḻvār tells his divine heart to call him out loudly such that the whole world vibrates with that call.

We shall take up the 50<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 50. Alaippan tirumalai

*aḻaippan tirumālai āngu avargaḷ sonna piḻaippil perum peyarē pēsi iḻaippariya āyavanē yādavanē enṟu avanai yār mugappum māyavanē enṟu madittu*

**Пословный перевод:**

iḻaippu ariya – difficult to think of; āyavanē – Oh one born as a covherd boy!; yādavanē – Oh one who came in the clan of yadu (as vasudēvar’s son); enṟu – saying so; māyavanē enṟu – saying, Oh one who has amazing qualities!; madittu – meditating; āngu avargaḷ sonna – those in tiruvāyppādi (gōkulam) who called out to emperumān; piḻaippu il perum peyarē pēsi – reciting the great names which engage the person (reciting them) and destroying them; yār mugappum – in front of everyone; tirumālai avanai – that emperumān tirumāḷ; aḻaippan – I will call

**Перевод:**

**aḻaippan tirumalai – āḻvār is calling out to the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, to see him. He is talking about emperumān without any inhibition. nammāḻvār too has mercifully mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 9-8-10 “sindai kalangit tirumāl enṟu aḻaippan ” (I will call out to emperumān emotionally). Will not a brāhmaṇa, even if he becomes deranged mentally, recite only vēdam (sacred text)? In the same way, āḻvār says that he will call out to emperumān even if his mind is not steady. Has it not been said in kriṣṇa karṇāmrutam 2-59 “vikrētu kāmā kila gōpakanyā murāri pādārpita cittavrutti: tadyādikam mōhavaśādavōcat gōvinda dāmōdara mādavēti” (when a covherd girl, infatuated with the divine feet of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) , set out to sell curd, called out to people to buy curd by shouting ‘gōvindā, dāmōdarā, mādavā’ \[instead of shouting ‘curd’\] due to her infatuation with him)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had told his divine mind to recite emperumān’s divine names. Just as the girls in tiruvāyppādi (gōkulam) could not continue after starting to recite his divine names and became weary, he too starts to recite his divine names. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

How is this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) calling out?

**āngu avargaḷ sonna** – saying the same words that those in tiruvāyppādi said, thinking that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will have sympathy for him if he calls out to emperumān in the same way that the people in gōkulam did. Could one not say words which will have the same meaning as the one that the covherd girls had said? No, only the same words should be told. siṟiyāttān said “more than the meaning of [dvaya mantram](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html), the words contained in it are important. emperumān will be happy if we recite them”  In the same way, repeating the words mentioned by those in tiruvāyppādi.

**piḻaippil perum peyarē pēsi** – reciting the divine names which will make it difficult to survive once someone meditates on their inner meanings, due to ecstasy. Even if the divine names have only two or three letters, since one would feel weary reciting them with complete involvement, such names have immeasurable greatness.

**piḻaippil perum peyar** – divine name which, after hearing, will not let one survive. In the assembly of sages, the divine names will be such that once they are recited, they will liberate the person from all sins, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 1-97 “*ya: patēt rāmacaritram sarvapāpai: pramucyatē*” (one who reads rāmacaritam (the history of śrī rāma) will be liberated from all sins). But in the assembly of [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), divine names will be like a poisoned river. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully said in tiruvāimoḻi 9-5-8 “*uyirkku adu kālan enṟu irandēṟku nīr kuyil paidalgāḷ kaṇṇan nāmamē kuḻaṟik konṟīr*” (āḻvār has warned the cuckoo birds not to tell the names of kaṇṇan as those names will kill him; yet the birds called out the names of kaṇṇan).

**perum peyar** – even if the divine name is made up of one letter, it will be immeasurable.

**īḻaippariya** – the divine name will be such that no one can approach it on his own efforts. Alternatively, the divine name will be such that it will be beyond one’s thoughts and will hurt women folk.

**āyavanē yādavanē enṟu avanai** – it is possible that the covherd girls will call out to him as “Oh kriṣṇa! Oh the son of śrī vasudēva!” thus referring to the clan in which he was born and in which he was brought up. Both being a covherd boy and being the son of vasudēvar attract others towards him. The benefit of being connected to these two clans is that he was able to marry neeḷaip pirātti (nappinnai; neeḷā dēvi) as a covherd boy and rukmiṇip pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) as a kśatriya warrior.

**yār mugappum** – calling him out as stated above, in front of everyone, without distinguishing between whether that person is a desired person or not.

**māyavanē enṟu madittu** – thinking that he is an amazing entity.

We shall take up the 51<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.
