## 51. Madikkanday nenje

*madikkaṇḍāy nenjē maṇivaṇṇan pādam madikkaṇḍāy maṟṟavan pēr tannai madikkandāy pērāḻi ninṟu peyarndu kadal kadainda nīrāḻi vaṇṇan niṟam*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh heart!; maṇivaṇṇan pādam – the divine feet of emperumān who has bluish complexion; madi kaṇḍāy – think about them; maṟṟu – also; avan pēr tannai – his divine names; madi kaṇḍāy – think of them; pēr āḻi ninṟu peyarndu – awakening from his sleep in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kadal kadainda – one who churned the ocean (to offer nectar to dēvas); nīr āḻi vaṇṇan niṟam – the complexion of emperumān which is like the colour of ocean; madi kaṇḍāy – meditate on it

**Перевод:**

**madikkaṇḍāy nenjē maṇivaṇṇan pādam – Oh heart! Think of the divine feet of the one who removes fatigue, like a blue gemstone.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, thinking about how he had slipped from the correct way \[of calling out to emperumān\] tells his heart “Oh heart! You enjoy his beauty etc separately in a steady way unlike me who became emotional”. He says that he will think about emperumān through his heart, apart from speaking about him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**maṇivaṇṇan pādam** – Is his divine form forgettable? Fall at his divine feet, having lost out to his beauty.

**madikkaṇḍāy maṟṟu avan pēr tannai** – not only that, think of his other divine names which refer to his beauty.

**madikkaṇḍāy** – think and try to get uplifted.

**pērāḻi ninṟu peyarndu kadal kadainda** – In order to give nectar to dēvas (celestial entities) as desired by them, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did not mind getting out of his ocean which was actually his bed and churned the same ocean. He carried out a task for those who did not ask for him but desired ulterior benefit. He churned his own bed since he attained those who came to him and requested him.

**nīrāḻi vaṇṇar niṟam** – Just like a black ocean churned the white one, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is black in colour, churned the milky ocean. Think of such a beautiful entity. Did he not churn in such a way that the standing ocean churned the lying ocean! One, who has the complexion of ocean, churned the ocean.

**niṟam madikkaṇḍāy** – dēvas thought only of their benefit and did not think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who carried out the task for them. Oh heart! Do not indulge in such meanness and think of emperumān.

We shall take up the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 52. Niram kariyan

*niṟam kariyan seyya nedumalrāḷ mārvan aṟam periyan ār adu aṟivār maṟam purinda vāḷarakkan pōlvānai vānavarkōn tānattu nīḷ irukkaikkuyttān neṟi*

**Пословный перевод:**

niṟam kariyan – (like dark clouds which have filled up water) the dark coloured; seyya nedu malrāḷ mārvan – emperumān who has periya pirātti, who is reddish in complexion and who resides in an expansive lotus, on his chest; aṟam periyan – he is very generous in his mercy; maṟam purinda – one who was inimical; vāḷ arakkan pōlvānai – mahābali, who was like the warrior demon rāvaṇa; vānavar kōn tānattu – in svargam, the land of dēvēndra (the Lord of celestial entities); nīḷ irukkaikku – enabling him to live for a long time; neṟi – in pātāḷa lōkam (nether world); uyttān – emperumān who sent him there; adu – his mercy; Ar aṟivār – who is capable to estimate?

**Перевод:**

**niṟam kariyan – emperumān has a divine physical beauty which will remove all the anguish of his followers. He has the capability to remove their fatigue.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān’s beauty will make him too, who has the ability to advise his divine mind, to be bewildered. He also recounts the mercy that he received due to emperumān’s connection with pirātti and says that this is exclusive for him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**seyya nedumalarāḷ mārvan** – he has the divine chest in which pirātti, who, like a lightning amidst cloud, having reddish complexion which is the exact opposite of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s dark complexion, resides always.

**aṟam periyan** – due to his being together with her, he is always merciful.

**seyya nedumalarāḷ mārvan aṟam periyan** – the reason for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s mercy is pirātti. The reason for pirātti’s wealth is emperumān.

**Ar adu aṟivār** – none knows this secret. It is like the nineteenth language which no one knows. Only [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) and their followers, [ācāryars](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/full-list/), know this.

**Ar adu aṟivār** – who knows his inner mind and external disposition? There is no one who knows [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) completely.

**maṟam purinda vāḷarakkan pōlvānai** – mahābali who is cruel like rāvaṇa. Both of them had the quality of considering emperumān’s possession as theirs. While rāvaṇa seized his consort sītāppirātti, mahābali took hold of his world. Thus, mahābali is apt to be beheaded like rāvaṇa. However, the reason for leaving aside mahābali unlike rāvaṇa who was beheaded, was because of the little bit of magnanimity that he had.

**vānavarkōn tānattu nīḷirukkaikku uyttān neṟi** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), with his grace, kept mahābali in pātāla lōkam (nether world) which is superior to indra’s kingdom since he felt “why should mahābali, who had a desire in kingdom, lose it? Why should he seize indra’s kingdom and why should I obtain it in an improper way? Let me give him his kingdom in a proper way”. Who would indeed know emperumān’s nature since he kept mahābali in pātāla lōkam where he could stay for a very long time unlike indralōkam where he could lose out indra’s kingdom to someone else like indra lost and feel miserable? *neṟiyālē uyttān* would also mean his natural quality (through grace). Was his nature such that anyone would have known? śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-30 says “*tadāmaratvam trida śātipatyam manvantaram pūrṇamapēta śatru:*” (mahābali  attained the position of dēvēndra, ruling the three worlds for one manvantaram, without enemies). Who will know the mercy of emperumān who kept mahābali in pātala lōkam so that he could attain the position of indra?

We shall take up the 53<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 53. Neriyar kularkarrai

*neṟiyār kuḻaṟkaṟṟai munninṟu pin tāḻndu aṟiyādu iḷam giri enṟu eṇṇi piriyādu pūm kodikkaḷ vaigum porupunal kunṟu ennum vēngadamē yām virumbum veṟpu*

**Пословный перевод:**

neṟiyār aṟiyādu – not knowing that they \[are cētanas who\] are deeply integrated with the path at tirumalai hills; kuḻal kaṟṟai mun ninṟu pin tāḻndu – sprouting from the hair on the front side of their heads and growing on their back side; iḷam giri enṟu eṇṇi – thinking that they are small hills; pūm kodikkaḷ – creepers with flowers; piriyādu – without leaving that place; vaigum – residing permanently; poru punal kunṟu ennum – being known famously as tirumalai, with abundant streams; vēngadamē – only tiruvēngadam; yām virumbum – the one that I desire; veṟpu – divine hills

**Перевод:**

**neṟiyār kuḻal kaṟṟai – on the locks of hair of those who are carrying out penance, thinking only of tiruvēngadam, avoiding being engaged with worldly pursuits.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that he has very high regard for tiruvēngadamalai \[tirumalai\] where emperumān, who has the nature of showering mercy even on mahābali, has taken residence so that he could offer himself to everyone. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mun ninṟu pin tāḻndu aṟiyādu iḷam giri enṟu eṇṇi** – the creepers there \[on tiruvēngadam hills\], bearing flowers, would not think that they are human beings with intelligence \[those mentioned in the earlier phrase\] since they are not breathing or moving their physical bodies, and would grow on them, from front to back, thinking that they are small hills. Alternatively, the words *mun ninṟu pin tāḻndu* could be construed as referring to those who have locks of hair on their front and back sides of head and the creepers would grow on them. In other words, they have locks of hair on front and back sides of their head due to their greatness.

**piriyādu pūm kodikkaḷ vaigum** – the creepers, without leaving that place, will carry on with their activities. The creepers will think that if they (who are fully integrated with the path in tiruvēngadam) are human beings, they will move at some point of time or the other and since they are not moving, the creepers will consider them as supporting poles for growing further.

**porupunal kunṟu ennum vēngadamē** – tirumalai having abundance of streams. This mentions about the waterfalls and springs which are seen in abundance in tiruvēngadam.

**pūm kodikkaḷ vaigum** – vēngadam which has flowering creepers which take residence on the hills.

**yām virumbum veṟpu** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that it is the place that he desires. While those who are carrying out penance in tirumalai would leave their bodies and go to some other world and assume different physical bodies, āḻvār says that he has reaped the benefit of being born with this body in this world itself and that he desires to enjoy the tirumalai hills.

Let us take up the 54<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 54. Verpenru irum solai

*veṟpenṟu irum sōlai vēngadam enṟu ivviraṇḍum niṟpu enṟu nī ninaikkum nīrmaipōl niṟpu enṟu uḷam kōyil uḷḷam vaittu uḷḷinēn veḷḷattu iḷam kōyil kaividēl enṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

veṟpu enṟa – widely known as tirumalai; irum sōlai – tirumāḷ irum sōlai; vēngadam – tiruvēngadam hills; enṟa ivviraṇḍum – thus these two hills; niṟpu enṟu – the place that we desire to reside in; nī madikkum nīrmail pōl – just as you have desired in your divine mind; uḷam kōyil – (my) heart, another temple; niṟpu enṟu – a place that we desire to reside in; uḷḷam vaittu – knowing that you are thinking of, in your divine mind; veḷḷattu iḷam kōyil – tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) which is like a bālalayam \[temporary structure to accommodate emperumān); kai vidēl enṟu – please do not give up, saying so; uḷḷinēn – I pray

**Перевод:**

**veṟpu enṟu irum sōlai – the divine hill in the southern part, called as tirumāḷ irum sōlai**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After knowing that āḻvār desires his frontier land tirumalai, emperumān kept the desire that he had towards his two dwelling places, tirumālirunjōlai and tiruvēngada malai, in āḻvār’s divine mind, to the same extent. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vēngadam enṟu ivviraṇḍum** – the divine hill tiruvēngadam on the northern side, these two places

**niṟpu enṟu** – the apt dwelling place for us \[here the term “us” is used in the honorific sense, to refer to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]

**nī ninaikkum nīrmai pōl** – just as the essence that you think

**niṟpu enṟu uḷam kōyil uḷḷam vaittu uḷḷinēn** – thinking that my heart, which is like a temple, is the dwelling place for you

**veḷḷattu iḷam kōyil kai vidēl enṟu uḷḷinēn** – I am afraid that tiruppāṟkadal, which is the origin for you before you shifted to tirumālirum sōlai and tiruvēngadam, should not go waste with grass growing there, after you decided to shift to my heart. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) prays to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) that he should not give up tiruppāṟkadal which was like a bālālayam for emperumān (temporary structure to take residence in) before he shifted to āḻvār’s divine heart. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully stated in his periya tiruvandādi 68 “*kallum kanai kadalum vaikunda vānādum pullenṟu oḻindana kol*” (emeprumān’s divine dwelling places such as tiruvēngadam, tiruppāṟkadal, śrīvaikuṇṭam etc have become very insignificant, with no one bothering about them after emperumān shifted his residence to āḻvār’s divine mind)

**iḷam kōyil kai vidēl** – Could one forget the place which had done great favour for him? Should one not be thankful for the favour received? Hence, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) prays to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not to give up tiruppāṟkadal. māndātā, a king, gave all his 50 daughters in marriage to a sage by name saubari. When māndātā went to daughters’ houses, individually, to check whether they were happy, each daughter told him “there is no problem for me. It is just that he does not go to anyone else’s house at all”. In the same way, āḻvār is also afraid that emperumān will stay only in his heart and will give up his other dwelling places such as tiruppāṟkadal etc.

We shall take up the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 55. Enru marandariyen

*enṟu maṟandaṟiyēn ēḻ piṟappum eppoḻudum ninṟu ninaippu oḻiyā nīrmaiyāl venṟi adal āḻi koṇḍa aṟivanē inbak kadal āḻi nī aruḷik kāṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

venṟi – being always victorious; adal – being in combat (with enemies); āḻi – cakkaram (disc); koṇḍa – having in the hand; aṟivanē – Oh the omniscient!; nī – you, who are like this; ēḻ piṟappum – in all births; eppoḻudum – in all states; ninṟu ninaiipu oḻiyā nīrmaiyāl – never forgetting to hold me in your thoughts; enṟum maṟandaṟiyēn – I never forgot you; inbam kadal āḻi – the huge ocean of happiness; aruḷik kāṇ – you should bestow on me

**Перевод:**

**enṟu maṟandaṟiyēn – I have never known forgetting you. It is mentioned that all that has been lost would be forgotten if he is seen. āḻvās was asked whether the reason for this is as mentioned in śāstram, viz. śravṇam (listening), mananam (thinking), nididyāsanam (meditating) about bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to emperumān) followed by upāsanam and druvānusmruti (thinking without any break, which is the final state of bhakti yōgam to worship emperumān). āḻvār says**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that after emperumān desired to dwell in āḻvār’s divine mind, āḻvār became full in both knowledge and strength without any shortcoming. He prays to emperumān that as a consequence, emperumān should carry out the means for āḻvār attaining him. He says that emperumān helped him to think of him in this world where it is very rare to think of emperumān. Now, emperumān should himself provide the means for āḻvār to attain him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ēḻpiṟappum eppoḻudum ninṟu ninaippoḻiyā nīrmaiyāl** – in all my states you \[emperumān\] had come in front of me and graced me sayhing “let him not forget us anytime and attain us”. Since this thought of yours \[about me\] never changes, I am having the quality of not forgetting you. śrī bhagavat gītā 15-15 says “*mattasmrutirjñānampōhananca*”(memory, knowledge and forgetfulness all emanate from me). Also, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-26](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-26/) “*vēdāham samatītāni vartamānāni ca arjuna, bhaviśyāṇi ca bhūtāni mām tu vēda na kaścana*”(only I know those who existed, who are existing and who will exist. Oh arjuna! There is none who knows me truly)! As mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-8-28 “*bhōdōyam viṣṇuriyam bhuddi:*” (this [viṣṇu](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself is knowledge. She ([mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)) is intelligence) emperumān is termed knowledge. [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) has mercifully mentioned in  tiruccanda viruttam 101 “*ninna pāda pangayam nirantaram ninaippadāga nī ninaikka vēṇḍumē*“ (you have to bless me so that I am able to think of your divine lotus feet all the time);  [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-10-marappum-gyanamum/) “*maṟappaṟa ennuḷḷē manninān*” (he stayed put in my heart such that I can never forget him). Even the knowledge for the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) has to be taken from emperumān’s treasure \[of knowledge\]. It is not only that knowledge is subservient to *darmam* (righteousness); ātmā’s *dharma bhūta jñānam* (knowledge through senses) is also subservient to emperumān. When his existence itself is under emperumān’s control, it goes without saying that his knowledge is also under emperumān’s control. Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he did not attain emperumān through karma yōgam (path of deeds), jñāna yōgam (path of knowledge) and the resultant bhakti yōgam (path of devotion) but through emperumān himself who is siddōpāyam (readily available as means).

**venṟi adalāḻi koṇḍa aṟivanē** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says “We have done what we were supposed to do; can you compel us to do more? ”. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) responds “You have the weapon which removes all hurdles \[discus\]. More than that, you know about yourself fully and you also know about me. You also know that I cannot attain you through my efforts and that I can attain you only through your grace. āren’t you the omnipotent and the omniscient? ” Hasn’t jitantē ślōkam 1-6 said about emperumān  “*naiva kincit parōkśantē pratyakśōsi na kasyacit; naiva kincidasiddantē na ca siddōsi kasyacit*” (there is nothing that you have not seen; you are not seen by anyone; there is  nothing that has not been attained by you; you are not attained by anyone)! Just as *ajñānam*  (ignorance) and *aśakti* (powerlessness) are cētana’s qualities, *jñānam* (knowledge) and *śakti* (power) are emperumān’s qualities. āḻvār says that he has not seen any shortcoming in emperumān’s knowledge and power. Hence

**inbak kadal āḻi nī aruḷik kāṇ** – you, who had granted the knowledge without any shortcoming, to attain you, should also enable me to have unlimited happiness out of attaining you, with which I can enjoy you. Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) removed the contraction in knowledge, he should also enable as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 7 “*ēśahyēvānandayāti*” (he – emperumān  – himself makes jīvātmā ecstatic). You should grant me the ecstasy born out of seeing you. When you carry out the difficult task of enabling me to see you, can you not carry out the easier task of granting the consequent happiness? Both the words *kadal*  and *āḻi* mean ocean. The combination of these two words *kadalāḻi* thus refers to excessive happiness, Viz. Ocean of happiness.

We shall take up the 56<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 56. Kanak kalikadal

*kāṇak kaḻi kādal kaimikkuk kāttināl nāṇap padum enṟāl nāṇumē pēṇik karumālaip ponmēni kāttāmun kāttum tirumālai  nangaḷ tiru*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālai kāṇak kaḻi kādal – the deep desire to worship emperumān; kai mikku kāttināl – if it starts increasing greatly; nāṇap padum enṟāl – if one should stay quietly; nāṇumē – is it possible to stay quiet?; karumālai – that emperumān who is of dark complexion; pon mēni mun kāttā – before his beautiful divine \[physical\] form identifies him; nangaḷ tiru – pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who is our puruśakāra bhūtai (one who is of recommendatory nature); pēṇi – with desire; kāttum – will identify

**Перевод:**

**kāṇak kaḻi kādal – an affection which will not diminish, even if not seen. Alternatively, an affection which goes on increasing as one goes on seeing**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “when you are very sure that the means to attain him is through his mercy, why are you, having  been in this swirl of births, compelling him to shower his grace on you when you should stay aloof, saying ‘let him do when he wants’?”. āḻvār responds saying “isn’t that behaviour of staying aloof, only for those with true knowledge \[about emperumān\]? Is it possible for those who have attachment towards emperumān born out of ignorance, arising from their affection for him, to stay quiet?”. āḻvār was again asked, “You are his servitor. Should you not be as per your nature \[of being his servitor\]? Could you compel him like this?” āḻvār says “even though that \[of staying quiet\] is the rule, when the affection starts growing, is it possible to remain like that?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kai mikkuk kāttināl** – various meanings are given – if it grows abundantly; if it breaks through the shore;  if it increases excessively; if I get involved more.

**nāṇappadum enṟāl nāṇumē** – is it possible for one, until [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself comes head over heels, to stand in a corner, unobtrusively, shyly? Is it possible to say that we should not fall head over heels towards him? Is it possible to say that you are the lord and I am the servant?

**nāṇumē** – is it possible to hear mere words and stay quietly?

On top of that

**karumālai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has dark complexion, which removes one’s fatigue.

**ponmēni kāttā mun** – before showing his form which attracts one’s mind

**tirumālai nangaḷ tiru pēṇi mun kāttum** – [tirumagaḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), who is periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi), will show us emperumān, the consort of tirumagaḷ, desirously. Just as it is mentioned in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 1 “*tirukkaṇḍēn ponmēni kaṇḍēn*”, only after having *darśan* (audience) of pirātti can one have *darśan* of emperumān. If the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (sentient entity) commits an offence by falling head over heels on them, not considering his faults, due to the radiance of their two being together, pirātti is there near [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to say, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 166-4 “*na kaścit nāparādyati*” (there is none who has not committed a fault) and to make emperumān to ignore his offence and unite with him.

**nāṇappadum enṟāl nāṇumē** – for the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), the involvement \[with emperumān\] is there without any shortcoming. emperumān does not mind the faults in the cētana. Why then should he feel shy?

**nangaḷ tiru** – this implies that she is the mother for us, to carry out the role of *puruśkāram* (recommend us to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). Since she is there to carry out that role, the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) does not have to move away from emperumān thinking that he is not qualified to attain him. Neither does he have to fear that he has committed an offence. He cannot also stand aloof, saying “let him shower his mercy when he chooses to”.

We shall move on to the 57<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 57. Tirumangai ninrarulum

*tirumangai ninṟaḷum deyvam nā vāḻttum karumam kadaippidimin kaṇḍīr urimaiyāl ēttinōm pādam irum tadakkai endai pēr nāl disaiyum kēttīrē nām*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumangai – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); ninṟu aruḷum deyvam – emperumān, in whose chest she stays permanently; nā vāḻttum karumam – the deed of praising with tongue; kadaippidimin kaṇḍīr – hold on, surely; nām – we; irum tadakkai endai – emperumān who has two long divine hands; padam – \[his\] divine feet; pēr – with his divine names; urimaiyāl – as per our nature; ēttinōm – praised; nāl disaiyum – those in the four directions; kēttīrē – did you listen?

**Перевод:**

**tirumangai ninṟu aruḷum deyvam – emperumān, in whose divine chest periya pirātti is dwelling permanently, as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu” (I will not leave this place even for a moment) and blessing \[devotees\]. pirātti is the identification for emperumān, never leaving him. śrī rangarāja stavam 1-9 says “lakśmīpadalākśaika lakśaṇam” (emperumān has the footmarks of pirātti’s divine feet as his identification). Only he, who has the marks of pirātti’s decorative designs on her divine feet, is the supreme lord. This identification is not coming out of his deeds such as creating the world etc. tirumaḻisai āḻvār has mercifully mentioned in his nānmugan tiruvandādi 53 “tiruvillāt tēvarai tēṟēlmin tēvu” (don’t consider as Lord, one, who is not associated with pirātti). At the beginning of gītā bāśyam \[a commentary on śrī bhagavat gītā by svāmy rāmānujar\] it is said “śriya: pati nikhilahēyapratyanīka kalyāṇaikatāna:” (one who is the consort of pirātti, being the opposite of all faults and having all the auspicious qualities). Even before saying that he has auspicious qualities, since it is mentioned that he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, this becomes his unique identification.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān, who is united with pirātti, is the one to be attained by all. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nā vāḻttum karumam** – in the activity of praising with tongue

**kadaippidimin kaṇḍīr** – instead of thinking “I am saying something”, keep this in your heart firmly.

**kadaippidimin kaṇḍīr** – be fully focussed on this.

For people like us, without any qualification, is it possible to praise like this?

**urimaiyāl ēttinōm** – did I praise him in some other method? Did I not worship him because he is my Lord! Hence, for you people also, there is no shortcoming in praising his divine feet.

**endai pēr nām urimaiyāl ēttinōm** – we praised his divine names because we are his followers. We, who know our relationship with him, praised him without trespassing that. We attained him with the blessings of [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), just like lakśmaṇa and vibhīśaṇa attained him. We did not try to attain him directly, just like sūrpaṇakā did, by breaking the method.

**irum tadakkai endai pēr** – we praised the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has two huge, well grown shoulders.

**nāl disaiyum kēttīrē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is asking whether the people in the directions listened to the meaning of this. By joining with the previous two words, making them as *endai pēr nāl disaiyum kēttīrē* one can construe the meaning as “aren’t these emperumān’s divine names! Did the people in all directions listen to these!”

We shall go on to the 58<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 58. Nam perra nanmaiyum

*nām peṟṟa nanmaiyum nāmangai nannenjattu ōmbiyirundu emmai ōduvittu vēmbin poruḷ nīrmaiyāyinum ponnāḻi pādenṟu aruḷ nīrmai tanda aruḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēmbu porulin nīrmai āyinum – (praising emperumān) even if it appears like bitterness, the quality of nīm leaves; pon āḻi – one who has the beautiful divine disc; pādu enṟu – let you sing; nā mangai – sarasvati \[the deity for jñānam, knowledge\]; nal nenjattu – in (our) good heart; ōmbi irundu – supporting; emmai – to us; ōduvittu – teaching us about emperumān’s matters; nām peṟṟa nanmaiyum – our benefit of singing poems; aruḷ nīrmai tanda aruḷ – came out of the grace of emperumān who has grace as his nature

**Перевод:**

**nām peṟṟa nanmaiyum nāmangai nannenjattu ōmbiyirundu emmai ōduvittu – it is my great benefit that sarasvati thought that my tongue is proper( place) for singing about emperumān and making me to praise him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that he got all these benefits through the mercy of emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ōmbi irundu** – when I was humble, making me praise him and when I was arrogant, allowing me to be in my way for some time and then teaching me

**ōduvittu** – teaching me in my way. Just like teaching princes without hurting them, making me to sing about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). vālmīki bhagavān composed śrī rāmāyaṇam due to the grace of brahmā. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) was graced by emperumān.

How did he shower his mercy on āḻvār?

**vēmbin poruḷ nīrmaiyāyinum ponnāḻi pādu enṟu** – you are seeing the beautiful divine disc in the divine hand of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! Its beauty is very sweet. Even if it appears to be bitter to you like the bitterness of nīm leaves, do not stop singing about him.

**ponnāḻi pādu enṟu** – is the matter to be sung about \[emperumān\] like the taste of nīm leaves? What is the reason for his showing partiality towards us?

**aruḷ nīrmai tanda aruḷ** – the reason for our getting the benefit of singing about him is the grace which he, who has grace as his nature, showered on us, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/).

**nāmangai ōduvittu** – this implies that sarasvati supported [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) in his singing about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-11-maniyai-vanavar/) “*soṟpaṇi sey āyiram* ” (among the thousand songs in which he carried out kainkaryam to emperumān through the faculty of speech). Hasn’t it been mercifully stated in śrīstavam 11 “*lōkē vanaspati:*” that sarasvati is a devotee of [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)!

We shall take up the 59<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 59. Arul purinda sindai

*aruḷ purinda sindai adiyār mēl vaittu poruḷ terindu kāṇguṟṟa appōdu iruḷ tirindu nōkkinēn nōkki ninaindēn adoṇkamalam ōkkinēn ennaiyum angu ōrndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

aruḷ purinda sindai – the divine mind focussed on mercy; adiyār mēl vaittu – keeping on us, his followers; poruḷ terindu – considering us as entities when we veren’t; kāṇguṟṟa appōdu – during that time when he showered his grace on us; iruḷ tirundu – getting rid of the darkness of ignorance; nōkkinēn – I analysed the nature of jīvātmā and paramātmā; nōkki – after analysing; oṇ kamalam adu – those divine feet \[of emperumān\] which are like beautiful lotus flowers; ninaindēn – meditated on them (as the goal to be attained); ōrndu – analysing (that there is no refuge for me other than these divine feet); ennaiyum – the ātmā; angu – at those divine feet; ōkkinēn – I offered

**Перевод:**

**arul purinda sindai – as mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 16-19 “tānaham dviśata: krūrān samsārēśu narādamān kśipāmi ajasram aśubān āsurīśvēva yōniśu” (I push those who hate me, those who are cruel, those who are the lowliest among humans and those who have inauspicious qualities, always into samsāram (materialistic realm) and that too in the clan of demons), instead of pushing me into such a clan, he showers his grace on me through his divine mind as said in śrī bhagavat gītā 10-10 “dadāmi bhuddiyōgam tam yēna māmupayāṇṭhi tē” (I give them intelligence with which they can reach me).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mentions about the first time that he received the grace from emperumān which was the cause for his benefits such as these. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aruḷ purinda sindai** – he has such a divine mind that he cannot sustain himself without showering grace. This implies that the faults present in the [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) are hidden from him by his grace.

**adiyār mēl vaittu** – he showers such grace on his followers like us. Showering his grace without looking at anything other than the criterion that we are his followers.  Here, the term *adiyār* (followers) can be construed to refer to followers in general or to the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) who was standing in the corridor \[of mrigaṇḍu maharishi in tirukkōvalūr\] along with the other two āḻvārs when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) started crowding around them with his consort \[he could be referring to the first three āḻvārs as *adiyār*\].

**adiyār mēl vaittu** – emperumān cannot exist without showering his grace on his followers. Hence, he showers the grace so that he does not lose himself; [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) terms this as causeless mercy.

**poruḷ terindu kāṇguṟṟa appōdu** – at that point of time when he showered his grace on us, so that we would know things as they are. Alternatively, this can be construed as: that point of time when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) considered them too as entities and showered his grace. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully stated in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-7-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/thiruvaimozhi-5-7-3-karulap-putkodi/) “*poruḷ allāda ennaip poruḷākki adimai koṇḍāy*” (you recognised me as a sentient entity when I was lying as an insentient being and made me a servitor).

Since he became qualified to receive his grace …..

**iruḷ tirindu nōkkinēn** – I found that I lost my ignorance which was a hurdle for me to get knowledge. Once I saw this ….

**nōkki** – I received the true knowledge that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the Lord and I am a servitor.  Hence …

**ninaindēn adoṇ kamalam** – I thought of your divine feet which are like lotus flower. I realised that I have to attain only your divine feet. I ascertained that this is my goal.

**ōrndu** – analysing that I, who am subservient to emperumān, have no connection with myself and that I am the possession of emperumān. Feeling sad that he had mistaken emperumān’s possession (the soul) as his own.

**ennaiyum angu ōkkinēn** – I submitted myself to his divine feet. I ascertained that he is the means \[to attain him\].

We shall take up the 60<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 60. Oruruvan allai

*ōruruvan allai oḷi uruvam nin uruvam īruruvan enbar irunilattōr ōruruvam ādiyām vaṇṇam aṟindār avar kaṇḍīr nīdiyāl maṇ kāppār ninṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ōr uruvan allai – (Oh emperumān!) you are not the one with unique divine form; oḷi uruvam – the radiant form (with which you are subservient to your followers); nin uruvam – is your form; irunilattōr – those who are on this earth; īr uruvan enbar – say that you have a huge form (of being the supreme lord); ōr uruvam – the incomparable form (of being subservient to followers); ādi Am vaṇṇam – being the cause for the salvation of all; aṟindār avar kaṇḍīr – only those who know (this); nīdiyāl – in the fair way; ninṟu – standing firmly; maṇ kāppār – have the ability to protect this world

**Перевод:**

**ōr uruvan allai – you are the one with the form which is independent. While kata upaniśat 13 says “nityō nityānām ” (supreme being has the quality of being permanent and being  of the same manner) and cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-1 says “ēkamēva advitīyam” (there is only one; there is no second), your form is not the one which is different from everyone/everything else and which controls everyone/everything else.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Does not āḻvār, who said this (please refer to the previous pāsuram), know that it is emperumān’s quality to do at the bidding of his devotees more than aiśvaryam! āḻvār says that emperumān will always protect his worlds and that he is very simple in his (āḻvār’s) matter. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**oḷi uruvan nin uruvam** – it is existing only for the sake of your followers, which is your quality. Just as it is mentioned in jitantē ślōkam 1-5 “*na tē rūpam nacākārō nāyudāni na cāspadam, tatāpi puruśākārō bhaktānām tvam prakāśasē*” (there is no quality, form, weapon or dwelling place exclusively for you; however, you are having all these qualities only for the sake of your followers) this is your true quality \[of existing for your followers\]. Your quality is not of the type mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/) “*uyarvaṟa uyarnalam udaiyavan*” (one who has auspicious qualities superior to those of everyone else). Rather, it is as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-2-elivarum-iyalvinan/) “*eḷivarum iyalvinan*” (having simplicity as his nature).

You, who are like this ….

**īr iruvan enbar irunilattōr** – those who think you have qualities beyond one’s capabilities to measure, are the ignorant ones. They would think that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the great supreme being, has only supreme qualities. An alternative meaning for *ōr uruvan allai* is that he does not have the same type of form always. In other words, as mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4-5](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-5/) “*bahūni mē vyatītāni janmāni*” (many births have passed for me) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-9-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/06/thiruvaimozhi-2-9-5-sirappil-vidu/) “*piṟappil palpiṟavip perumāḷ*” (emperumān who has had many births of his own volition, unlike we [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who were born due to our deeds), he has had many incarnations and divine names. *oḷi uruvam nin uruvam* – even when you were born like that \[with many incarnations\], your divine form was more and more of *śudda satvam* type (pure good state) and with unlimited radiance. Just as it is mentioned in yajur aśtakam 3-6 3-9 “*sa u śrēyān bhavati jāyamāna:*” (he becomes great just by being born), he has the quality as mentioned in these pramāṇams. *īr uruvan enbar iru nilattōr* – We can construe this as saying that the people of the world say that emperumān has one divine form as his basic form and another which he takes on whenever he incarnates.

**ōr uruvam** – the divine form which is radiant, since he incarnates for the sake of his followers.

**ādiyām vaṇṇam aṟindār avar kaṇḍīr** – those who know that it \[as mentioned in the previous verse\] is the one which is the cause for all the worlds. In other worlds

**ādiyām vaṇṇam aṟindār** – the vaiṣṇavas who know that others’ existence is only because of him ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) who is like this.

**ādi** – one who is responsible for the salvation of his followers

**nīdiyāl maṇ kāppār ninṟu** – they are the ones who stand firmly and protect the world, as mentioned in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 3-3 “*tīdil nanneṟi kātti engum tirindu*” (going to all the places, showing the correct path without any defect) and in [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 14](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/10/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-14/) “*tīrttakararāmin tirindu*” (going to various places and purifying them). If [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is termed as *svatantran* (independent) the world will go astray and only if he is termed as *paratantran* (dependent on his followers) will it remain in the correct way. Hence, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is correcting those people who say that he is difficult to attain. Earlier he gave up because of *ajñānam* (ignorance); now he is trying to give up due to *jñānam* (knowledge)

We shall take up the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 61. Ninrador padam

*ninṟadōr pādam nilam pudaippa nīṇḍa tōḷ senṟu aḷandadenbar disaiyellām anṟu karumāṇiyāy iranda kaḷvanē unnaip piramāṇittār peṟṟa pēṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – long ago; karumāṇi āy – as a bachelor with black complexion; iranda – asking for alms (from mahābali for 3 steps of earth); kaḷvanē – Oh cunning emperumān!; ninṟadu ōr pādam – one divine foot which stood (to measure the earth); nilam – the entire earth; pudaippa – hiding it; nīṇḍa tōḷ – the huge divine shoulder which grew; disai ellām senṟu – permeating through all directions; aḷandadu enbar – (knowledgeable people) say that it measured (the upper worlds); unnai piramāṇittār peṟṟa pēṟu – is the great benefit that all those who believed you, got

**Перевод:**

**ninṟadōr pādam nilam pudaippa – when nobody had even thought of, a divine foot suddenly hid the entire earth.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār narrates how emperumān showed that he is devoted to his followers. He says that not only his form, even his activities are devoted to his followers only. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ōr pādam** – āḻvār is not one who would say things in a mathematical way such ‘as one time one is one’ just as we would when one mentions about one divine foot, we would immediately say that the other divine foot did this. Hence, having mentioned about divine foot, immediately he talks about what the divine shoulder did.

**nīṇḍa tōḷ senṟu aḷandadu enbar disai ellām** – those with knowledge would say that once he obtained the alms he had asked for, his shoulders spread out in all directions and permeated everywhere. They would say that the moment mahābali poured water on his hand as a symbol of having acceded to vāmana’s request for alms, the shoulders became extremely joyful as if they had got something which they could never get and grew in all directions and measured.

**enbar** – āḻvār feels distressed that he is listening to this through his ears today, and is not seeing the incident! Where did I get lost on that day!

**anṟu karumāṇiyāy iranda kaḷvanē** –  Oh one who has the divine form which removes  the ennui \[of followers\], one who vouldn’t go until he obtained what he had asked for in alms, one who made himself as an expert in asking for alms!

**māṇiyāy** – vasn’t it the disguise of the great emperumān! If someone calls the great tirumalai hills as a ‘stone’ vouldn’t his mouth be torn asunder?

**iranda** – when mahābali was arrogant to claim the worlds of emperumān as his own, did not emperumān go to him and ask his own worlds from him as alms!

**kaḷvanē** – he showed such a \[deceitful\] divine form to mahābali and seized everything from mahābali.

**kaḷvanē** – did he cheat mahābali? Was it not āḻvār that he attracted with his beauty! Those who appropriate [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), which is actually emperumān’s, as their own are called *ātmāpahāris* (abductors of ātmā). Since emperumān abducts such people also, he can be called the ātmāpahāri of ātmāpahāris (abductor of abductors). However, when he had to take back his possession \[from mahābali\], instead of taking it directly, he had to obtain it through alms. The reason for that is ….

**unnaip piramāṇittār peṟṟa pēṟu** – the purpose of redeeming earth from mahābali was not for helping indra alone. Was it not to enable those who had given you the responsibility of taking care of themselves, to sleep in peace since there is nothing for them to do \[you would be doing everything for them\] after they think of this act of yours! [svāmy āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) says in [stōtra ratnam 25](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-25/) “*tvadagrē śaraṇāgatānām parābhava: natēnu rūpa:*” (it is not proper for you if those who surrendered to you suffer); just as he included everyone instead of talking about only him, if people obtain a benefit it will be for everyone and not for just one person. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 8-1-4 “*enganam tēṟuvar umarē*” (how will people believe that you are the protector?), talking in the plural instead of only about himself. It is said that [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) was asking of himself one day “these śrīvaiṣṇavas, after hearing one person say ‘I will take care of your responsibilities’ (gītācāryan/emperumān), do not carry out any activity themselves for their lives. Yet they live in comfort, without any shortcoming. What is the reason for this? ”. Later he mercifully said “I have understood the reason. Since they have given their responsibility to an entity, they will be without any worry. Since that person will carry out everything for them, including going as a messenger, driving their chariots, taking alms on their behalf etc, they will be peaceful without any shortcoming”

We shall take up the 62<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 62. Peronrum munnariyen

*pēṟonṟum munnaṟiyēn peṟṟaṟiyēn pēdaimaiyāl māṟenṟu solli vaṇanginēn ēṟin perutteruttam kōdu osiyap peṇṇasaiyin pin pōy eruttiṟutta nal āyar ēṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

peṇ nasaiyin pin pōy – possessed by the desire in the matter relating to nappinnaip pirātti; ēṟin – (the seven) bulls’; perutta eruttam – the huge necks; kōdu – and horns; osiya – such that they are broken; eruttu – the necks (of those bulls); iṟutta – broke; nal āyar ēṟu – kriṣṇa, the head of the great covherds; māṟu – being the enemy (of our sins); enṟu solli – thinking this way; vaṇanginēn – attained him; mun – before this; pēṟu onṟum aṟiyēn – I had not known of such an incomparable puruśārtam (end benefit); pēdaimaiyāl – due to ignorance; peṟṟu aṟiyēn – (until now) I had not attained it and thus lost

**Перевод:**

**pēṟu onṟum mun aṟiyēn – before this, I had not known of any benefit like this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that meditating on emperumān who is devoted to his followers and being firm that emperumān will remove all his hurdles and grant him what he needs, he attained emperumān. He says that just as he removed the bulls which were a hurdle in his attaining nappinnaip pirātti, and made her his own, he would remove the hurdles for him too. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**peṟṟu aṟiyēn** – I had lost it due to my ignorance. This benefit would have accrued to those who had known its existence. However, I had not even known its existence.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) gives the reason for not knowing it and not attaining it…..

**pēdamaiyāl** – Oh my! What will ignorance not do! īsn’t only this ignorance, the cause for everything!

**māṟu enṟu solli vaṇanginēn** – I attained him because he is the hurdle for the results of my deeds from reaching me. It is not that I attained him in the proper way. I attained him because he is capable of destroying my enemies.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) gives an example to show that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is inimical to his enemies…

**ēṟin perutteruttam kōdu osiya** – breaking the huge necks and horns of the bulls

**pen nasaiyin pin pōy** – this implies that being possessed by his desire for nappinnaip pirātti, and in anticipation of attaining her, unmindful of his own safety, he clashed with the bulls.

**eruttu iṟutta** – breaking the necks of bulls. He will be affectionate towards his followers just as he is affectionate towards his consorts. He will destroy their enemies.

**nal āyar ēṟu** – he is greater than all the covherds who are of his age. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he attained such an entity who would be inimical to his enemies (his sins). As result, just as he removed the seven bulls which were hurdles to his getting nappinnaip pirātti, āḻvār says that he would remove āḻvār’s hurdles too and offer himself to āḻvār.

We shall take up the 63<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 63. Erelum venradartta

*ēṟēḻum venṟadartta endai eri uruvattu ēṟēri patta vidu sābam pāṟēṟi uṇḍatalai vāy niṟaiyak kōttangai oṇkurudi kaṇḍa poruḷ sollil kadai*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēṟu ēḻum – the seven bulls; venṟu – being victorious over them; adartta – destroyed; endai – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa), my svāmy (lord); eri uruvattu ēṟu ēṟi patta – suffered by rudra who has a fiery form and who has bull as his vehicle; vidu sābam – curse given by brahmā; pāṟu ēṟi uṇḍa talai vāy niṟaiya – to fill up the mouth of the skull which eagles eat as food; kōdu – hand which is like a bud; am kai – with beautiful hands (given after tearing); oṇ kurudi – with beautiful blood; kaṇḍa poruḷ – that incident seen (when the skull fell out of \[rudra’s\] hands); kadai – will end up like mahābhārata story

**Перевод:**

**ēṟu ēḻum venṟu adartta endai – the lord who was victorious over the seven bulls (for the sake of nappinnaip pirātti)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that only emperumān is apt to be attained since he removed the curse of the egotistic rudra. He says that when there is any danger, without making any distinction, he would remove such danger, whether it is for his consorts or for the egotistic rudra. This implies that it is only emperumān who is greater than everyone else and that other īśvaras (lords) cannot be refuge for others as well as to themselves (in other words they are not capable of protecting others  or themselves). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eri uruvattu ēṟu ēṟi** – Siva who has the complexion of fire and who has bull as his vehicle

**ēṟu ēṟi** – Siva who has a bull (much like a mendicant having bull for his survival)

**patta idu sābam** – brahmā, who allowed until Siva plucked out his head, cursed him saying “since you have carried out the heinous crime of severing my head, let this skull never leave your hand”.

**idu sābam** – mātsya purāṇam 182 says “*kapālee tvam bhaviśyasi*” (you will become one having skull), the curse given by brahmā. brahmā, who could not tolerate the fame of Siva, asked him to sever the head and then put the blame on Siva for severing it.

**pāṟu ēṟi uṇḍa** – *pāṟu* refers to birds such as eagles, vultures etc. Siva went from place to place, carrying the skull, which is normally eaten by eagles and vultures, with the birds feeling happy looking at the sight.

**talai vāy niṟaiya** – to enable the (mouth of the ) skull to fill up

**kōttangai** – beautiful hand like a bud

**oṇ kurudi kaṇḍa poruḷ sollil** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) sprayed the sweat from his divine chest on the skull and made it to break out of Siva’s hand.

**oṇ kurudi** – beautiful blood. It was similar to the saffron on the chest of emperumān.

**am kai** – with the beautiful hand which was capable of removing the heinous crime.

**oṇ kurudi** –  mātya purāṇam 182 says “*tasya raktasya nisrutā*” (the flow of blood). Some people say that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) sprayed blood. Is it correct to say that blood was similar to vermillion? There is not much of a difference between the two. It is not incorrect to say this since it compares with the incomparable emperumān.

**kaṇḍa poruḷ sollil kadai** – If truth were to be told..

**kadai** – this is like mahābhāratam. Siva need not be an īśvaran like this; neither does he need to be one who had committed a heinous crime. nampiḷḷai would narrate an incident involving nanjīyar: One day, nanjīyar gave a mango to nampiḷḷai and asked him to give it to a person by name nambi tiruvaḻudivaḷanādu dāsar. When nampiḷḷai went to him, dāsar was explaining the meaning for this pāsuram (63<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram) “We have not heard that kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) who had killed seven bulls committed a crime or that he had to go to someone’s door to carry out any atonement. But Siva did not kill brahmā (brahmā was very much alive after the head was severed). By plucking the head of one who was alive even after losing his head, Siva committed a crime and started going to various places to get rid of its effect. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) removed that crime due to his connection. īsn’t this the difference between emperumān and others! ” (it is implied here that the one who killed the bulls did not commit any crime but the one who has bull as his vehicle, committed a crime by severing a head, though the person whose head he had severed, did not die!). Who is īśvaran among these three – Siva, who committed the crime by severing a head? ōr brahmā, who lost his head and suffered? ōr emperumān who removed the curse of Siva? You be the judge!

We shall move on to the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 64. Kadaiyin perum porulum

*kadaiyin perum poruḷum kaṇṇā nin pērē idayam iruandavaiyē ēttil kadaiyin tirumoḻiyāy ninṟa tirumālē unnaip parumoḻiyāl kāṇap paṇi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇā – Oh kriṣṇa!; kadaiyin – in itihāsams (epics) and purāṇams (ancient biographies); perum poruḷum – eminent meanings; idayam – their inner connotations; irundavaiyē – as they are; ēttil – if known and praised; nin pērē – (they are all) your divine names only; kadaiyin tirumoḻiyāy ninṟa tirumālē – Oh tirumāḷ (śrīman nārāyaṇa)! You are the epitome of all that is mentioned in the great vēdas; unnai – you; paru moḻiyālē – to enjoy after identifying you with the great words of vēdas; paṇi – please show mercy

**Перевод:**

**kadaiyin perum poruḷum kaṇṇā nin peyarē – if one were to delve deep into itihāsa (epics) and purāṇas (ancient literature dealing with biographies), what they reveal are your divine names which deal with your auspicious qualities and your divine activities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār prays to emperumān “you are the one who carries out beneficial activities even for those who are proud that they are īśvaras. You should help me, who cannot do anything without you and who am considering you as everything, to enjoy reciting your divine names.” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**idayam irundu avaiyē ēttil** – even if the meaning is not taken in its entirety and only the inner meaning is considered and [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is praised (it will be the same). If one were to consider the heart of *itihāsas* and *purāṇas* \[in other words, the central theme of these sacred texts\], it is your divine names which have been mentioned in them with finality. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) has used the verses *kadaiyin perum poruḷ* in the same context as he had used earlier in the verses *ōttin poruḷ mudivum ittanaiyē uttaman pēr ēttum* in the [39](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-39/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-39/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-39/).

**kadaiyin tirumoḻiyāy ninṟa tirumālē** – the knowledge mentioned in upaniśats also mentions words which identify you. Any word with meaning would reveal [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and pirātti. pirātti is word and emperumān is the meaning. Hasn’t viṣṇu purāṇam 1-8-18 said “*artō viṣṇu: iyam vāṇī* (viṣṇu is the meaning and she (pirātti) is the word)!

**unnai** – you, tirumāḷ, who have been established in *itihāsas* and *purāṇas*

**unnaip parumoḻiyāl kāṇap paṇi** – instead of seeing you with the help of these words, you should shower mercy on me so that I could say as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “*ahamannam ahamannam ahamannam*” (“I am food” repeated several times) in a loud voice and see you. You should grace me such that I would feel elated seeing you, praising you with words which do not have any fault, and enjoying you. Only when I am able to see you, could I praise you with words which are without faults. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) should grace him such that he is given divine eyes with which he would be able to see emperumān clearly. In the words *kadaiyin tirumoḻiyāy ninṟa*, the term *kadai*  could be construed to mean incidents taken from *itihāsas* and *purāṇas* instead of incidents taken from upaniśat.

We shall take up the 65<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 65. Paninden tirumeni

*paṇindēn tirumēni paingamalam kaiyāl aṇindēn un sēvadi mēl anbāy tuṇindēn purindētti unnaip pugalidam pārttu āngē irundētti vāḻum idu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumēni – (Oh tirumāl!) your divine form; paṇindēn – vorśipped; un sēvadi mēl – on your reddish divine feet; paim kamalam – beautiful lotus flowers; anbu āy – with affection; kaiyāl aṇindēn – offered with hands; unnai – you; purindu ētti – praising desirously; pugal idam pārttu – looking at your dwelling in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); āngē irundu ētti idu tuṇindēn – I ascertained to myself that staying there and praising you is the life that I should live

**Перевод:**

**paṇindēn tirumēni – āḻvārs prefer his divine form to the divine qualities which describe his svarūpam (basic nature). Hence, āḻvār says here that he vorśipped at the divine feet of emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “Is there anything new that I have to do for you? Haven’t you attained everything already?”. āḻvār thinking of the benefit that he has attained, responds “the benefit that I had obtained is the affection that I have for you”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**paṇindēn** – āḻvār says that he, who was similar to the nature of rāvaṇa when he had said as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyam*” (even if I am split into two, I will not fall at the feet (of śrī rāma)), after thinking about the beauty (of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form) fell at his divine feet.  Being a servitor, he changed the words in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 1 “*ponmēni kaṇḍēn*” (saw the divine golden form) to “*paṇindēn tirumēni* ” (vorśipped the divine form).

**paingamalam kaiyāl aṇindēn un sēvadi mēl anbāy** – with affection, I offered lotus flowers at your reddish divine feet and held on to them.

**kaiyāl** – since I submitted the flowers like this, I fulfilled the objective of having been given hands.

**aṇindēn** – Is it not proper for the losers to serve the victorious!

**anbāy paṇindēn** – vorśipping you like this is not because I obtained knowledge. It is because of my involvement with you.

**purindu ētti** – when you set out to go, I vorśipped you from the front; later, after you left, I vorśipped you from behind too and praised you.

**pugalidam pārttu** – when you went back inside, I saw the beauty of your backside.

**āngē irundu ētti** – without having the mind to leave you from there, seeing the beauty of your backside, I did not want to forget the thought of praising you at that place, and ascertained that my praising you should continue like this. I became firm in that belief. A person by name śrī paranguśa dāsar moved closely with [śrī tirumalai nambi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periya-thirumalai-nambi/) (one of the five ācāryars of [svāmy rāmānujar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)). He would quote śrī tirumalai nambi as follows: “In this pāsuram, the word *pugalidam* refers to śrīvaikuṇṭam to which emperumān returns after completing his incarnation on earth. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that as mentioned in [tiruppaḷḷāṇḍu 12](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-12-pallandu-enru/) ‘*sūḻndu irundu ēttuvar pallāṇḍē’ ,* he would praise [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) amidst nityamuktas (eternal dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam and those who reached śrīvikuṇṭam after getting liberated from samsāram) and live there”. As mentioned in paramasamhitai “*cāyā vā satvamanugaccēt*” (like shadow following any sentient being) it can be construed that āḻvār is saying that he would look at the servitorśip being carried to emperumān at śrīvaikuṇṭam and remain ecstatic there.

We shall move on to the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 66. Idu kanday

*idu kaṇḍāy nannenjē ippiṟaviyāvadu idu kaṇḍāy ellām nāmuṟṟadu idu kaṇḍāy nāraṇan pēr ōdi naragattarugaṇaiyā kāraṇamum vallaiyē kāṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nenjē – Oh good heart!; ip piṟavi āvadu idu kaṇḍāy – the cruelty of samsāram (materialistic realm) is like this, do observe; nām uṟṟadu ellām idu kaṇḍāy – the sorrows that we experienced (in this samsāram) are like these; nāraṇan – śrīman nārāyana’s; pēr – divine names; ōdi – reciting them well; naragattu – the hell called samsāram; arugu – near; aṇaiyā kāraṇamum – the reason for hating that we should not be here; idu kaṇḍāy – is only the fault of this samsāram, do observe; vallaiyēḷ – if you are capable of knowing; kāṇ – see for yourself (the lowliness of this samsāram)

**Перевод:**

**nal nenjē – Oh heart who agrees with me when I say that life in this world is terrible!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the benefit obtained out of engaging with matters relating to emperumān is great. But it is essential not to have desire with worldly pursuits, thinking of the ruins that one would end up with such acts. He says that the reason for attaining emperumān and for removing of hurdles in this path is not vorśipping emperumān but the ability to see the faults in worldly pleasures. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**idu kaṇḍāy ippiṟaviyāvadu** – The nature of this samsāram (materialistic realm) is not what we had thought of, earlier. It is that which has been shown to us by emperumān. Life on earth is the epitome of sorrow. All that we had thought of life on this earth as something great is mere delusion. Even faults in this life will appear to our eyes as faults, only with the grace of emperumān. Otherwise, they will appear to be without faults. Just as we cannot see emperumān unless he reveals himself to us, we cannot see the faults too in life unless he reveals them to us.

**idu ellām nām uṟṟadu** – just as there is only happiness in enjoying emperumān, there is only sorrow in life. Hasn’t this (samsāram) given to us only sorrow, in the garb of pleasure, for a very long time! Just as there is only happiness in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) with emperumān’s grace, there is only sorrow in samsāram. Has not [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) mercifully mentioned in [stōtra ratnam 25](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-25/) “*abhūtapūrvam mama bhāvi kim vā sarvam sahē mē sahajam hi du:kam*” (there is no sorrow that I have not experienced. I am tolerating all the sorrows. īsn’t sorrow born with me)! It appears that sorrows will be there as long as [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is there \[in this samsāram\]. Even though this ātmā belongs to emperumān, have you \[referring to his heart\] not seen all the sorrows that we experienced!

**idu kaṇḍāy nāraṇan pēr ōdi naragattu arugu aṇaiyāk kāraṇamum** – The reason for reciting the divine names of the apt Lord emperumān and for not going towards naragam (hell) is only seeing (knowing) the faults in the worldly life that I had mentioned earlier \[says the āḻvār to his heart\]. Just as emperumān enables us to attain him and relieves us from worldly pursuits, seeing the faults in worldly pursuits will enable us to relieve ourselves from these and to hold on to emperumān.

**vallaiyēl kāṇ** – Some people will lose their belongings even when they are awake. In the same way, even while we know about worldly pursuits, their faults will not dawn in our hearts. Hence, even you have to suffer, see clearly the nature of the worldly pursuits. Even as we are experiencing the sorrows, isn’t our sin the reason for not moving away from the worldly life!  In this matter, doesn’t even seeing with our eyes become useless! Just as we desire if we see something good, should we not become averse if we see something bad? The opinion here is that since attaining emperumān is not due to our efforts and since the involvement in worldly pursuits is our desire, what is needed for the jīvātmās is to see the faults in the worldly pursuits. ḍoesn’t śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-28 say “*mana Eva manuśyāṇām kāraṇam banda mōkśayō!*” (it is the person’s mind which is responsible for both bondage and liberation). When the cētana (sentient entity) gets involved in unnecessary pursuits, emperumān, who helps in attaining the necessary entity and avoiding the unnecessary, prevents the cētana from involving in the unnecessary and gives what he really needs. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) initially saw emperumān as the head of nityasūris (*imaiyōr talaivan*). This āḻvār says that he should help in releasing us from falsified knowledge, bad conduct and impure body, all of which are opposite of his being the lord of nityasūris.

We shall take up the 67<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 67. Kanden tirumeni

*kaṇḍēn tirumēni yān kanavil āngu avankaik kaṇḍēn kanalum sudar āḻi kaṇḍēn uṟunōy vinai iraṇḍum ōttuvittup pinnum maṟu nōy seṟuvān vali*

**Пословный перевод:**

yān – adiyēn (the servitor); kanavil – in the experience of the mind, similar to dream; tirumēni – the beautiful divine form; kaṇḍēn – vorśipped; āngu – at that time; avan kai – in his divine hand; kanalum sudar āḻi kaṇḍēn – I saw the divine, radiant disc, which was spitting fire; uṟunōy vinai iraṇḍum – the two deeds of vice and virtue, which are like a disease, fully united (with ātmā); ōttuvittu – getting rid; pinnum – beyond that; maṟu nōy seṟuvān – emperumān who gets rid of even the scent of these deeds which spring up like off-shoots; vali – strength; kaṇḍēn – I was able to see

**Перевод:**

**kaṇḍēn tirumēni – I had the fortune to see the divine form of emperumān. According to āḻvārs, enjoying the qualities of emperumān is something that comes on its own. But enjoying his divine form is very important.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Once āḻvār told his divine mind “Please see”, emperumān showed himself to āḻvār causelessly. āḻvār mercifully tells about the known entities among those that he saw. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**yān kanavil** – instead of seeing him through the sensory perceptions I saw him through my heart without any interruption. Instead of seeing him through my eyes which is a manifestation of the knowledge of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and which is part of the exterior sensory perceptions, I saw him through my mind, as if seeing in the dream. As mentioned in manusmruti 12-122 says “*svapna dī gamyam*” (he is to be perceived through the experience of mind, like a dream), I saw him beautifully through my heart, like a dream. It is opined here that since he was able to see him \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] extensively and since he was great, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) could not extricate himself from that vision.

**āngu avan kai** – in the divine hands of an entity who had such a beauty

**kaṇḍēn kanalum sudarāḻi** – I saw the divine disc which comes fiercely at the enemies, without any interruption.

**kaṇḍēn uṟu nōy iraṇḍum ōttuvittu pinnum maṟu nōy seṟuvān vali** – I saw him \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] who gets rid of *pāpa* (vice) and *puṇya* (virtue) which are stuck to the ever-lasting [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and who has the ability to prevent them from coming back again. *maṟu nōy* refers to the traces of one’s deeds which would return to the ātmā.

**vali kaṇḍēn** – being a [cētana (sentient entity)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) the only requirement for him is to know the nature of ātmā which is afraid, on thinking of samsāram and which loses that fear, on thinking of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

We shall consider the 68<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 68. Vali mikka

*vali mikka vāḷ eyiṟṟu vāḷavuṇar māḷa vali mikka vāḷ varai mattāga valimikka vāṇāgam suṟṟi maṟugak kadal kadaindān kōṇāgam kombu ositta kō*

**Пословный перевод:**

vali mikka – being mightily strong; vāḷ eyiṟu – having canine teeth resembling swords; vāḷ – having an army of swords; avuṇar – demons; māḷa – to be destroyed; vali mikka vāḷ varai mattāga – having mantara mountain, which is very strong and radiant, as the churning staff; vali mikka vāḷ nāgam – the serpent vāsuki, which is very strong and radiant; suṟṟi – using it as a rope around the churning staff; kadal maṟuga kadaindān – one who churned the ocean such that it became a slimey mass; kōḷ nāgam – the exulting elephant called as kuvalayāpīdam, which is very strong; kombu – its tusks; ositta – one who broke it (playfully); kō – the Lord

**Перевод:**

**vali mikka vāḷ avuṇar māḷa – making the demons, who are strong, who have radiant canine teeth and who have weapons, to get destroyed. In other worlds, emperumān made the strength of demons to get destroyed and that of dēvas (celestial entities) to grow.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān is such that if someone comes to pray to him asking for other benefits instead of himself, he cannot keep quiet; even if he has to strain himself, he will carry out tasks for his followers. Instead of getting rid of the scent of samsāram from them with the help of such an entity, the celestial people (dēvas) obtained nectar, which is like salt water, from him. Thus he speaks about the activities of emperumān by which he grants what each person wants from that persons’ position and how he avoids giving what is not required. āḻvār says that people lose him on their own \[by not asking for him\] and that there is no shortcoming in emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vali mikka vāḷ varai mattāga** – when the omnipotent emperumān himself churned the ocean, he used the *mantara* mountain which is very strong and which would not decay under the impact, and which is radiant. He made it very strong to use it as churning staff.

**vali mikka vāḷ nāgam suṟṟi** – when he used *mantara* mountain as a churning staff, he used the serpent *vāsuki*  as the rope for coiling around the staff since vāsuki would not rupture during the churning process, being very strong and was as radiant as the mountain. In order to ensure that vāsuki would not tear off during the churning process, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) gave it additional strength.

**maṟugak kadal kadaindān** – he churned the ocean such that the bottom layers of the ocean came on top. The ocean was agitated like curd is agitated in a vessel and suffered the same pain that the curd in the small vessel suffered.

Who churned the ocean like that?

**kōḷ nāgam kombu ositta kō** – one who is like an anchor for all of us. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the leader who broke the tusks of the elephant kuvalayāpīdam \[the royal elephant of King kamsan\] which came to harm him. He got rid of his enemy and made us as his servitors through the exhibition of such valour. After destroying his enemy and protecting himself, emperumān offered himself as the leader of the world. Through this act, he made us all as his servitors. Did not such an emperumān churn the ocean during that time, in the bargain suffering pain in his divine form!

We shall move on to the 69<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 69. Kovagi manilam kattu

*kōvāgi mānilam kāttu nangaṇ mugappē māvēgic celginṟa mannavarum pūmēvum sengalanābiyān sēvadikkē ēḻpiṟappum taṇ kamalam ēyndār tamar*

**Пословный перевод:**

kō āgi – being king of kings; mā nilam kāttu – ruling over the expansive earth; nam kaṇ mugappē – in front of our eyes; mā ēgi selginṟa – riding atop horses; mannavarum – kings; sem kamalappū mēvum – one with the reddish lotus flower adorning; nābiyān – emperumān with divine navel; sē adikkē – to the divine feet; ēḻ piṟappum – over many births; taṇ kamalam – cool lotus flowers; ēyndār – offered; tamar – followers

**Перевод:**

**kōvāgi – being the leaders of the world; since they are ruling the world, they will be very egoistic. They will think that they are also like emperumān and will be very conceited. They will be egoistic like pouṇḍra vāsudēvan, who thought that he was like emperumān. viṣṇu darmam 43-47 says “dēvēndras tribhuavanam artamēka pinga: sarvarditam tribhuvanagām ca kārtavīrya:, vaidēha: paramapadam prasādya viṣṇum samprāpta: sakalapalapradō hi viṣṇu:” (vorśipping viṣṇu, dēvendra obtained the three worlds, kubēra obtained wealth, kārtavīrya obtained greatness in the three worlds and janaka attained paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). īsn’t viṣṇu bhagavān the magnanimous person who gives what we ask of him!)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not only dēvas who approach emperumān and get their needs fulfilled, but even the kings, who are seen by us protecting the world, approach the divine feet of emperumān who is the cause for the world and these kings obtain their positions through emperumān only. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mānilam kāttu** – protecting the expansive earth

**nam kaṇ mugappē mā ēgic celginṟa mannavarum** – the kings who mount elephants, horses etc even as we are seeing them

**pūvēgum sengamala nābiyān sēvadikkē** – other flowers, when they look at the beauty of the lotus flower shooting from the divine navel of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), being unable to compete with that, lose out and get pushed over. On the divine feet of such emperumān who has such a divine navel with beautiful lotus flower…

**pū mēvum** – sweet lotus; if the text is taken as **pū ēgum** the meaning will change to: the divine navel of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/),  like a lotus, never changing  its beauty. Since the other flowers will lose their beauty in a moment, they cannot compete with emperumān’s divine lotus like navel and will get pushed over.

**ēḻ piṟappum taṇ kamalam ēyndār tamar** – they (the kings) would have offered cool flowers at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) throughout their multitudinous births. Alternatively, this can be construed as: they would be the followers of those who had offered cool flowers at the divine feet of emperumān over many births. Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that those who had become followers of emperumān have become kings in this birth, going atop horses.

We shall move on to the 70<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
