## 70. Tamarullam tanjai

*tamaruḷḷam tanjai talaiyarangam taṇkāl tamaruḷḷum taṇ poruppu vēlai tamaruḷḷum māmallai kōval madiḷ kudandai enbarē ēvalla endaikku idam*

**Пословный перевод:**

tamar uḷḷam – devotees’ heart; tanjai – tanjai māmaṇik kōyil \[a divine abode in tanjāvūr\]; talai arangam – (among all divine places) most special tiruvarangam; taṇkāl – tiruttaṇkāl \[a divine abode near present day sivakāsi\]; tamar uḷḷum – what the followers have thought of (as everything for them); taṇ poruppu – the cool tirumalai (tiruvēngadam); vēlai – tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); tamar uḷḷum – places meditated upon by followers; māmallai – tirukkadal mallai \[mahābalipuram\]; kōval – tirukkōvalūr; madiḷ kudandai – kudandai \[kumbakōṇam\] with divine fortified walls; E valla endaikku idam enbar – \[his followers\] will say are the residences for cakravarty tirumagan (śrī rāma) who is an expert at shooting arrows

**Перевод:**

**tamar uḷḷam – āḻvār is referring first to the hearts of followers since these are the most important among all the abodes where emperumān has taken residence. Thus, āḻvār says that unlike other divine abodes, emperumān will give prominence to his followers’ hearts.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that if such people are favourable to him (emperumān) he will not see any distinction between their hearts and the divine abodes where he has taken residence with happiness. Alternatively it could be said that āḻvār is mercifully mentioning the abodes where emperumān has taken residence for the sake of his followers. This pāsuram describes the infatuation that emperumān has for his followers. Thus, in the 68th pāsuram, the way he gave nectar to those who desired it was mentioned. In the 69th pāsuram, the way he gave kingdoms to those who desired them was mentioned. In this pāsuram, the way he gave himself to those who desired him is mentioned. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tanjai** – the divine abode tanjai māmaṇikkōyil, in tanjāvūr.

**talai arangam** –  the periyakōyil (huge temple) at tiruvarangam, considered to be the first among all divine abodes of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and one that could be thought of to be on a par with paramapadam. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) has mercifully spoken of this abode in his tirunedundāṇḍagam 14 as “*appāl mudalāy ninṟa aḷappariya āramudai arangamēya andaṇanai*” (one who is in paramapadam took residence in tiruvarangam, as the primary cause of existence for both nityavibhūti (śrivaikuṇṭam) and leelā vibhūti (samsāram) with his wonderful form which will remove all faults); nammāḻvār too has mercifully mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 7-2-3 as “*katkilee … titkodi madiḷ sūḻ tiruvarangattāy*” (Oh one who is invisible to our eyes! …who lies inside tiruvarangam surrounded by huge walls with firm creepers on them).

**taṇkāl** – this divine abode of *tiruttaṇkāl* is the one which removed [tirumangai āḻvār’](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)s infatuation. tirumagai āḻvār has mercifully spoken of this abode in his periya tirumoḻi as “*taṇkāl tiṟalvaliyai*” (the powerful entity at tiruttaṇkāl).

**tamar uḷḷum taṇ poruppu** –   tiruvēngdamalai (the hills at tiruvēngadam) which is considered by followers as a treasure and which is spoken of by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in tiruvāimoḻi 9-3-8 as “*ninṟa vēngadam nīṇilattu uḷḷadu*” (the abode tiruvēngadam where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) stood awaiting his followers is on the expansive earth). This abode is everything to śrīvaiṣṇavas.

**vēlai** – the abode tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) which is the cause for emperumān to take residence in all these divyadēśams (divine abodes). Since this abode is approached only by entities such as brahmā et al, āḻvār is not qualifying this abode with any specific adjective and mentions it merely as *vēlai*.

**tamar uḷḷum māmallai** – the abode  tirukkadal mallai \[present day mahābalipuram\] where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took a lying posture on the ground in order to shower his mercy on his follower puṇḍarīka muni.

**kōval** – the divine place tirukkōvalūr where emperumān jostled with [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/), [bhūdattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) and [pēyāḻvār](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/thiruvaimozhi-5-10-3-peyyum-punguzhal/). īsn’t it the place which exulted after making the three of them jostle with each other!

**madiḷ kudandai** – the divine abode tirukkudandai \[present day kumbakōṇam\] which is surrounded by well fortified walls. An abode which was very dear to [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/).

**enbarē ēvalla endaikku idam** – people say that these are the abodes which are the dwelling places for cakravarty tirumagan (son of emperor) \[śrī rāma\] who is capable of uprooting the enemies. He is the one who destroyed his enemies and made his followers as his servitors. āḻvār says that these are all abodes where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took residence in order to redeem his followers from samsāram.

We shall take up the 71<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 71. Idangai valampuri

*idangai valampuri ninṟārppa erigānṟu adangār odunguvittadu āḻi vidangālum tīvāy aravaṇaimēl tōnṟal disaiyaḷappān pūvār adi nimirtta pōdu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vidam kālum – spitting poison; tī vāy – having frightening mouth; aravu – anantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); aṇai mēl – (reclining) on his bed; tōnṟal – the supreme being; disai – all the worlds; aḷappān – to measure; pū Ar adi – the soft divine feet, which are like flowers; nimirttapōdu – during the time when \[emperumān\] lifted and made them grow; valampuri – his conch; idangai – on his left hand; ninṟu – standing firmly; ārppa – blew vociferously; āḻi – divine disc \[sudarśana\]; eri kānṟu – spat fire; adangār – enemies such as namuci; odunguvittadu – made them incapacitated

**Перевод:**

**idangai valampuri ninṟārppa – In order to express its happiness when emperumān measured the worlds, the conch pāncajanya, on his left hand, blew loudly. It blew in such a way that enemies, who heard it, fell onto the ground in defeat.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram, how nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam), thinking of the activities undertaken by emperumān during his avatārams (incarnations), fear for his safety out of affection for him and suffer, is told. īsn’t emperumān, who is having many people who have deep affection for him, taking residence in places where there is none having affection for him, wonders āḻvār. In the earlier pāsuram, it was mentioned as to how emperumān came of his own volition to various divine abodes. This pāsuram mentions the nature of nityasūris who desire only emperumān. Even if emperumān incarnates here, only nityasūris have affection for him and there is none in this world who has affection for him. āḻvār is asking as to for whose knowledge the nityasūris are remaining there, having affection for emperumān. In the earlier pāsuram, he mentioned as to how he comes as a simple person to various divine abodes. In this pāsuram he mentions emperumān’s nature as an easily approachable person. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**erikānṟu adangār odunguvittadu āḻi** – the divine disc, not being able to blow like the conch, spat fire on enemies such as namuci (son of king mahābali) and frightened them.

**vidam kālum tīvāy aravaṇai mēl tōnṟal** – even as emperumān is reclining on him, due to his affection for emperumān, tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) wants to kill the enemies. Accordingly, in the same position (being a bed for emperumān) he spat poison. emperumān has such a soft body that his followers \[nityasūris\] fear as to what will befall his divine form, even as he is lying on tiruvanantāḻvān who has such a frightening mouth.

**disai aḷappān** – to measure the directions which had forests and ridges

**pū Ar adi nimirtta pōdu** – when he spread out his soft, divine legs. At that time, the divine conch in his left had blew loudly; the divine disc on his right had spat fire; ādiśēhan spat our poison from his place. Thus all these followers suffered like this.

**pū Ar adi** – even if he had remained quiet without measuring the worlds, his divine feet were so soft that even his consorts would worry themselves out of affection for him, as to what will happen to his divine fieet. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too mercifully stated in his tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-10 “*malarmagaḷ maṟṟum nilamagaḷ pidikkum melladi*” (both śrīdēvi and bhūdēvi would gently stroke his soft, divine feet). His divine feet were so soft that even śrīdēvi, who is fragrance personified and bhūdēvi, who is patience personified, would fear touching them! The divine feet are so soft that they would turn red even if his consorts think about stroking them, even before they touch them! They are like flowers which have been spread out; they are so soft.

We shall move on to the 72<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 72. Podarindu

*pōdaṟindu vānarangaḷ pūnjunai pukku āngalarnda pōdarindu koṇḍu ēttumbōdu uḷḷam pōdu maṇivēngadavan malar adikkē sella aṇivēngadavan pēr āyndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vānarangaḷ – monkeys; pōdu aṟindu – waking up early in the morning (and getting up); pū sunai pukku – entering ponds with flowers (and having a bath); āngu alarnda – just then blossomed; pōdu – flowers; arindu koṇḍu – plucking and offering them; ēttum – will worship; uḷḷam – Oh mind!; pōdu – you too start (to carry out like that); maṇi vēngadavan – tiruvēngadavan who is like a blue coloured gem; pēr – divine names; āyndu – meditating on; vēngadavan malar adikkē sella – ensuring that they reach the lotus-like divine feet of; pōdu aṇi – offer the flowers

**Перевод:**

**pōdu aṟindu – just as sages get rid of their rajō guṇam (being passionate) and tamō guṇam (being ignorant) and have only sātvika guṇam (being noble) knowing that it is brahma muhūrtam (the time span between 4 and 6 in the morning), the monkeys also become aware of the time and do not get sleep during this time frame, like ēkāgis (solitary people).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār’s divine mind took a step back saying that if this is the position of the great nityasūris with regard to emperumān, do I have the qualification to approach him? āḻvār tells his divine mind “you need not fear like that. emperumān is such a person of easy access that even animals approach him. Hence stay focussed on that”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vānarangaḷ** – monkeys worship at the tirumalai hills where nityasūris come and worship with affection.

**pūnjunai pukku** – entering ponds which have flowers. Just as sages take a dip in the water unmindful of the cillness, the monkeys too have a bath.

**āngu** – in that place

**alarnda pōdu** – beautiful flowers which are neither buds nor fully blossomed.

**arindu** – plucking them, they will worship. They will approach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with these.

**uḷḷam pōdu** – Oh my mind! Step out! Do not step back in the time available.

What are we going to do? You also, like those monkeys, with beautiful flowers …..

**maṇi vēngadavan malaradikkē sella** – ensuring that the flowers reach the beautiful divine feet of tiruvēngadavan who has the complexion of a blue gem

**aṇi** – while doing that

**vēngadavan pēr āyndu koṇḍu** – meditating on the divine names of tiruvēngadavan, wondering at his simplicity, we shall ensure that we offer the flowers to his divine feet. We shall surrender to him with flowers.

If the text is taken as *aṇi vēngadavan* instead of *maṇi vēngadavan*, the meaning will change to: tiruvēngadamudaiyān who is like a decorative jewel to the world. We can also interpret this as – even when going towards tiruvēngadavan’s flower-like divine feet, offer (offer such that they reach tirvēngadavan’s divine feet).

We shall take up the 73<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 73. Aynduraippan

*āynduraippan āyiram pēr ādi nadu andivāy vāynda malar tūvi vaigalum  ēynda piṟaikkōttuc cengaṇ kari vidutta pemmān iṟakkādpadat tuṇinda yān*

**Пословный перевод:**

piṟai ēynda kōdu – having crescent shaped tusks; sem kān – having reddish eyes; kari – the elephant gajēndrāḻvān; vidutta – one who released it from the jaws of crocodile; pemmān – the supreme being; iṟaikku – to śrīman nārāyaṇa; āḷ pada – to be his servitor; tuṇinda yān – I became firm; ādi nadu andivāy vaigalum – during all times in the morning, noon and night; vāynda malar – flowers that I could get my hands on; tūvi – strew them haphazardly; āyiram pēr – the thousand divine names; āyndu uraippan – I will meditate in my heart

**Перевод:**

**āyndu uraippan – I will be constantly meditating on his divine names**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully states in this pāsuram that thinking about how emperumān remains attainable by everyone, he became sure that he will carry out kainkaryam permanently at his divine feet. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āyiram pēr** – When meditating on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names, we would not have any rule that we will recite only such and such divine names and will leave out such and such divine names. We will recite all the divine names. Should one look for a proper time and place for mentioning one’s mother’s name? Only for those who desire only wealth are there rules that only such and such divine names are to be recited. For those who are not looking for any such benefit, there is no such rule. Just as one will swallow a chip of candy that one gets to lay his hands on, we will recite the divine names.

**ādi nadu andi vāy** – during beginning, middle and end periods of the day. There is no rule with regard to time for reciting the divine names of emperumān. One can recite during morning, noon and night, at all times.

**vāynda malar** – even among flowers, there is no rule about which flowers are to be offered to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). With any flower that one could get his hands on.

**tūvi** – there is no rule in the way the flowers are offered. Scattering them without following any procedure

**vaigalum** – we will attain him at all times. Just as we eat everyday, we will recite his names everyday.

**ēynda piṟaikkōttuc cengaṇ kari vidutta pemmān** – elephant with crescent shaped tusks and reddish eyes. The elephant could be considered as either referring to kuvalayāpīdam which was incited by kamsa to kill kriṣṇa or to śrī gajēndrāḻvān which was protected by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) from crocodile. If it refers to kuvalayāpīdam, we can construe it as referring to emperumān removing the hurdles for his followers. If it refers to śrī gajēndrāḻvān, we can construe it as referring to emperumān’s affection towards those who approach him.

**kari viduttu** – unable to let go of the one flower that gajēndran wanted to offer, releasing that gajēndran from the jaws of crocodile.

**pemmān** – to my svāmy (master); to my Lord who is the supreme entity. The knowledge that he is a servitor to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) gives the confidence to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) to attain him.

**iṟaikku** – emperumān, the ordained lord.

**ātpadat tuṇinda yān** – I, who became sure that I will carry out service to emperumān; I who became sure in the servitorśip which is the benefit of attaining him. āḻvār says that he carried out service, as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) removed the hurdles; I who became sure that I will carry out service to emperumān who is capable of destroying enemies.

We shall take up the 74<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 74. Yane tavam seyden

*yānē tavam seydēn ēḻpiṟappum eppoḻudum yānē tavam udaiyēn emperumān yānē irum tamiḻ nanmālai iṇaiyadikkē sonnēn perum tamiḻan nallēn peridu*

**Пословный перевод:**

emperumān – Oh my svāmy (Lord)!; ēḻ piṟappum – in all births; eppoḻudum – in all states; tavam seydēn – carried out penance; yānē – only I; tavam udaiyan yānē – it is I who reaped the benefit of carrying out that penance; irum tamiḻ nal mālai – beautiful garlands of words strung in great tamiḻ; iṇai adikkē – (to your) two divine feet; sonnēn – I offered; perum tamiḻan – being an expert in great tamiḻ arts; peridu – to a great extent; nallēn – being great; yānē – only I

**Перевод:**

**yānē tavam seydēn – only I had carried out penance. This is the difference between me and the people of this world.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that with the happiness derived out of carrying out kainkaryam for emperumān,  he was blessed to be qualified to sing paeans on him. Just as nammāḻvār mercifully spoke in tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-9 “vānavar kōnaik kavi solla vallēṟku ini māṟuṇḍē” (is there any enemy for one who is capable of singing paeans on the lord of nityasūris?), bhūdattāḻvār also mercifully speaks in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ēḻ piṟappum eppoḻudum** – It was I who carried out the penance, the benefit of which was to sing paeans about attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), in all births and in all states. sītāppirātti said  “*īdruśam puṇyapāpam*” (puṇyapāpam (virtue and vice) are like this) – pirātti said that there is reason for her unlimited sorrow. The sin (vice) for her is śrī rāma’s anger. In the same way, this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that there is a reason for the benefit that accrued to him (in other words, he says that the reason is emperumān’s mercy). He affirms the reason based on the benefit that he obtained. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully stated in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-6-madhusudhanai-anri/) “*enakkē aruḷgaḷ seyya vidi sūḻndadāl*” (emperumān had to shower his mercy only for my sake).

**yānē tavam udaiyēn** – it was I who attained the benefit of carrying out this penance.

**emperumān**  – the reason for this is that he is my Lord. Hence, both his penance and the benefit are [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) only. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in his tirumoḻi 6-3-4 calls emperumān as “*pōndēn puṇṇiyanē*”, saying that emperumān is his virtue. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) asks whether there is anything for him to lose after emperumān becomes his Lord. Only I have both the penance and the benefit of that penance, says the āḻvār. The reason for this is that ….

**yānē irundamiḻ nanmālai** – there is a major difference between the garland of vēda (sacred text) and the garland of tamiḻ words \[divya prabandam\] that āḻvār had strung. The garland of vēda could be learnt only by limited people \[male members in the first three varṇas) while the garland of tamiḻ that āḻvār had strung could be learnt by everyone.

**yānē tavam uadiyēn yānē irum tamiḻ nanmālai iṇai adikkē sonnēn** –  only I had carried out the penance. As a result of that penance, only I had the fortune of offering to your divine feet, a great tamiḻ garland, made with superior words without any fault in either the grammar or the poetic metrics, and which could be learnt by everyone.

**perum tamiḻan** – there is none who has immersed in the drāvida śāstram (the sacred text in tamiḻ) as much as I have. Is it only that there is none to equal me in this?

**peridu nallēn** – it is not because of egoistic bent of mind that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is calling himself as great. Just as nammāḻvār had sung in tiruvāimoḻi 7-9-4 “*ennāgiyē tappudalinṟit tanaik kavi tān solli*”, āḻvār says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), through āḻvār, composed paeans without any faults, on himself.

**nallēn peridu** –Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-5-8-namakkum/) “*yāvar nigar agal vānattē*”, this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that there is none who is his equal. He says “nityamuktars (the permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam and those who reached śrīvaikuṇṭam after being liberated from samsāram) will worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) without any procedure and I too vorśipped without following any rule or order. But that my prabandam is without any fault is my special greatness over them (nityamuktars).  They are vorśipping him from the apt place of paramapadam singing, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “*ētat sāmagāyannāstē*” (singing sāma vēdam like this) whereas I am vorśipping him from the inapt samsāram (this materialistic world); hence I am greater than they”.

We shall move on to the 75<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 75. Perugu mada velam

*perugu mada vēḻam māppidikku mun ninṟu iru kaṇ iḷa mūngil vāngi arugirunda tēn kalandu nīttum tiruvēngadam kaṇḍīr vān kalanda vaṇṇan varai*

**Пословный перевод:**

perugum – flowing copiously; madam – the liquid that comes out during periods of exultation; vēḻam – (male) elephant; mā pidikku mun ninṟu – standing in front of its great female elephant; iru kaṇ iḷa mūngil vāngi – plucking a bamboo sprout with two nodes; arugu irunda tēn kalandu – dipping (that bamboo sprout) in honey available nearby; nīttum – when it offered (to the female elephant, with its trunk); tiruvēngadam kaṇḍīr – is that not tiruvēngadam!; vān kalanda vaṇṇan varai – it is the hill which is the residence for emperumān who has the complexion of cloud

**Перевод:**

**perugum mada vēḻam – elephant which secretes the liquid during its period of exultation, in overflowing manner like a river. Unlike the way periyāḻvār mentioned in his tirumoḻi 1-7-1 “mummadappunal” instead of secreting from three places in its body, the liquid was flowing from various places, like a flood, for this elephant, by looking at which even the lions will be scared. When the flow of liquid stops, the elephant will be able to gain some sense. But when it is in exultation, the elephant cannot be controlled in any way.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār said that he is great, emperumān asked him to sing a poem on him and this pāsuram is the poem that āḻvār sang then. Just as śrī rāmāyanam came out of vālmīki’s poems, details about tiruvēngadam come out of this āḻvār’s poem. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māppidikku mun ninṟu** – it will stand in front of of its proud female elephant. The pride for the female elephant is that it is able to control the male elephant which is otherwise uncontrollable when it is in exultation.

**māppidi** – the female elephant is capable of making the male elephant to starve for several days for its sake.

**mun ninṟu** – Just as lakśmaṇa told śrī rāma in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 15-7 “*kriyatāmiti mām vada*” (please command me that this \[activity\] should be carried out), the male elephant, when in exultation, would stand humbly before the female elephant. It would carry out any task at the command of the female elephant. All the activities of the male elephant would be under the control of the female. Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who controls everyone, was under the control of pāṇḍavas (the five sons of king pāṇḍu), the male elephant would be under the control of its female.

**māppidikku mun ninṟu** – the reason for cētana not being afraid of emperumān’s *svātantriyam* (independence) is pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi). While his *svātantriyam* in itself is the cause for his forgiving  and accepting us, the real reason for this is pirātti’s *puruśakāram* (recommendatory role). śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam 30-39 in āraṇya kāṇḍam says “*tam druśtvā śatruhantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham \| babhūva hruśtā vaidēhi bhartāram pariśasvajē \|\|*” (after seeing śrī rāmapirān who destroyed the enemies and comforted the sages, sītā realised that she existed and embraced her husband tightly).

**iru kaṇ iḷa mūngil vāngi** – in the same way, the female elephant is present here. Even though the male elephant is not under its own control, it would strive to keep the female elephant happy. It will pluck a bamboo sprout with two nodes and offer it to the female.

**iru kaṇ** – Even if the bamboo is so long that it will reach until the nether world, it has only two nodes.

**vāngi** – Even though the male elephant is so strong that it can bring down a mountain, when it comes to doing anything for its female, it will be very gentle with whatever it handles for the female. This verse refers to the gentleness of the bamboo and the care exhibited by the male elephant. It handled the bamboo shoot as if it were butter. The male will think that even if there is any shortcoming in its greatness, the female will not accept it.

**arugirunda tēn kalandu** – just as inside the sannidi (sanctum sanctorum) of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), various materials used in emperumān’s *tirumanjanam* (divine bath) will be kept in their places without any fault, there too (in the forest) sweet materials such as honey will be available at various places without any shortcoming. Honey will be present inside the mountainous caves. The male elephant will dip the bamboo shoot in that honey and offer it to the female. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) has used the word *kalandu* (mixed) this implies that bamboo and honey will be like two liquid materials \[such soft bamboo\].

**nīttum** – even if the female turns its face away not accepting it from the male, the male will continue to offer it. It will consider its offering \[the bamboo\] itself as the benefit. The female will be indifferent to its offering, saying “give it to those you like” and the male will beg the female to accept it.

**nīttum** – just as śrī rāma offered to sītā as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 96-2 “*idam mēdyam idam svādu*” (this is pure; this is sweet), the male elephant will offer \[the honey dipped bamboo shoot\] to the female.

**tiruvēngadam kaṇḍīr** – isn’t it such tirumalai!

**vān kalanda vaṇṇan varai** – it is the mountain of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is having the complexion of cloud. Just as the bamboo shoot and honey mixed well together with the same taste, the colour of cloud and that of emperumān are the same here. It will be difficult to distinguish between the colour of cloud and the form of emperumān.  Both the subject and the object of comparison are equal in all aspects.

We shall take up the 76<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 76. Varaic candana kulambum

*varaic candanak kuḻambum vān kalanum pattum viraippolinda veṇ malligaiyum niraittukkoṇḍu ādikkaṇ ninṟa aṟivan adiyiṇaiyē ōdip paṇivadu uṟum*

**Пословный перевод:**

varaic candanak kuḻambum – sandalwood paste made from sandalwood trees in mountains; vān kalanum – great jewels; pattum – silken clothes; virai polinda – full of fragrance; veṇ malligaiyum – garlands of white jasmine flower; niraittukkoṇḍu – gathering these materials; ādikkaṇ ninṟa aṟivan – the omniscient emperumān who has been there from time immemorial as the causative factor for the worlds; adi iṇaiyē – the two divine feet; ōdi – praising him handsomely with the mouth; paṇivadu – bowing down to him with the head; uṟum – will be apt (for the nature of cētana, the sentient entity)

**Перевод:**

**varaic candanak kuḻambum – sandal paste taken from a good place (mountain). Sandal paste which has no shortage in fragrance. tirumangai āḻvār, in his periya tirumadal, has mercifully stated “tennan podiyil seḻum sandanak kuḻambum” (the high quality sandal paste prepared from the sandalwood trees in the podiyamalai range of mountains in the kingdom of pāṇḍiyas).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that just as the song was sweet for him, collecting the materials for his worship and falling at the divine feet of emperumān, who has taken residence near us and who is the ordained Lord for us, is the act which will bring benefit to the ātmā. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vān kalanum** – highly valued, divine jewels which are apt for emperumān. nammāḻvār too has mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-2-pusum-sandhu/) “*tēsamāna aṇikalanum*” (radiant jewels).

**pattum** – good silken clothes suitable for emperumān’s divine waist and appropriate to his softness.

**viraip polinda veṇ malligaiyum** – jasmine flower, full of fragrance and being white in colour; being sweet to merely look at, not even needing to be worn.

**niṟaittukkoṇḍu** – all these materials \[mentioned thus far\] should be such that even as emperumān sees them, they create an involvement for him towards us. [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/), considering himself as yaśōdāppirātti, says in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-4-4 “*anjanamum koṇḍu vaittēn*” (I kept the decorative black coloured pigment for applying to the eyes), with lot of affection. We should also exhibit similar affection for emperumān when taking the materials \[mentioned earlier for vorśipping him\]. Taking the materials in the order in which they should be taken, as mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in tiruviruttam 21 “*sūttu nanmālaigaḷ tūyana ēndi*” (carrying the beautiful, pure, garlands appropriate for emperumān).

**ādikkaṇ ninṟa** – emperumān who has been standing from time immemorial \[waiting for the ātmās to reach him\]. emperumān was existing even when the ātmās did not have a physical form or sensory perceptions \[during deluge\]. Before that, being the causative factor for all these ātmās. When asked which entity should be vorśipped, upaniśat said “*kāraṇam tu dyēya:*” (meditate on the that entity who is the cause for everything).

**aṟivan** – the omniscient. If the cētana does a favourable act towards emperumān who is apt to be vorśipped, he will always remember that, being the omniscient, as he himself mentioned in mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 47-22 “*gōvindēti yadāgrandat kriṣṇā mām dūravāsinam ruṇam pravruddamiva mē hrudayānnāpasarpayati*” (when draupadi called out to me from a far away distance, it did not leave my heart, similar to the interest on interest for a loan given). He will wonder whether the cētana has to do anything more towards him.

**adiyiṇaiyē** – his two divine feet which are comparable only to each other.

**ōdip paṇivadu uṟum** – it is apt to praise and worship him. āḻvār says that praising emperumān with the mouth and bowing with the head would be apt for this ātmā.

**uṟum** – this \[praising and vorśipping\] would be of a very high order, be sweet at that moment and forever too. āḻvār says that praising and vorśipping emperumān would be similar to being offered sugar candy as a wage for eating a piece of jaggery \[adding sweet to sweetness\]. He says that to worship emperumān who is *aprākrutam* (extraordinary; not related to materialistic world) one need not take *aprākruta* materials. Materials from this world such as sandal paste, jewels, silk cloth, jasmine etc are sufficient.

We shall take up the 77<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 77. Urum kanday

*uṟum kaṇḍāy nannenjē uttaman naṟpādam uṟum kaṇḍāy oṇkamalam tannāl uṟum kaṇḍāy ēttip paṇindavan pēr īraijñūṟu eppoḻudum sāṟṟi uraittal tavam*

**Пословный перевод:**

nannenjē – Oh good heart!; uttaman – the puruśōttaman \[emperumān\]; nal pādam – the great divine feet; (paṇivadu) – to attain; uṟum kaṇḍāy – it is very apt, see for yourself; oṇ kamalam tannāl – with beautiful lotus flowers; (sāṟṟip paṇivadu) – to decorate and to pay obeisance; uṟum kaṇḍāy – is very apt for us, see for yourself; sāṟṟi – offering (those lotus flowers with our hands); paṇindu – bowing (with our heads); ētti – praising him handsomely with the mouth; avan pēr īraijñūṟu – his thousand divine names; eppoḻudum – at all times; uraittal – the act of reciting them; tavam – penance; uṟum kaṇḍāy – is apt, see for yourself

**Перевод:**

**uṟum kaṇḍāy – the word uṟudum has been shortened as uṟum, throughout this verse. We can construe the meaning for this as taking the materials mentioned in the previous pāsuram (76) and attaining him. Alternatively, this could be construed as āḻvār telling his heart to think of this (being explained in subsequent words) as a great act, instead of treating it similar to other benefits.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since āḻvār who thought of attaining emperumān in the proper way, found that the way was very nice, tells his heart mercifully that more than getting the experience born out of attaining emperumān, the  experience obtained in observing the activity for attaining him is in itself sufficient for him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**uṟum kaṇḍāy** – for one who has been used to looking for lowly benefits, attaining the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the most apt act \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) tells his heart\]. You know very well how we had suffered in lowly things from time immemorial.

**nannenjē** – Oh heart, who is suitable for me! Alternatively, it can be taken as the heart which comes ahead of āḻvār, with excitement, with lot of involvement in this matter.

**uttaman** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who considers the form, that he takes to protect us, as being for his own benefit. Even during the time when we shunned him, he came behind us.

**naṟpādam** – His divine feet are superior to him to the same extent by which he is superior to us. [pēyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/), in his mūnṟām tiruvandādi 88 has mercifully stated “*podu ninṟa ponnangaḻal*” (his divine, golden, beautiful feet are there for all of us in common). As prayed by kāḷiyan (the serpent which troubled people and cattle in yamunā nadi during kriṣṇa’s time in gōkulam), as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-7-10 “*sōham tē dēvadēvēśa nārcanātau stutau na ca sāmartyavān krupāmātra manōvrutti: prasīda mē*” (Oh Lord of dēvas! I am not proficient in praising and vorśipping you. For such a person as I am, only you have to shower mercy on me and grace me with your divine mind), the divine feet come on their own and rest on our heads.

**uttaman naṟpādam uṟum kaṇḍāy oṇkamalam tannāl** – one can attain his divine feet with a beautiful lotus flower. Thus we do not have to carry out any great deed. There is no need to look for an *aprākruta* (extraordinary) flower which is apt for him. A normal lotus flower will do.

**uṟum kaṇḍāy ēttip paṇindu avan pēr īraijñūṟu eppoḻudum sāṟṟi uraittal tavam** – the penance of vorśipping him and reciting his thousand divine names always is apt. This implies that more than attaining him, the activity undertaken to attain him is very sweet. The period spent in trying to attain *puruśārtam* (the benefit) is sweeter than the period when we attain *puruśārtam.* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) tells his heart to see for itself the benefit of reciting emperumān’s divine names which is similar to carrying out penance. Would reciting his divine names be a penance? Since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) considers this as penance, in empeurmān’s opinion this will be taken as carrying out penance.

We shall move on to the 78<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 78. Tavam seydu

*tavam seydu nānmuganē peṟṟān taraṇi nivandaḷippa nīttiya poṟpādam sivanda tan kai anaittum ārak kaḻuvinān gangai nīr peydu anaittup pēr moḻinda pin*

**Пословный перевод:**

taraṇi – the earth; aḷappa nivandu – growing up in size as trivikrama to measure; nīttiya – (the divine foot that) he raised; poṟpādam – the great divine foot; gangai nīr peydu – carrying out service by pouring gangā water which was actually darmam (all the righteousness)\[ which melted upon seeing emperumān’s divine foot\]; anaittu pēr moḻindu – reciting (emperumān’s) all the divine names wholeheartedly; pin – later; than sivanda kai anaittum Ara – to make his beautiful hands become benefitted; kaḻuvinān – one who washed the divine foot; nānmuganē – it is only nānmugan; tavam seyudu peṟṟān – who reaped the benefit out of carrying out the penance of reciting the divine names of emperumān

**Перевод:**

**tavam seydu nānmuganē peṟṟān – even though many persons have carried out penance, they did not get the fruits of their penance. Only brahmā reaped the benefit of the penance that he had undertaken. emperumān considered as penance the sweet activity of brahmā reciting emperumān’s divine names. nammāḻvār too has divinely mentioned in tiruvāymoḻi 1-3-5 “vaṇakkudait tavaneṟi” (emperumān considers the act of vorśipping too as a penance)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār exclaims that it was only nānmugan (brahmā) who carried out penance like this and effortlessly attained the divine feet of emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**taraṇi nivandaḷappa** – the activity of emperumān to help someone else (indra) became the fruit of brahmā’s penance. The activity carried out for someone ended up as a benefit for somebody else.

**nanmuganē peṟṟān** – is there anyone who benefitted as much as brahmā got, by carrying out penance?

**nīttiya poṟpādam** – the great divine feet raised in order to measure the earth. āḻvār says that while brahmā stayed in satyalōkam, out of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s reach, it fructified for him (did not emperumān lift his divine foot up to the place where brahmā stayed!).

**sivanda than kai anaittum ārak kaḻuvinān** – brahmā washed the divine feet such that his reddish hands became satisfied. He obtained the benefit of having had many hands.

**kai anaittum Ara** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that brahmā washed till the hands became satisfied just as one says that a person ate till his stomach became satisfied.

**gangai nīr peydu** – dharma (righteousness) filled up brahmā’s *kamaṇḍala* (a pitcher used by ascetics) even as brahmā thought of washing the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Just as īśvara samhitai says “*gruhītvā darmapānīyam*” (taking the water of righteousness), taking the water which formed out of dharma.

**anaittup pēr moḻindu** – by reciting all the divine names of emperumān, brahmā attained the benefit of having been provided with a mouth.

**pin** – after carrying out such an act, brahmā attained the benefit of that penance. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that it is only brahmā who reaped such a benefit.

We can also sequence the above by saying that righteousness transformed into gangā; then brahmā recited all the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/); then he washed the divine feet of emperumān till his reddish hands felt satiated; then brahmā obtained the benefit of carrying out this penance.

We shall take up the 79<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 79. Pin ninru

*pinninṟu tāyirappak kēḷān perumbaṇaittōḷ munninṟu tānirappāḷ moymmalarāl sol ninṟa tōḷ nalandān nēr illāt tōnṟal avan aḷanda nīḷ nalandān attanaikkum nēr*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāy – the mother, kausalyā; pin ninṟu – following \[śrī rāma\]; irappa – praying (not to go to forest, leaving her); kēḷān – not heeding to that; perum paṇai tōḷ – one who is having divine shoulders similar to great bamboo; moy malarāḷ tān – sītā, who is personification of mahālakśmi, who in turn was born in a lotus flower; mun ninṟu – taking the lead (to go to forest); irappāḷ – on praying (to take her too to the forest); sol ninṟa – one who acted as per the word (of śrī rāmāyaṇam); tōḷ nalam tān – in the matter of the strength of shoulder; nēr illā tōnṟal – one who has none comparable, śrī rāma pirān; attanaikkum – for that activity (of going to the forest not looking at the ills of the forest and boldly going there); avan aḷanda nīḷ nilam tān nēr – only the activity of measuring this huge world, is equivalent

**Перевод:**

**pin ninṟu tāy irappak kēḷān – kausalyā, mother of śrī rāma, followed śrī rāma and told him “dear son, I have only one son; can I live if I am separated from you? Please do not go to the forest”. Not heeding to her plea, śrī rāma went to the forest.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the activity of vāmana, measuring the worlds, is equivalent to śrī rāma going to the forest despite his mother stopping him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

More than that

**perum paṇaittōḷ mun ninṟu** – [sītā pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), who is soft and is incarnation of śrī mahālakśmi, and who is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 60-20 as “*bāhū rāmasya samśritā*” (one who embraced the shoulders of [śrī rāma](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and who said as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāndam 27-7 “*agratastē gamiśyāmi*” (I will precede you \[to the forest\]), told śrī rāma that she cannot survive without him and prayed to him to let her precede him to the forest. In śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 30-18 didn’t sītāp pirātti tell śrī rāma “*yastvayā saha sasvargō nirayō yastvayā vinā*” (it will be heavenly if I am with you and hellish if separated from you)! sītāp pirātti’s divine shoulder has been described here as *perum paṇaittōḷ moymmalarāḷ.* It is opined here that sītāp pirātti’s gentleness and divine beauty were such that śrī rāma himself would come and beseech her.

**irappāḷ** – Just as one would throw a beautiful garland into fire thinking that it would make it more beautiful, the person with a gentle and divine beauty went to the forest \[the simile stresses that both acts are absurd\]. sītāp pirātti prayed to [śrī rāma](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) that she will not live separated from him and that she would precede him to the forest. śrī rāma, thinking whether it would be apt to take sītāp pirātti to the forest which will be similar to a garland being thrown in sunlight, refused to take her. While sītāp pirātti was constantly thinking of śrī rāma, śrī rāma was constantly thinking of sītāp pirātti.

**sol ninṟa tōḷ nalandān nērillāt tōnṟal** – the epic śrī rāmāyaṇa was focussed on describing the valour (the strong quality of shoulders) of śrī rāma. Even at the beginning (of the epic) it mentioned as in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 1-3 “*vīryavān*” (one who is valorous) and in kiśkintā kāṇḍam 3-15 it mentioned “*āyatāśca suvrutāśca bāhava: parigōpamā:*” (shoulders which were long and which were well rounded). Even sūrpaṇaka, who eats corpses, saysof śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 19-14 “*sukumārau mahābalau*” (they are very gentle and very strong). rāmapirān, who has such shoulder power and who is incomparable …

**tōnṟal** – śrī rāma appeared as if he is the epitome of gentleness.

**avan aḷanda nīḷ nilam tān attanaikkum nēr** – the great expanse of earth which was measured during the incarnation of trivikrama, would equal that. Just as it is mentioned in periya tirumadal “*ponnagaram pinnē pulamba valangoṇḍu mannum vaḷanādu kaivittu*” the act of going to the forest as ordained by his father, not listening to the pleas of his relatives, is equivalent to measuring the worlds. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that the act of going through the stones and thorns in the forest, in rāmāvatāram, with his gentle divine feet is equivalent to measuring the world with its forests and ups and downs with his gentle divine feet during the incarnation of trivikrama.

We shall take up the 80<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 80. Nernden adimai

*nērndēn adimai ninaindēn adu oṇ kamalam ārndēn un sēvadimēl anbāy ārnda adikkōlam kaṇḍavarkku enkolō munnaip padikkōlam kaṇḍa pagal*

**Пословный перевод:**

adimai nērndēn – (at your divine feet) I got to carry out service; oṇ kamalam adu ninaindēn – I thought about the beautiful lotus-like divine feet; un sēvadi mēl – in the matter of your divine feet; anbu āy ārndēn – I became the epitome of affection; ārnda – that which measured the worlds; adi kōlam – the beauty of the divine feet; kaṇḍavarkku – to those who were fortunate to have a darśan (vision) of; padi kōlam kaṇḍa munnaip pagal en kolō – how would it have been to those who were able to have darśan of the natural decoration in the previous times?

**Перевод:**

**nērndēn adimai – I involved myself in service to emperumān. I attained the service which was apt for me. Just as he had used the term tuṇindēn in pāsuram 65 (I resolved), āḻvār is using the term nērndēn here. Kings get to have their coronation. In the same way, I got to carry out service.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the moment he thought of emperumān, he got engaged with him and that the services that he renders at the divine feet of emperumān were very much delicious for him. He wonders that if this is the status for a person who just thought of emperumān, what would have befallen those who got to see him entirely, directly. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ninaindēn adu oṇ kamalam** – I thought of his beautiful divine feet. This is coronation for this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-6-kala-chakkaraththodu/) “*kōlamām en sennikku un kamalamanna kurai kaḻalē*” (your lotus-like divine feet are the apt decoration for my head) and [kulaśēkara āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kulasekarazhwar/) in his perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7-11 “*māladi mudi mēl kōlamām kulasēkaran*” (the decoration on the head of kulasēkara āḻvār when kept on the divine feet of tirumāḷ) have mercifully mentioned that the divine feet of emperumān would be the decoration on their heads. Those who wish to be servitors should meditate only on the divine feet. In śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-10 it is said “*divyajñānōpapannāstē*” (the sages with divine wisdom surrendered to śrī rāma) and in ayōdyā kāṇḍam 83-9 bharata said “*druśta Eva hi na śōkam apanēśyati rāghava:*” (śrī rāma will remove our hurdles as soon as he sees them).

**ārndēn un sēvadimēl anbāy** – I was full of affection for your divine reddish feet and as a result became saturated with that love. I was fully involved in your divine feet with love.

**ārnda adikkōlam kaṇḍavarkku en koḷo munnaip padikkōlam kaṇḍa pagal** – For those who had the fortune of seeing emperumān’s divine feet, what would have happened when they had the fortune of seeing the divine form of emperumān, which is the causative factor for the divine feet! In other words, how much would they have suffered \[emperumān’s beauty would have left them bewildered\]! Alternative meaning – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) refers to himself as one who had the fortune of seeing his divine feet and wonders that if he is so bewildered looking at his divine feet, what would have those, who got to see his complete divine form in earlier period, have suffered? We can also consider this as āḻvār wondering as to how those who had earlier seen the beauty of his divine form would now have suffered after seeing the beauty of his divine feet. Another explanation is that for those who got to see emperumān’s divine feet during the time of his trivikrama incarnation, how they would have have felt when they had earlier seen the natural beauty of his divine form with the divine feet.

We shall take up the 81<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 81. Pagal kanden

*pagal kaṇḍēn nāraṇanaik kaṇḍēn kanavil migak kaṇḍēn mīṇḍavanai meyyē migak kaṇḍēn ūn tigaḻum nēmi oḷi tigaḻum sēvadiyān vān tigaḻum sōdi vadivu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pagal kaṇḍēn – I saw continuous daylight, without any admixture with night, which was like a new dawn; nāraṇanaik kaṇḍēn – I saw śrīman nārāyaṇa (who is like the never-setting sun); mīṇḍu – again; kanavil – more than seeing him directly, through the mind; miga meyyē – very clearly; avanaik kāṇḍēn – I got to see him; Un tigaḻum nēmi – having the divine disc on his divine form; oḷi tigaḻum sēvadiyān – emperumān having radiantly divine feet; vān tigaḻum – in the paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); sōdi vadivu – the radiant divine form; miga kaṇḍēn – I got to see well (here)

**Перевод:**

**pagal kaṇḍēn – I saw a good dawn. Days before this were full of heavy darkness. Instead of such a heavily dark day, I saw dawn. Just as akrūra said, as in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-2 “suprabhātā ca mē niśā” (my night became a beautiful dawn for me today itself), on the day that he went to see kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa), āḻvār also says similarly. There is only one night and one day. In other words, all the times before knowing emperumān are nights only and all the times after knowing him are only days. There will be no admixture of nights in this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke of the status of those who got to see emperumān. In this pāsuram, he mercifully explains the status of emperumān on showing himself to his devotees. In the previous pāsuram he spoke of the status of the sages of dandakāraṇyam (forest area) on seeing the divine form of śrī rāma pirān. We can construe that in this pāsuram, āḻvār says that not only those sages, but even he had the fortune to see emperumān and as a result, a new dawn occurred to him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nāraṇanaik kaṇḍēn** – When āḻvār was asked “Your saying that you had seen dawn is not clear to us. Your words are incomprehensible, similar to those of vadugar’s. Please say it in a way which is comprehensible to us”, he says that he had seen the sun which does not set. He says that he had also seen himself not sleeping. What has been mentioned in gaudapāda kārikai 21 “*anādi māyayā sūpta:*” ([jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) sleeping from time immemorial due to prakruti sambandam (connection to primordial matter)) is not natural to jīvātmā. He \[jīvātmā\], like iḷaiyaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) is expected to give up his sleep and carry out service. It is said of nityasūris in rig aśta. 1-2-7 “*sadā paśyanti…. jāgruvām samindatē*” (always awake and seeing). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he saw [śrīman nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the lord of both the vibhūtis. He is the sūriya (sun) who does not travel. He is not one who is appointed but one who gives out commands. Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 8 “*bhīśōdēti sūrya:*” (sun rises due to his fear of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), he orders sūriya also. Not only sūriya. Just as taittirīyavalli Ara. 3.11 says “*anta: praviśta: śāstā janānām sarvātmā*” (being the indvelling soul of all jivātmās, he permeates all and controls them) he controls all.

**kanavil migak kaṇḍēn mīṇḍu avanai** – instead of seeing with the help of sensory perceptions \[with the sensory eyes\] [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he saw emperumān fully, with his mind. He saw emperumān with the help of his mind which cannot be confused by sensory perceptions.

**meyyē migak kaṇḍēn** – I saw beautifully such that I could know very well.

What did I see?

**Un tigaḻum nēmi** – *cakrāyudam* (disc) which has a beautiful form; or, cakrāyudam which kills the enemies and stays in their bodies; or, cakrāyudam which stays on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form. īsn’t he praised by [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his [tiruppallāṇḍu 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-2-adiyomodum/) “*vadivār sōdi valattuṟaiyum sudarāḻi*” (cakrāyuda which dwells on emperumān’s right hand and is radiant)!

**oḷi tigaḻum sēvadiyān** – one who has divine feet which are radiant; the divine feet which promulgate the greatness of the lord.

**vān tigaḻum sōdi vadivu** – I got to see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who resides in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) with radiant form. Alternatively *vān tigaḻum* could mean having a form like cloud and [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he saw here itself the radiant form which remains in the sky.

We shall take up the 82<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 82. Vadikkola val nedungan

*vadikkōla vāḷ nedungaṇ māmalarāḷ sevvip padikkōlam kaṇḍagalāḷ pannāḷ adikkōli jñālattāḷ pinnum nalam purindadu enkolō kōlattāl illai kuṟai*

**Пословный перевод:**

vadikkōlam – having filtered (pure) beauty; vāl nedum kaṇ – having divine sweet eyes which are shining; mā malarāḷ – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) who was born in a great lotus flower; sevvi padik kōlam – having a natural beauty which is appropriate \[referring to emperumān\]; kaṇḍu – enjoying with her divine eyes; pal nāḷ agalāḷ – is always together with emperumān, without separating from him even for a moment; pinnum – even after seeing periya pirāttiyār being engaged with the beautiful form of emperumān; jñālattāḷ – bhūmippirātti (bhūdēvi); adikkōli – being ecstatic; nalam purindadu enkolō – why is she nurturing her desire for emperumān?; kōlattāl kuṟai illai – (despite many people enjoying) there is no shortcoming in the beauty (since emperumān has unlimited beauty, there is no embargo on many persons desiring his beauty at the same time)

**Перевод:**

**vadikkōlam – pirātti’s beauty is such that it is fully filtered so that there is no fault in it and only the pure form of beauty is present. nanjīyar (preceptor of the commentator nampiḷḷai) will ask as to whose beauty is the one which is made from the residue (of pirātti’s beauty) (The opinion here is that it is emperumān’s beauty which is made from the residual beauty of pirātti’s). emperumān’s beauty is such that all the other beautiful forms in the world are faulty in comparison with his while pirātti’s beauty is such that emperumān’s beauty is faulty in comparison with hers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “Is it that I saw only that beautiful form?”. In this pāsuram he says that he also saw pirātti, who has a matching form for emperumān, along with him. In the previous pāsuram he said that he got to see nārāyaṇa. In this pāsuram he says that he saw him together with tirumagaḷ (mahālakśmi). It is not the brahmacāri (bachelor) nārāyaṇa, but tirunārāyaṇa (śrīman nārāyaṇa) that he saw. Just as radiance is to sun, pirātti is for emperumān and hence he says that he saw emperumān with radiance. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāḷ negungaṇ māmalarāḷ** – pirātti is such that she has this faultless beauty, is radiant and has wide, expansive divine eyes (vāḷ here means radiance). She is the personification of sweetness.  Her eyes have one target and one person. Even those who enjoy this cannot see the boundary for this beauty. Has it not been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-14 “*trailōkyarājyam sakalam sītāyā: nāpnuyāt kalām*” (even the entire three worlds cannot match a part of sītā pirātti’s beauty)! The term *vāḷ nedungaṇ* implies that her eyes have the filtered portion out of the filtered beauty of her divine form.

**mā malarāḷ** – pirātti has fragrance as her causative factor. She has the softness and fragrance of lotus flower.

**sevvip padikkōlam kaṇḍu agalāḷ pannāḷ** – looking at the divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which is beauty personified, she will not leave him even for a moment.

**sevvi** refers to the freshness of the flower which has just blossomed.

**padikkōlam** refers to his beauty which is referred to as “*arumbinai alarai* ” (budding flower, about to blossom) in triuvāimoḻi 7-10-1 and as “*sadaika rūparūpāya*” (having unchanging form) in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam. Even as [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) is constantly enjoying his beautiful form, she will not leave him even for a moment as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*iṟaiyum agalagillēn*”(cannot separate even for a moment). She will not feel satisfied even after seeing and enjoying  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) for a very long time and will not leave him. Is it because she has no other alternative? No, it is because she cannot take her eyes off him and cannot move away from him and will continue to enjoy him.

Even while periya pirāttiyār is like this

**adikkōli jñālattāḷ** – bhūmip pirātti (bhūdēvi), with lot of desire, got in to enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). While [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) enjoys only emperumān, bhūmip pirātti enjoys both emperumān and periya pirātti. Consistent with this, she enters with lot of expectation. For periya pirātti’s eyes, the target is only emperumān while for bhūmip pirātti, since the target is double with the union of emperumān and periya pirātti, her longing is twice as much.

**jñālattāḷ** – epitome of patience.

**pinnum nalam purindadu enkolō** – In a deep water body \[lake or river or ocean\] when an expert in swimming struggles to stay afloat, the person in the shore should not enter that place and instead, plan to move away. [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), unable to enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) fully, struggles but bhūmip pirātti decides to enter the fray. When periya pirātti, who is far superior to everyone else, herself struggles to enjoy emperumān, bhūmip pirātti, instead of moving away from the place, engages with emperumān. What is the reason for this?

**kōlattāl illai kuṟai** – For the people in the world, the beauty will be such that even two people will be one too many to enjoy that beauty. But [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s beauty which is without any shortcoming is such that, unlike the former case, anyone who comes to him will immerse in it. The beauty of his divine form will attract even those who are on the shores. Even when her nose and ears were cut off, did not sūrpaṇakā describe the beauty of rāma and lakśmaṇa, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 19-14 “*taruṇau rūpa sampannau*” (they are young and beautiful)! Due to her ill thoughts, she went the wrong way. People will be caught in emperumān’s divine form in whatever state they are in. In samsāram, there isn’t enough beauty for one person to enjoy another. Thus if there are many people, it leads to fight. However, in the case of emperumān, his greatness is such that many people can enter the river \[of emperumān\] to enjoy him. All that is required is a sense of longing to enjoy him.

We shall take up the 83<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 83. Kuraiyaga venjorkal

*kuṟaiyāga venjoṟkaḷ kūṟinēn kūṟi maṟaiyāngena uraitta mālai iṟaiyēnum eeyungolenṟē irundēn enaippagalum māyan kaṇ senṟa varam*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṟai – vēdas (sacred texts); āngu ena uraitta mālai – stating that emperumān is like that (instead of like this); kuṟai āga – having faults; vem soṟkaḷ – with words which singe the eardrum; kūṟinēn – I spoke; kūṟi – apart from speaking (like that); māyan kaṇ senṟa varam – with the mercy which is present in him; iṟaiyēnum – at least a little bit; eeyum kol enṟē – will give me (expecting the benefit); enaip pagalum – for a long time; irundēn – I am waiting

**Перевод:**

**kuṟaiyāga venjoṟkaḷ kūṟinēn kūṟi – āḻvār says that these are the wrong actions that he had done: I spoke in such a way that there will be faults. I spoke harsh words. I spoke about emperumān who is full in everything, with belittling words. On top of that, I spoke in such a cruel way that the ears of those who listened to those words would turn red.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In pāsuram 74, āḻvār called himself as “perum tamiḻan nallēn” (an expert in great tamiḻ arts), mentioning about his greatness and composed poems also \[to prove his point\]. Now, thinking of the union of emperumān and pirātti, he feels ashamed and says “am I attempting to sing about them! What a courageous act did I try to enact!” feeling repentence. Earlier, since he spoke about emperumān who is greater than everyone else, he asked whether there is anyone who is equal to him. But since he has now seen emperumān along with pirātti, he says that he has not said anything appropriate to their greatness. nammāḻvār too has mercifully mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 3-1-6 “pūvinmēl māduvāḻ mārvināy ensolli yān vāḻttuvanē” (Oh one who has śrīmahālakśmi, who stays on a lotus flower, residing in your chest! How can I praise you?). He feels ashamed that he attempted to sing about emperumān after seeing his greatness. Now it appears to him that what he did earlier (in pāsuram 74) and his desire to obtain benefit from emperumān are inappropriate. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kuṟaiyāga** – If a person sees an ocean, can he estimate its depth or the length of its shore? Looking at the greatness of emperumān and his own lowliness, it appears to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) that his words were belittling.

**vem soṟkaḷ** – I spoke in such a way that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine mind would have felt hurt.

**kūṟinēn** – I spoke about something which I couldn’t have seen. I spoke of a matter which cannot be estimated, in a way which was limiting it.

**kūṟi** – not only doing the above. With the knowledge that I had, I did not even repent for my act. I felt that the act that I had undertaken was correct.

**maṟai āngu ena uṟaitta mālai** –  If the person who was spoken about by me were one who could be forgotten, all that I had done would have been without any fault. But emperumān, who was spoken about me, is such that whatever he utters becomes vēda. He is such a supreme lord. muṇḍaka upaniśat 2-1-4 says of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “*vāgvivrutāśca vēdā:*” (whatever was uttered by him became vēda); he is superior to everyone else. Alternatively, vēdas which came to explain the true meanings would define him such that he cannot be estimated; he has such greatness. We can construe this as vēdas saying that he is *āngu* meaning he is “like that”.

**iṟaiyēnum eeungol enṟē irundēn** – even though none of whatever I had composed on him deserved any present (gift), I waited in anticipation as to whether emperumān would give some present for it. Not only did I say something with fault, but I was waiting for being complimented for it!

**enaip pagalum** – for a very long time

**māyankaṇ senṟa varam** – the grace which was present in that wondrous [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**iṟaiyēnum eeyungol enṟē irundēn** – after seeing the way he was giving gifts to those who sang about him earlier, I was also expectantly waiting whether he would not gift me anything.

Will this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) sing a hymn for getting a benefit? Even though he did not sing the hymns for a benefit, if emperumān looks at him and graces him with his eyes, āḻvār would treat that as a benefit for his svarūpam (basic nature).

**senṟa varam** – we can construe this as meaning that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) was waiting in anticipation as to whether [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) would grant the boon at least a little bit.

We shall take up the 84<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 84. Varam karudit tannai

*varam karudit tannai vaṇangāda vanmai uram karudi mūrkkattavanai naram kalanda singamāyk kīṇḍa tiruvan adiyiṇaiyē angaṇmā jñālattu amudu*

**Пословный перевод:**

varam karudi – keeping the boons (granted by dēvas, celestial entities) in mind; tannai vaṇangāda – not bowing down to him (emperumān); vanmai uram karudi – thinking about (his) strength of boons and physical strength; mūrkkattavanai – iraṇiyan (hiraṇyakaśyap) who was arrogant; naram kalanda singamāyk kīṇḍa – breaking him, taking the form of narasimha (face of lion with body of human); tiruvan – the beautiful; adi iṇaiyē – the two divine feet; am kaṇ mā jñālattu – in this expansive beautiful place; amudu – (sweet) nectar

**Перевод:**

**varam karudi – iraṇiyan had thought too great of the boons that he had obtained from other dēvatās. Just as a person would trust a boat with a hole in it, he was trusting those boons.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that let other things remain as they are. The matter which is very delicious for me is only this (emperumān). He gives out the reason for his not moving away from emperumān.  After seeing the way emperumān gave himself for one devotee, prahlāda, he felt his fear leaving him and he enjoys emperumān as “he is ours”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tannai vaṇangāda vanmai** – he was of the opinion that he would not bow down to the sarvēśvara, who is the causative factor for those dēvatās too. iraṇiyan had the same behaviour as rāvaṇa who said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyantu kaśyacit*” (even if broken into two, I will not bow down before anybody).

**uram karudi** – being vain due to his haughtiness; being egoistic

**ūkkattavanai** – one who felt proud because of the strength of his boons and his shoulders; one who is egoistic. *ūkkam* refers to a state of being. If the text is considered as **mūrkkattavanai** the meaning will change to – one who is arrogant and stubborn.

**naram kalanda singamāyk kīṇḍa** – iraṇiyan had taken a boon that he should not be killed by anything which had been created until then. Hence [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took a form which was a combination of lion and human being, *narasimha*, a form which had not been created until then and eliminated him. He incarnated combining both lion and human.

**naram kalanda singam** – like a mixture of concentrated milk and sugar or a mixture of milk and honey, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took incarnation as a combination of lion and human. If he had taken a form of only lion or man, he would have had to be pushed out.

**tiruvan** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that even if emperumān had combined two forms which were incompatible with each other, he had total radiance about him. He says that emperumān was like *śrīmat* \[refers to mahālakśmi being always with emperumān\]. Haven’t nānmugan tiruvandādi 22 “*aḻagiyān tane ariyuruvam tānē*” (even if he has the form of lion, he is beautiful) and śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam says “*nārasimhavapu: śrīmān*” (he is lion and human forms put together and has the support of mahālakśmi) conveyed the same meaning!

**adiyiṇaiyē** – his two divine feet, which complement each other.

**angaṇ mā jñālattu amudu** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the nectar consumed on the beautiful, expansive earth. The nectar which is to be enjoyed in *paramapadam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is available to be enjoyed on this earth itself, he says.

**tiruvan** – one who is with [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). īsn’t  the reason for removing the enemies of followers, to please the mother of such followers! Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 30-39 “*tam druśtva śatruhantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham babhūva hruśtā vaidēhi bhārtāram pariśasvajē*” (after seeing śrī rāma who annihilated the enemies and gave comfort to the sages, sītā felt that she existed; she was joyous; she embraced him, her husband, tightly), did not sītāp pirātti become happy because the demons were eliminated and hence hugged him! Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as **tiruvan** because he killed iraṇiyan.

**angaṇmā jñālam** – since the earth was apt for emperumān to take incarnation, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) calls it as *angaṇmājñālam* (beautiful, expansive world).

We shall take up the 85<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 85. Amudenrum tenenrum

*amudenṟum tēnenṟum āḻiyānenṟum amudanṟu koṇḍugandān enṟum amudanna sonmālai ēttit toḻudēn solappatta nanmālai ētti navinṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

amudu enṟum – that he \[emperumān\] is like nectar; tēn enṟum – that he is like honey; āḻiyān enṟum – that he has the divine disc \[cakrāyudam\]; anṟu amdu koṇḍu ugandān enṟum – that he had, in previous time, (churned the ocean and) gave nectar (to dēvas) and was happy; ētti – vorśipping (him); solappatta – mentioned (like these in śāstras, the sacred texts); nal mālai – the very great emperumān; amudu anna sol mālai – with these pāsurams (hymns) which are like nectar; ētti navinṟu toḻudēn – vorśipped him, praising him many times

**Перевод:**

**amudu enṟum – just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “rasō vai sa:” (he is very sweet) emperumān is like nectar.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Even as āḻvār is enjoying the delicious experience of emperumān, he says that he strung a garland of words, which was like nectar, and vorśipped him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tēn enṟum** – rig maṇḍalam 1-21-154 says “*viṣṇō: padē paramē madva utsa:*”   (honey flows from the supremely divine feet of śrī viṣṇu)

**āḻiyān enṟum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the very sweet cakrāyudam (divine disc) capable of annihilating enemies.

**amudenṟum tēnenṟum āḻiyān enṟum** – it appears that the word *cakrāyudam* is very much like the words nectar and honey \[in being sweet to followers since their enemies are annihilated\].

**amudu anṟu koṇḍu ugandān enṟum** – when dēvas, the celestial entities, out of fear from asuras, the demonic entities, sought refuge under [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), he churned the ocean and offered nectar to the dēvas, who always seek only other benefits. He is one who goes in search of what the dēvas want, which is the lowly nectar, when they did not want him, who is far sweeter than that nectar.

**ugandān** – He was not angry that the dēvas did not ask for him but wanted only nectar. He was happy that they came to him for seeking the other benefits too. When people come to him seeking aiśvaryam (wealth), kaivalyam (soul enjoying itself infinitely, instead of carrying out service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) etc, isn’t he the one who celebrates these acts \[of people coming to him for other benefits too\] as mentioned by him in bhagavat gītā 7-18 “*udārā: sarva ēvai tē*” (they are all magnanimous persons, who come to me for alms)!

**amudanna sonmālai ēttit toḻudēn** – apart from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is like nectar and the nectar of dēvas’, the nectar for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is the word spoken about emperumān.

**sonmālai** – this word \[about emperumān\] itself is the special nectar for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) as both emperumān and the nectar of dēvas’ have faults. Since emperumān has all sweetness within himself, he is termed as nectar. Since āḻvār’s word contains [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) within itself, he says that his word is nectar.

**ēttit toḻudēn** – after praising him with such unbounded sweet words, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he fell at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) getting rid of his possessive qualities. He says that it is not that he composed poems on emperumān knowingly. He merely praised emperumān and vorśipped him. The praised words became a poem, due to emperumān’s  mercy. As mentioned in authentic texts, “*yasmin akrutrimagirām gatirēkakaṇṭā:*” (in whom all the faultless words coalesce with one voice), all authentic sources celebrate emperumān; they celebrate emperumān who is superior to everyone else; emperumān who is the meaning for all words; emperumān who is praised as in jitantē ślōkam 1-9 “*vacasām vācyamuttamam*” (he is the greatest meaning for all words).

**ētti navinṟu** – I vorśipped him with lot of affection; I vorśipped him such that my involvement was explicit in my praise. I vorśipped him with a garland of fine words.

We shall take up the 86<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 86. Navinruraitta navalargal

*navinṟuraitta nāvalargaḷ nāṇmalar koṇḍu āngē payinṟadanāl peṟṟa payan enkol payinṟār tam meyttavattāl kāṇbariya mēgamaṇivaṇṇanai yān ettvattāl kāṇbankol inṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

navinṟu uraitta – (through other means, reciting) emperumān’s divine names for a long time; nāvalargaḷ – poets; nāḷ malar koṇḍu – taking flowers which had just then blossomed; āngē payinṟu – approaching that emperumān closely; adanāl peṟṟa payan enkol – what is the benefit (that they had) thus obtained?; payinṟār tam – those who carry out efforts through other means \[than emperumān’s mercy\]; mey tavattāl – the penance that they carry out with their physical bodies; kāṇbariya – one who is impossible to see; mēga maṇivaṇṇanai – emperumān who has the complexion of cloud and blue coloured gem; yān – adiyēn (the servitor, I); inṟu – now; ettavattāl kol kāṇban – with which penance will I see?

**Перевод:**

**navinṟu uraittu – reciting emperumān’s divine names repeatedly, with lot of  involvement**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is asking whether those who got to see emperumān through their own efforts, by observing other means, could equal him who got to see emperumān through his mercy. To reach emperumān who does not have anyone superior to him or even equalling him, śouldn’t one follow a means which is like emperumān himself – without anything superior or equal to it? (is there any such means other than emperumān’s mercy!) āḻvār says that when compared to the benefit that he had obtained through emperumān’s mercy, those who had attained emperumān before him have not got anything. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nāvalargaḷ** – entities such as brahmā et al, who desire other benefits

**nāḷ malar koṇḍu** – taking objects such as freshly blossomed flowers; taking good flowers

**āngē payinṟadanāl peṟṟa payan enkol** – what benefits did such people get after attaining emperumān? The benefit which a person obtains with his own efforts is not a benefit at all.

**payinṟār tam** – those who carry out means with their own efforts, i.e. those who attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) this way.

**meyttavattāl** – through the penance that they carry out by subjecting their physical bodies to pain

**kāṇbariya mēga maṇivaṇṇanai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is difficult to see through their own efforts, who is having the complexion of cloud and blue coloured gems, who has a divine form which removes the fatigue of those who see him

**yān ettavattāl kāṇban kol inṟu** – what penance did I, a very lowly entity, carry out and see him? I saw him because he chose to show himself to me. It is only this much and nothing else. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that despite not carrying out any effort, he was able to see emperumān because of his natural grace. Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said of himself that there is none like him as in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-5-8-namakkum/) “*yār nigar agal vānattē*” (who, in śrīvaikuṇṭam, can equal me?) and as in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-5-9-vanaththum/) “*vānak kōnaik kavisolla vallēṟku ini māṟu uṇḍō*” (is there anyone who can substitute me in singing paeans on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)?), this āḻvār too is asking if there is anyone who is his equal.

**kāṇban kol inṟu** – unless I had known yesterday that I am going to see emperumān today, how could I find a reason for this? He says that he got to see emperumān who is causeless, only with the sheer grace of emperumān.

We shall move on to the 87<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
