## 87. Inravariginren allen

*inṟāvaṟiginṟēn allēn iru nilattaic cenṟāngu adippadutta sēvadiyai anṟu karukkōttiyuḷ kidandu kai toḻudēn kaṇḍēn tirukkōtti endai tiṟam*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu karukkōttiyuḷ kidandu – lying inside the womb, earlier; tirukkōtti endai tiṟam – the nature of tirukkōttiyūr perumān; kaṇḍēn – I had the fortune to enjoy; kai toḻudēn – (as a consequence of that experience) I vorśipped with folded hands; irunilattai – this expansive mass of land \[earth\]; āngu senṟu aḷanda tiru adiyai – (during the time of trivikrama avatāram) the divine foot which spread out, on its own, to various places and measured them; inṟā aṟiginṟēn allēn – it is not that I knew about it only today (I have not forgotten it right from my days of being in the womb)

**Перевод:**

**inṟā aṟiginṟēn allēn – am I knowing something new? I am not starting to know this just now. I had received his \[emperumān’s\] grace earlier itself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was asked as to what is the greatness of the benefit that he attained, he says “don’t think that I attained this benefit only today. Right from the time that I was in the womb, I have known my nature of servitude to emperumān and have been enjoying him“. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iru nilattaic cenṟu āngaḷanda tiruvadiyai** – not that someone prayed him to do it, but emperumān went on his own and measured this expansive earth. Did the earth go to the place where he wanted to measure? No; did he not go to where the earth was and measure it!

**anṟu** – much earlier, when no one had the qualification to observe any means, he went on his own and measured it.

**karukkōttiyuḷ kidandu kai toḻudēn kaṇḍēn** – I saw \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] even when I was inside the womb.

**kaṇḍēn kai toḻudēn** – in order to manifest the experience that I enjoyed I involved in servitude such as joining the palms together (*anjali*). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has also mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-3-3-ariyak-kalaththulle/) “*aṟiyāk kālattuḷḷē adimaikkaṇ anbu seyvittu*” (even when I was ignorant, you created the craving desire in me for carrying out service to you). The ruśis of daṇḍakāraṇya said of themselves in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-21 “*garbhabhūdāstapōdanā:*” (even while residing inside the womb, we adopted penance as wealth). Just as it is mentioned in mahābhāratam śānti parvam 358-73 “*jāyamānam hi puruśam yam paśyēt madusūdana: sātvikas satu vijñēya: sa vai mōkśārta cintaka:*” (it is to be known that that ātmā who is blessed by madusūdana even as he is born, is the one with *satvaguṇa* (noble qualities); it is he who thinks about attaining śrīvaikuṇṭam), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) graced me even while I was in the womb.

**tirukkōtti endai** – the one who has taken residence in tirukkōttiyūr (one of the divine abodes of emperumān) saw my pitiable state and blessed me.

**tirukkōtti endai** – while I saw emperumān’s greatness in śrīvaikuṇṭam, I saw his simplicity (easily approachable quality) in tirukkōttiyūr. At the very first sight itself, I saw his completeness in [*arcāvatāram*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html) [(emperumān’s incarnation in deity form)](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html).

**endai tiṟam** – I saw the qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is my Lord.

We shall go on to the 88<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 88. Tirambirru iniyarinden

*tiṟambiṟṟu iniyaṟindēn tennarangattu endai tiṟambā vaḻi senṟārkku allāl tiṟambāc cedinaragai nīkkit tām selvadan mun vānōr kadi nagara vāsal kadavu*

**Пословный перевод:**

then arangattu endai – my svāmy (Lord) who is reclining in the beautiful temple \[śrīrangam temple\]; tiṟambā vaḻi – in the path from where one cannot fail; senṟarkku allāl – except for those people; tām – on their own; tiṟambā sedi naragai nīkki – trying to sever themselves from the terrible hell called samsāram (materialistic realm) which is like an unseverable bush; selvadan mun – before they could go; vānōr – nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); kadi – having well fortified; nagaram – the city of śrīvaikuṇṭam; vāsal kadavu – the entrance door; tiṟambiṟṟu – will close; ini ārindēn – I know now

**Перевод:**

**tiṟambiṟṟu – for those who thought that they would get on their own efforts, it did not fructify.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān gives himself only to those who he blesses and takes under him and not to others. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ini aṟindēn** – I know now.

**tennarangattu endai** – kaṇṇabirān (kriṣṇa) told arjuna in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*mām ēkam śaraṇam vraja*” (hold on to me as the means). He said “leaving aside various means which are insentient, hold on to me who is [*paramacētana*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) [(supremely sentient)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) and who is trying to attain you”. Those words were spoken to one arjuna at one point of time. periya perumāḷ (śrīranganāta, who is dwelling in the śrīrangam temple), tells at all times “*mām ēkam śaraṇam vraja*” The word *mām* refers to the simplicity of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The simplicity found in periya perumāḷ is far greater than that found in kriṣṇa. periya perumāḷ’s simplicity will make the simplicity of kriṣṇa to run for life. āṇḍāḷ has mercifully stated in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-10 “*semmaiyudaiya tiruvarangar*” (śrīranganātar who acts in unison through his thoughts, speech and actions) and in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-3 “*sengōludaiya tiruvarangaccelvanār*” (śrīranganātar who rules both materialistic and spiritualistic realms with a spectre in his hand). Even to reach śrīvaikuṇṭam, he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] has to decide first.

**tiṟambā vaḻi senṟārkku allāl** – other than those who hold on to him as the means

**tiṟambā vaḻi** – the means which will never fail. In other words, the way which was determined in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*mām ēkam*” (he is the only way). Unlike the other forms of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (para, vyūha and vibhava which are approachable only to nitya-muktars (eternally free and liberated ones respectively), muktaprāyars (those who are at par with liberated ones) and the fortunate ones), arcāvataram is easily accessible to everyone. It can also be construed as – attaining other dēvatās is the wrong path and attaining emperumān is the way which will not fail.

**tiṟambāccedi naragai** – the hellish place (materialistic realm) which was made that way by people themselves, from which escaping is impossible.

**tiṟambā** – the hell from which one cannot escape. The place which will remain despite experiencing it for a long time.

**nīkkit tām selvadan mun** – even for those who can escape this.

But, before that

**tān selvadan mun** –  this [cētana (sentient entity)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who is *omniscient and omnipotent* is unable to cross this simple hurdle! (in other words, is it possible for the cētana who is ignorant and incapable, to cross this hurdle?) The opinion here is that the time that he had spent carrying out sinful activities has been without any beginning, hence he does not have enough time to cross this samsāram.

**vānōr kadi nagara vāsal kadavu** – the door to śrivikuṇṭam which is fortified by the presence of nityasūris. *kadi* refers to protection. Hasn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself said in varāha carama ślōkam “*nayāmi paramām gatim*” (I will take you to a great status)! Instead of attaining him by not shunning him when he comes to help us, how sad it is that one loses the opportunity by attempting to attain him through one’s own efforts by destroying one’s own *svarūpam* (basic nature)!

**kadi nagaram** – kadi can also refer to fragrance, in which case the meaning will change to śrīvaikuṇṭam which is sweet.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that he learnt that he somehow escaped from this predicament. Attaining him through his mercy is the best. When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is attempting to attain the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), the cētana making efforts to attain him and losing him through such activities is very lowly. There are people who lose him because they are inimical to him. There are also people who lose him, despite being favourable to him, by attempting to reach him on their own as stated previously. Thus there are two types of people who lose him.

We shall move on to the 89<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 89. Kadavikkadam siranda

*kadavikkadam siṟanda kanjanai mun kāyndu ādavip pōr yānai osittu padaviyāy pāṇiyāl nīrēṟṟup paṇḍorukāl māvaliyai māṇiyāy koṇḍilaiyē maṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadam siṟanda – very angry; kanjanai – kamsa; mun kadavik kāyndu – getting angry with him in his presence; poṟ yānai – the elephant kuvalayāpidam which came to fight with him \[kriṣṇa\]; adavi – controlling it; osittu – breaking its tusks; paṇḍu oru kāl – once upon a time; padaviyāy – having the quality of gentleness; māṇiyāy – as a brahmacāri (bachelor); māvaliyai – king mahābali; pāṇiyāl – with hand; nīr ēṟṟu – taking water as indication of accepting alms; maṇ koṇḍilaiyē – did you not obtain earth?

**Перевод:**

**kadam siṟanda kanjanai – kamsa who was very angry**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “Have I helped anyone who has held on to me, for you to say like this?” to which āḻvār responds “Haven’t you done so?”. āḻvār says how the karmas (deeds, both good and bad) of those who hold on to him disappear. He tells about the way emperumān carries out activities for his followers and how he removes their enemies. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kadavi mun kāyndu** – like spitting fire

**mun kāyndu** – doing to himself what he wanted to do (i.e. while kamsa wanted to kill kriṣṇa, kriṣṇa killed kamsa)

ōr

**kadavi** – getting angry

**kadam siṟandu** – it can be said that the anger itself appeared to be beautiful

**adavip pōr yānai osittu** – breaking the tusks of the elephant, incapacitating it. kriṣṇa kept dodging the elephant and drove it such that the elephant became tired and he pulled out its tusks without any effort. This was done to help vasudēva and dēvaki.

**padaviyāy** – being very simple. Since you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\], despite having so much strength, saw a good quality in mahābali. Alternatively the word *padaviyāy* can be construed as – unlike killing kamsan, emperumān honourably let mahābali to live.

**pāṇiyāl** – with the hand which had only given to others all along

**nīr ēṟṟu** – getting very joyous at getting back his possession of the worlds as if it is something which he could never get

**paṇḍoru kāl māṇiyāyk koṇḍilaiyē maṇ** – did you accept it as a gift such that your wealth (greatness) showed? Did you not accept the gift as a bachelor, manifesting the quality of seeking alms! It appeared as if your hands had got scarred by accepting alms continuously. Even as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) left śrīvaikuṇṭam for his incarnation as vāmana, he kept thinking about seeking alms.

**pāṇiyāl nīrēṟṟup paṇḍorukāl māvaliyai māṇiyāyk koṇidilaiyē maṇ** – with the hand which you had used only for giving to others, you accepted the water which mahābali offered \[as a sign of giving what [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) sought\]. Once upon a time when mahābali had vowed only to give, did you not obtain earth as alms with the hands which had got scarred by accepting alms? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is asking whether he did not put an end to the enemy of indra who had attained him.

We shall take up the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 90. Mannulagam alene

*maṇṇulagam āḷēnē vānavarkkum vānavanāy viṇṇulagam tannagattu mēvēnē naṇṇit tirumālaic cengaṇ adiyānai engaḷ perumānaik kai toḻuda pin*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālai – the svāmy (lord) of pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); sem kaṇ – having divine eyes like reddish lotus; nediyānai – the supreme being; engaḷ perumānai – emperumān; naṇṇi – approaching; kai toḻuda pin – after vorśipping him with joined palms; maṇ ulagam āḷēnē – can I not administer this leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) under my control!; vānavarkkum vānavanāy – being the head of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); viṇ ulagam than agattu – all places in śrīvaikuṇṭam; mēvēnē – will I not enjoy great joy, comfortably!

**Перевод:**

**mannulagam āḷēnē – if one takes a step towards emperumān, is ruling the earth a great task? Have I not got that too!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “you are saying that I have killed kamsan; I have killed kuvalayāpīdam etc. You are only talking about my activities. What is it that you want? Say that.”. āḻvār says “Have I not received all the benefits!”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vānavarkkum vānavanāy viṇṇulagam tannagattu mēvēnē** – is being the head of nityasūris and being in śrīvaikuṇṭam a great task? Have I not got that too!

**vānavarkkum vānavanāy** – I will be as much greater than nityasūris as nityasūris are greater than samsāris.

**mēvēnē** – just as śrī rāma gave his sandals to śrī bharatāḻvān and asked him to look after the kingdom, I too got śrīvaikuṇṭam! Will śrīvaikuṇṭanāta not give his divine feet and grace us to be like him! Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had mercifully stated in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-11-uyvupayam/) “*vaiyam manni vīṟṟirundu viṇṇumāḷvar maṇṇūdē*”, will [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not make the two vibhūtis (nitya vibhūti and leelā vibhūti) mine! hanuman had mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116-24 “*artaśca mayā prāptā dēvarājyādayō guṇā: hata śatrum vijayinam rāmam paśyāmi sustitam*” (after seeing the victorious śrī rāma who had annihilated the enemies, all great things including the world of dēvas have been obtained by me). In the same way [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) too mentions that he has got both vibhūtis.

**vānavarkkum vānavanāy** – for the word *vānavar* instead of nityasūris, one can also construe dēvas such as brahmā et al and we can consider the meaning as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) saying that he got all the wealth such as brahmā et al.

**tirumālaic cengaṇ nediyānai engaḷ perumānaik kai toḻuda pin** – After approaching [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, emperumān who has the quality of motherly forbearance and emperumān who is our Lord, and vorśipping him with joined palms, is there any shortcoming in happiness and mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)? āren’t all these things present in emperumān himself?

**tirumālai** – did I approach brahmācāri (celibate) [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? Did I not approach *tirumāḷ* (consort of śrī mahālakśmi)?

**sem kaṇ nediyānai** – one who looks at those who worship him with lot of affection. Is emperumān not of the type mentioned in mahābhāratam uttara parvam 47-22 “*gōvindēti yadāgrandat kriṣṇa mām dūravāsinam ruṇam pravruddamiva mē hrudayānnāpasarpati*” (the calling out of draupadi from afar towards me did not leave my heart and was like a debt which kept increasing with interest over interest)!

**engaḷ perumānai** – did I attain a place which is not apt for me? Did I not approach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is very much related to me! Just as it is mentioned in śaraṇagati gadyam “*asman svāmin*”, is he not our svāmy (lord)?

**kai toḻuda pin** – isn’t it true that both the vibhūtis become ours after vorśipping him with joined palms!

We will take up the 91<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 91. Pinnal arunaragam

*pinnāl arunaragam sērāmal pēduṟuvīr munnāl vaṇanga muyalminō pannūl aḷandānaik kārkkadal sūḻ jñālattai ellām aḷandān avan sēvadi*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnāl – after the body falls down; aru naragam – the cruel hell; sērāmal – how to prevent from reaching there; pēduṟuvīr – Oh people, who are bewildered!; pal nūl – in different types of śāstras (sacred texts); aḷandānai – one who has been affirmed; kār kadal sūḻ jñālattai ellām aḷandān avan – emperumān who measured all the earth which is surrounded by dark oceans; sēvadi – the fresh divine feet; munnāl – now itself; vaṇanga – to worship; muyalmin – make an effort

**Перевод:**

**pinnāl aru naragam sērāmal pēduṟuvīr – naragam (hell) is waiting to cause suffering once the soul leaves the body. This world is already giving sorrows.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that once a person has subordinated himself to prakruti (primordial matter; here it refers to samsāram, the materialistic realm), it is not possible to avoid narakam (hell) once the ātmā leaves the body. Hence he tells us to make an effort to worship emperumān. He says, keep your head on the divine feet of emperumān and your feet on the head of yama. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pēduṟuvīr** – Oh people who are indulging in evil deeds,  ignorant of the impending sorrow, and now being bewildered out of repentance!

**munnāl vaṇanga muyalminō** – you cannot postpone this any further. You have to do it immediately. First worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and then carry on with any other work. Instead of giving excuses such as “We are children; we can do it later; we are young, what is the urgency now? we have become old, what is the use of doing it now?”, take a moment, decide, and even if it leads to a fracture of your limbs, go and fall at the feet of emperumān. Just as vibhīśaṇa hurriedly went, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-1 “*ājagāma muhūrtēna yatra rāmas sa lakśmaṇa:*” (he hurriedly reached the place where śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa were together), go immediately and fall at the divine feet of emperumān.

Is there any authentic source of information to reach him like this?

**pannūl aḷandānai** – he is confirmed in many sacred texts as the supreme being. He is attempted to be known through the collection of words. Has it not been said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 15-15](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/15-15/) “*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya:*” (only I am known through all the vēdas (sacred texts)) and in yajur āraṇya 3-11 “*sarvē vēdā: yatraikam bhavanti*” (in whom all the vēdas confluence together)!

**kār kadal sūḻ jñālattai ellām** – all the earth surrounded by dark coloured ocean.

**jñālattai ellām aḷandān** – There is no need to search for vēdas to know that he is the lord of all the worlds. It can be known from the fact that he is ruling all these worlds.

**aḷandān** – because you were asked to attempt \[to fall at this feet\], don’t wonder whether it should be done; do it. Since he did not shun anyone when he measured the worlds, not shunning \[him\] is what is required now also.

**sēvadi** – just as an infant seeks the bosom of its mother, go and worship his divine feet. Is it required to pay for mother’s milk? Drinking mother’s milk itself is the wage for the mother. Otherwise, will not the relationship between the mother and the infant become false?

**pēduṟuvīr** – Is there any point in being fearful now, after carrying out all the sinful deeds?

We shall take up the 92<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 92. Adiyal mun

*mun – before (kamsa could harm him) adiyāl – vit his divine feet kanjanai – kamsa seṟṟu – killed him by kicking amarar ēttum padiyān – one vho is vorśipped by dēvas kodi mēl puḷ koṇḍān – one vho has garuda on his flag nediyāṇ tan – sarvēśvaran vho has a deep memory namamē – reciting his divine names ēttumingaḷ – vorśip him ēttināl – if (you) vorśip him like tis tām vēṇḍum kāmam – te benefit tat one desires kadidu – quickly kāttum – (te divine names vill) grant*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**adiyāl – emperumān stamped on kamsa’s chest with his divine feet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that if we attain him, we will get all the benefits. He says that we have to attain him not only for not going to hell \[as mentioned in the previous pāsuram\], but also for getting desired benefits. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: adiyāl mun kanjanaic ceṟṟu amarar ēttum padiyān kodi mēl puḷ koṇḍān nediyān than nāmamē ēttumingaḷ ēttināl tām vēndum kāmamē kāttum kadidu*

**adiyāl** – he jumped on kamsa with his beautiful, divine feet. He had to keep his divine feet on his enemy kamsa when he should have kept them elsewhere \[refers to keeping them on soft, fragrant lotus flower\].

**mun kanjanaic ceṟṟu** – he sought out kamsa who wanted to cheat him and did to him what kamsa wanted to do to him \[i.e. killed him\].

**amamrar ēttum padiyān** – since kamsa was killed, dēvas such as brahmā et al were very happy that they had got back their tenements and praised [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Weren’t they the ones who lost their possessions because of kamsa!

**kodi mēl puḷ koṇḍān** – he has a flag only for protecting others.

**kodi mēl puḷ koṇḍān** – he is said to have *periya tiruvadi* (garuda) on his flag. īsn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) the one who has a unique identification for himself with one who came and attained him!

**nediyān than nāmamē ēttumingaḷ** – recite the divine names of one who has a deep memory and who will not ever forget us even if we recite his names just once and forget. īsn’t he the one who said in mahābhāratam uttara kāṇḍam 47-22 “*gōvindēti yadāgrandat kriṣṇa mām dūravāsinam ruṇam pravruddamivamē hrudayānnāpasarppati*” (the cry-out of draupadi when she called “*gōvinda!”* when I was very far away, has not left my heart even after so many years, and remains as interest on interest for a debt taken)! *nediyān* can also mean supreme being.

**ēttināḷ tām vēṇḍum kāmamē kāttum kadidu** – it will grant you all your wishes. It will give you everything including removing your hurdles, wealth, kaivalyam (soul enjoying itself) and [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself .

**kadidu** – unlike vorśipping other deities where the benefit will come much delayed, here the benefit will be granted immediately. *kadidu* could also mean ”worship him immediately”.

We shall take up the 93<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 93. Kadidu kodunaragam

*kodu naragam – te cruel hell kadidu – vill be so horrible tat ve cannot even see it piṟkālum – on top of tat seygai – te activities (of yama and his follovers tere) kodidu – cannot be borne enṟu – knoving tus adu kūdā munnam – before tat event happens \[to us\] vadi sangam koṇḍānai – one vho has te śarp conc in his divine hand kūndal vāy kīṇḍānai – one vho destroyed te demon kēśi by tearing apart his mout kongai nanju uṇḍānai – emperumān vho drank te poison from te bosom (of demon pūtanā) uṟṟu – attain him ettumin – vorśip him*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kadidu kodu naragam – the cruel hell will be horrible even to look at.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells us that before we attain hell we should worship emperumān who has unlimited sweetness and who removes our enemies. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: kadidu kodunaragam piṟkālum seygai kodidenṟadu kūdā munnam vadisangam koṇḍānaik kūndal vāy kīṇḍānaik kongai nanju uṇḍānai ēttuminō uṟṟu*

**piṟkālum seygai kodidu** – the activities there will be even more horrible. The followers of yama will indulge in activities such as throwing people in a river of blood, throwing them over grass which will be sharper than sword etc.

**piṟkālum seygai kodidu** – more than seeing hell, seeing their activities there will be even more horrible. Their \[followers of yama\] grotesque forms will be terrible to look at. *piṟkālum* means on top of that. Have the cruelties in hell not been mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-5-7 “*tennavantamar seppamilādār sēvadakkuvār pōlap pugundu pinnum vankayiṟṟāl piṇitteṟṟip pin munnāga iḻuppadan munnam*” (the followers of yama, who do not have any finesse in their actions, will drive the soul as if they are driving oxen; they will drag the soul upside down with their ropes)!

**kodidenṟu** – thinking that as being very cruel

**adu kūdā munnam** – before reaching that place \[hell\]

**vadi sangam koṇḍānai** – one who has the sharp śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch) on his divine hand. Do we need to talk about its sharpness since it is a weapon! ōr, if we take the sound emanating from the conch as referring to its sharpness, then we can say that this talks about the greatness of conch whose sound can kill the enemies. Has it not been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 1-19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/1-19/) “*sa ghōśō dārtrāśtrāṇām hrudayāni vyadārayat*” (the sound of kriṣṇa’s conch tore the hearts of drutarāśtra’s sons). ōr, we can construe that the word *vadi* also refers to its beauty. In other words, one who has the beautiful conch in his divine hand.

**kūndal vāy kīṇḍānai** – one who tore the mouth of the demon kēśi who came in the form of a horse. Horse is referred to us *kūndalmā* \[animal with hair\]. Since horse has hair on the nape of its neck, it is referred to as *kūndal****.***

**kongai nanju uṇḍānai** – one who killed pūtanā. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) killed kēśi in later years while he killed pūtanā as an infant. īsn’t destroying enemies a natural act for him!

**ēttuminō uṟṟu** – think of him through your heart and praise him with your mouth. After knowing that hell is horrible, if you worship him who removes enemies, he will remove the hurdle ‘hell’ for us.

**kadidu kodu naragam** – vasn’t even darmaputra \[yudiśtra\] bewildered after seeing hell!

We shall move on to the 94<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 94. Urru vanangi

*(You also, like tis) uṟṟu vaṇangit toḻumin – hold on to him, vorśip him and attain him (vitout leaving him)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**uṟṟu – approaching; instead of saying that we do are not qualified and leaving him, approach him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “Haven’t you seen how my heart worships emperumān! You also worship like that”. He implores us to attain emperumān who protects the world and who has unlimited sweetness. Just as the elders carry out worship first and then ask the children to worship, and just as the elders carry out activities such as sandyāvandanam and tell others to do likewise, āḻvār demonstrates vorśipping emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: uṟṟu vaṇangit toḻumin ulagēḻum muṟṟum viḻungum mugilvaṇṇan paṟṟip porundādān mārvidandu pūmpādagattuḷ irundānai ēttumen nenju ulagu ēḻum muṟṟum viḻungum – swallowing all the worlds without leaving out anything (so that deluge cannot destroy them) mugilvaṇṇan – one who has the complexion of cloud porundādān – iraṇiyan (hiraṇyakaśyap) who was not a match for him paṟṟi – catching hold of him pūmpādagattuḷ irundānai – emperumān who is sitting  in the beautiful divine abode of tiruppādagam en nenju – my heart ēttum – will worship*

**vaṇangit toḻumin** – fall at his divine feet without having any ego. Fall at his divine feet and hold on to him.

**ulagēḻum muṟṟum viḻungum mugilvaṇṇan** – ensuring that worlds such as earth do not go separately, keeping them in his divine stomach and protecting everyone because of the danger \[of deluge\]

**mugil vaṇṇan** – just as clouds let out rain without any distinction, irrespective of whether the place where it rains is land or sea \[i.e. raining everywhere\], [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also protects everyone without making any distinction. Just as a mother feels happy while nurturing her infant, emperumān’s divine form will feel happy because of protecting us. emperumān, whose form removes our fatigue and who has the quality of magnanimity. That he protects us will be known from his divine form itself.

**paṟṟip porundādān mārvu idandu** – the way [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) held iraṇiyan, who was no match for him, iraṇiyan fell down dead. He tore iraṇiyan’s heart. Alternative interpretation – the way emperumān held iraṇiyan, he thought that iraṇiyan would fall at this feet. But once he knew that he will not be agreeable for that, he tore his heart.

**pūm pādakattuḷ irundānai** – since then, emperumān has taken dwelling at the sweet divine place tiruppādagam (in kāncipuram) in order to remove the enemies of his followers.

**pūm pādakattuḷ irundānai** – it  is the divyadēśam (divine abode) where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) removes the deluge for his followers, which is samsāram, and creates deluge for their enemies, which is fear. Even though one iraṇiyan had been annihilated, since this world has many more iraṇiyans, he has taken residence here to destroy them too.

**ēttum en nenju** – my heart worships him. prahlāda (son of iraṇiyan) vorśipped narasimha, who incarnated briefly and disappeared. But this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is vorśipping the emperumān who has taken arcāvatāra (deity) form forever at tiruppādagam. This itself is the difference between the two.

**ēttum en nenju** – āḻvār says, you also worship him.

**pūmpadagattuḷ irundānai** – he has taken residence at tiruppādagam so that everyone can see how he had helped a child.

We shall move on to the 95<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 95. En nenjameyan

*dānavanai – te demon (iraṇiyan) val nenjam – strong cest kīṇḍa – one vho tore maṇivaṇṇan – one vho has te complexion of a blue coloured gem stone munnam sēy ūḻiyān – he vas everyting in te earlier period of creation ūḻi peyarttān – one vho destroyed everyting during te time of deluge ulagu ēttum āḻiyān – one vho is vorśipped by te people of te vorld and vho reclines in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) attiyūrān – one vho is residing in tiruvattiyūr en nenjam mēyān – he stood firmly in my heart en senniyān – he took residence in my head*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**en nenjam mēyān en senniyān – once āḻvār’s heart liked emperumān, emperumān made himself present in all the parts of āḻvār’s body. Even as āḻvār kept one part of his body with emperumān, he entered all the parts of āḻvār’s body.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān who is the reason for the creation of all the worlds and is the refuge for all the worlds entered all the parts of his body, from his dwelling place at tiruvattiyūr (kāncipuram). He talks about how he exists without knowing anything other than emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: ennenjamēyān en senniyān dānavanai vannenjam kīṇḍa maṇivaṇṇan munnam sēy ūḻiyān ūḻi peyarttān ulagēttum āḻiyān attiyūrān*

**en nenjamēyān en senniyān** – he is inside my heart. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 10-6-6 “*talaimēla tāḷiṇaigaḷ tāmaraikkaṇ ennammān nilaipērān ennenjattu eppoḻudum*” (my Lord, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), with lotus eyes, will never leave my heart once he keeps his divine feet on my head).

**dānavanai vannenjam kīṇḍa** – he tore the strong heart of iraṇiyan who proclaimed himself as God.

**van nenjam** – iraṇiyan had a heart which had no compassion either towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) or his followers. He had a heart which will not hesitate to carry out a disrespectful deed towards emperumān or his followers. Even when he saw the fearful form of narasimha, his heart did not soften. One can even assume that it was so hard that even cakrāyudam (emperumān’s divine disc) could not crack it open.

**maṇivaṇṇan** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has a form which removes fatigue, with the happiness that with  iraṇiyan destroyed, the enemy of prahlāda had been destroyed. This implies that emperumān’s form is like a blue coloured gem, being very soft and great.

**munnam sēy ūḻiyān** – Just as it is mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat “*sadēva sōmya idam agra āsīt*” (Oh child, this existed even before creation), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had both cit and acit (sentient and insentient entities) in subtle form, as his body, even before creation, when the world had been destroyed and they were waiting for their time. We can also construe this as emperumān who was present even at the time of deluge, preparing for creation.

**ūḻi peyarttān** – He destroyed them \[the worlds\]. He destroyed them since he did not get the benefit out of creating them. As the time approaches, it is he who destroys too.

**ūḻi** **peyarttān** – it can also be taken that it was [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who created everything indicated by time.

**ulagēttum āḻiyān** – he reclines in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) in the form of anirudda, to be vorśipped by everyone. ōr, it can be construed as emperumān who is holding the divine disc, to be vorśipped by everyone.

**attiyūrān** – one who is dwelling in tiruvattiyūr (kāncipuram)

**attiyūrān en nenjamēyān** – tiruvattiyūr is just like tiruppāṟkadal.

**āḻiyān attiyūrān en nenjamēyān** – just as [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) mercifully said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-10 “*ida vagaigaḷ igaḻndittu en pāl ida vagai koṇḍanaiyē*” (leaving aside your divine abodes, haven’t you taken residence in my heart!)

We shall take up the 96<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 96. Attiyuran

*puḷḷai ūrvān – one vho has periya tiruvadi (garudāḻvān) as his vehicle aṇi maṇiyin tutti sēr nāgattin mēl tuyilvān – one vho reclines on ādiśēśan vho has beautiful carbuncles and sveetly identified hoods muttī maṟaiyāvān – one vho is described by vēdas (sacred texts) vhic talk about te rituals vit tree types of agni (fire) mā kadal nanju uṇḍān tanakkum iṟai āvān – he is te svāmy (lord) for rudra (śivan) vho svalloved te poison vhic got generated during curning of te big ocean engaḷ pirān – our lord attiyūrān – residing at tiruvattiyūr \[kāncipuram\]*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**attiyūrān puḷḷai ūrvān – one who has garuda as his vehicle. śri kūrattāḻvān divinely mentions in his varadarājastavam 25 “ādirājayamadikam bhuvanānāmīśa tē bhiśu nayan kila mouḷi:” (Oh Lord, your crown gives out the fact that you are the lord of all the worlds!). Being the indvelling lord of tiruvattiyūr and having the vēdasvarūpi (one who manifests vēdas) garuda as his vehicle would indicate that he is the supreme being.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the one who liked him is the lord of both leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) and nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: attiyūrān puḷḷai ūrvān aṇimaṇiyin tutti sēr nāgattin mēl tuyilvān muttī maṟaiyāvān makadal nanjuṇḍān tanakkum iṟaiyāvān engaḷ pirān*

**puḷḷai ūrvān** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-8-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-8-1-odum-pulleri/) “*ōdum puḷḷēṟi*”, only he who rides on garuda and controls the worlds can be the supreme entity.

**aṇi maṇi in tutti sēr nāgattin mēl tuyilvān** – he reclines on tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who is decorated with carbuncles and has beautiful hoods. *aṇimaṇi* means beautiful gems. It refers to carbuncles worn beautifully by ādiśēśan. *in tutti* refers to the identification on the beautiful hood. *sēr* refers to ādiśēśan having the beauty to be appropriate as the mattress for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) since he has gems and identification on his beautiful hoods. *nāgattin* ***–*** tiruvanantāḻvān has all the qualities of being the mattress for emperumān – softness, fragrance, coolness etc. *mēl tuyilvān* – it is evident that one who has such ādiśēśan as his mattress is the supreme being. ḍidn’t kauśītaki upaniśat say “*amitaujā: paryanka: tasmin brahmā āstē*” (anantan who has excessive strength is the mattress; brahmam (supreme being) resides on top of that mattress)!

**muttī maṟaiyāvān** – vēdas talk about the three types of agnis (ritualistic fire) *gārhaspatyam*, *āhavanīyam* and *dakśināgni.* [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is vorśipped by the vēdas with the three *agnis.* Unlike other purāṇas and āgamas which were created by people, vēdas haven’t been created by anyone \[they are called as apauraśēyam, not created by any puruśa or soul\]. Thus, while the other purāṇas and āgamas may talk about other deities also, vēdas have affirmed emperumān as the supreme being.

If the text is taken as *mutti maṟaiyāvān*, it will indicate that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is affirmed as the means for attaining mōkśam (liberation) by the upaniśat part of vēdas.

**mā kadal nanjuṇḍān tanakkum iṟaiyāvān** – emperumān is the *īśvaran* (one who controls) for rudra who drank the poison which came first when the big ocean was churned (for obtaining nectar) and due to which , out of ego, he called himself as *īśvaran*. rig maṇḍalam 10, sūtram 136-7 says “*kēśa viśasya pātrēṇa yadrudrēṇa apibad saha*” (kēśava, who has beautiful locks of hair, drank the poison along with rudra, who was a container for that poison). rudra, who goes around saying that it was he who drank the poison, was just a vessel and was it not [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who as his antaryāmi (indvelling soul of rudra) drank that poison!

By saying *puḷḷai ūrvān……nāgattin mēl tuyilvān* which includes nityasūris garuda and ādiśēśan, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s lordship over nityavibhūti has been indicated. By saying *mā kadal nanjuṇḍān,* rudra, the head of samsāris, was referred, thus indicating emperumān’s lordship over leelā vibhūti. Since *maṟaiyāvān* was mentioned in between these two sets of words, it was indicated that vēda has affirmed his lordship over both the vibhūtis.  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says next that emperumān, without considering his superiority, came to tiruvattiyūr in order to help us.

**engaḷ pirān attiyūrān** – hasn’t such an [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) come to tiruvattiyūr in order to help us! By saying “*en nenjamēyān en senniyān”* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that emperumān, apart from having affection towards him, came to *hastigiri* (sanksrit name for tiruvattiyūr) to help all those who are connected to him either directly or through lineage.

We shall move on to the 97<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 97. Engal peruman

*tirumārbā – ōh one vho has periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) on his cest! sem kaṇ nedumāḷ – as te supreme being vit reddiś divine eyes imaiyōr talai magan – you are te lord of nityasūris engaḷ perumān nī – you are our lord kudamūkku il – having te kumbakōnam region kōyilāyk koṇḍu – tinking of it in your divine mind as te temple pongum pada mūkkin – having vell spread out hoods and nose āyiram vāy – one vit tousand mouts pāmbu – ādiśēśan (serpent mattress of emperumān) aṇai mēl – on top of tat mattress sērndāy – you reclined and blessed us*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tirumārbā – Oh one who has tiru (śrī mahālakśmi) on his chest! The opinion here is that the primary reason for emperumān to have us as his servitors is the recommendatory role played by pirātti. āḻvār is thinking of the first part of dvaya mahāmantram. parāśara bhattar in his śrī guṇaratna kōśam 44 had said “vēdāntās tatvacintām murabhidurasi yatpādacinhaistaranti” (vēdāntas (upaniśats) bring to an end the discussion about who is the supreme being by looking at the identification marks of pirātti’s divine feet on the chest of murāri (emperumān, the killer of mura)). In the same way, puruśa sūktam in uttara bhāgam has said “hrīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau” (śrīdēvi and bhūmidēvi are your consorts). Thus emperumān’s supremacy was established through his connection with his consorts.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that in order to bless him, emperumān came to tirukkudandai and took a reclining position, as a simple entity. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: engaḷ perumān imaiyōr talaimagan nī sengaṇ nedumāḷ tirumārbā pongu padamūkkil āyiravāyp pāmbaṇaimēl sērndāy kudamūkkil kōyilāyk koṇḍu*

**imaiyōr talaimgan** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/) “*ayarvaṟum amarargal adipati*” (he is the lord of nityasūris who do not forget anything). rig  vēdam says “*tadviṣṇō: paramam padam sadā paśyanti sūraya:* ” (nityasūris constantly keep looking at that great dwelling place of viṣṇu). The opinion in both these is that he is the supreme entity, being the lord of nityasūris. Next, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) enjoys the divine eyes of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with which he has enslaved the nityasūris.

**sengaṇ nedumāl** – cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6-7 says “*tasya yatākapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvam akśiṇī* ” (there are two divine eyes for the supreme entity, which are like lotuses blossomed by sun). *nedumāl* – very great entity. The opinion here is that it is clear that he is the supreme entity who is referred to by vēdāntam (upaniśat) going by his divine eyes.

**engaḷ perumān** – “to be our lord” is the opinion of [appiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/appillai/) (one of the vyākhyātas for this divya prabandam) while [periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/) as well as [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nampillai/) take it as an exclamatory (calling out) part.

**nī** – you, who are having such greatness as well as simplicity. *pāmbaṇaiyin mēl sērndāy* is supposed to be its continuation.

**kudamūkkil kōyilāyk koṇḍu** – having the divine abode tirukkudandai as his temple. *kudamūkku il* refers to the region of tirukkudandai.

**pongu pada mūkkin āyiravāyp pāmbaṇai mēl sērndāy** – the well spread out hoods which ādiśēśan has due to contact with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), and his noses as well as his thousand tongues with which he is ready to praise emperumān due to the resultant happiness. emperumān is reclining in a divine manner on such ādiśēśan. periyavāccān piḷḷai’s opinion is that apart from blessing us in standing posture at tiruvattiyūr \[previous pāsuram\], emperumān has taken position in tirukkudandai in reclining posture with a vow that he will not get up until he uproots samsāram, with his greatness and simplicity. appiḷḷai’s opinion is that just as he enslaved us showing his beauty in tiruvattiyūr, he enslaved us showing his beauty in tirukkudandai.

We shall take up the 98<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 98. Kondu valarkka

*at tat time uḷ odunga – śrinking vitin (in a corner) uṇḍadu – ate as food ulagu ēḻum – te seven vorlds! (Hov amazing!)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**kōvalanāy – the supreme being, emperumān, came in the clan of covherds who do not know the difference between left hand and right hand \[they are so ignorant\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār enjoys the activities of the one who blessed him with his grace. He says that if one were to look at the way emperumān, who controls all the worlds, blends freely with a lowly samsāri such as himself, this would also appear to be one of those great activities of emperumān. It can also be said that āḻvār is saying that just as world cannot exist without emperumān, emperumān also cannot exist without him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: koṇḍu vaḷarkkak kuḻaviyāyt tān vaḷarndadu uṇḍadu ulagēḻum uḷḷodunga koṇḍu kudamādik kōvalanāy mēvi ennenjam idamāgak koṇḍa iṟai kōvalanāy – being born as a covherd kudam koṇḍu ādi – dancing with pots on hand and head and, in the same position en nenjam mēvi – getting into my heart idamāgak koṇḍa iṟai – the lord, considering( my heart) as the temple for himself koṇḍu vaḷarkka – enabling others to cuddle and nurture him kuḻavi āy – as an infant tān vaḷarndadu – he grew up mercifully*

**mēvi** – fitting well into that clan. When he was asked by the covherds, after seeing his superhuman activities (including lifting the gōvardana hill for seven days to protect them and their cattle), he said, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-13 “*nāham dēvō na gandarvō na yakśō na rākśasa:* I *aham vō bādavō jāta: naicintyamitō’nyatā:* īī” (I am not a dēva or gandarva or yakśa (all these three are different types of celestial beings) or rākśasa (demonic clan). I was born as your relative; you should not think in any other way). He affirmed that he is one among the covherds since he fitted so well in their clan.

**kudam koṇḍu ādi** – being born in the clan of covherds, he danced their traditional dance with pots. Dancing with pots on their hands and heads is typical of covherd people.

The word *mēvi* could be taken together with *en nenjam* and meaning for this would be:

**kudamādi en nenjam mēvi** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that even as kriṣṇa came dancing in the street with pots, seeing the involvement of āḻvār in his activity, he entered the heart of āḻvār and carried on with the dance in his heart.

**idamāgak koṇḍa iṟai** – the supreme being entered the hard heart of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) which will not easily melt for anything, made it melt by his dance and thus made āḻvār’s heart as his dwelling place. āḻvār says that not only did [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) have the soft quality of taking āḻvār’s heart as his residence, but he also has the quality of *aghatitaghatanā sāmartyam* (ability to perform impossible tasks) since he melted āḻvār’s heart which was stronger than stone, iron , diamond etc. In the same breath, āḻvār mentions another *aghatitaghatanā* *sāmartyam* of emperumān …

**koṇḍu vaḷarkkak kuḻaviyāyt tān vaḷarndadu** – did he not recline on the tender banyan leaf as a child who would be caressed and nurtured by everyone! [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) was moved so much by his innocent form of an infant on the tender banyan leaf that he praised him in his [tiruvāimoḻi 4-2-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thiruvaimozhi-4-2-1-balanay-ezhulagundu/) ”*bālanāy ēḻulaguṇḍu parivinṟi ālilai anna vasam seyyum aṇṇalār*”and in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-7-10-adiyarndha/) “*adiyārnda vaiyamuṇḍu āḷilai anna vasam padiyādumil kuḻavi*” (he reclined on a tender banyan leaf as an innocent infant with none to care for him, after swallowing the worlds)

What were the activities that he indulged in as an infant? ……

**uḷḷodunga uṇḍadu ulagēḻum** – what he ate as an infant, and which would occupy a small corner of his little stomach, were all the worlds! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is amazed at this activity of the infant swallowing all the huge worlds,  making them rest in a corner of his stomach and lying on a tender banyan leaf. *ēḻulagam* is indicative of all the worlds in samsāram.

We shall take up the 99<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 99. Irai emperuman arul

*imaiyōr – dēvatās (suc as brahmā, rudra et al) emperumān – ōh our lord! iṟai – ōh supreme being! aruḷ enṟu – please śov mercy (in our matter), so saying muṟai ninṟu – standing in te form of servitors moy malargaḷ – beautiful flovers tūva – scattering tem in a disorderly vay aṟai kaḻala sēvadiyān – having divine feet adorned vit varrior’s anklets sem kaṇ – having reddiś eyes nediyān – being superior to everyone else māl – having affection tovards his follovers kuṟaḷ uruvāy – incarnating in te form of vāmana (dvarf) māvadivil – vit mahābali maṇ koṇḍān māl – measured te eart*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**iṟai emperumān aruḷ enṟu – Oh supreme being for the world and our ordained Lord! Show us mercy.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār talks about how emperumān carries out the activities for dēvatās who desire other benefits \[and not emperumān\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: iṟai emperumān aruḷenṟu imaiyōr muṟai ninṟu moymmalargaḷ tūva aṟaikaḻala sēvadiyān sengaṇediyān kuṟaḷuruvāy māvadivil maṇ koṇḍān māl*

**iṟai emperumān aruḷ enṟu** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-2-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-2-10-kalva-emmaiyum/) “*kaḷvā! emmaiyum ēḻulagum ninnuḷḷē tōṟṟiya iṟaiva!* ” (Oh the mischievous lord who created us and all the worlds within you! Oh our lord!) the dēvatās will worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**aruḷ enṟu** – if the people living in the town find that one of their belongings has been stolen, they will assemble at the house of the head of the town who protects them and cry out about the loss of their property/possession. In the same way, these dēvatās will also come to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is their protector and plead with him if their possessions / lands are taken away by demons such as rāvaṇa et al. Who are these people who come and pray…..

**imaiyōr** – these are the people mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoḻi 2-2-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-2-10-kalva-emmaiyum/) as “*veḷḷēṟan nānmugan indiran vānavar* ” (dēvatās such as rudra, brahmā, indra et al); they are the people, who have their own worlds, vehicles, flags, weapons etc \[granted by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] and who, blowing their own trumpets, will call themselves as *īśvarōham* (I am the Lord). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says that they leave aside their ego and trumpets and come and pray to emperumān.

**muṟai ninṟu** – they will know that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the lord and that they are the servitors, and holding their hands together, will bow down to emperumān.

**moymmalargaḷ tūva** – just as their followers worship them with beautiful flowers, these dēvatās will worship emperumān with beautiful flowers.

**tūva** – in their anxiety that whatever they have prayed for now should be granted , they will sprinkle the flowers, not at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) but haphazardly on his head.

What does emperumān do when they worship him like this?

**aṟai kaḻala sēvadiyān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who stands with his warrior’s anklets sounding reverbrantly on his divine feet. Even as they are praying, he gets ready to do their bidding. *kaḻal* is indicative of all other ornaments worn by him on his divine feet.

**sengaṇ nediyān** – one who has lotus like eyes, indicative of the fact that he is the lord for all these dēvatās, as mentioned widely in sacred texts – śvētāsvatara upaniśat says of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “*tam īśvaraṇām paramam mahēśvaram  tam dēvatānām pramamca daivatam* ” (he is the mahēśvaran for all these īśvarars and he is the dēvatā for all these dēvatās).

**nediyān** – he is the greatest among all.

**māl** – even though they come to him for helping them with lowly benefits, he doesn’t frown at them but showers affection on them like water flooding the channels once the sluice gates are opened in a lake. He is affectionate towards them in the hope that at some point of time in the future, they will come to him, asking for himself instead of such lowly benefits.

Did he not lower his status while carrying out their tasks!

**kuṟaḷ uruvāy** – the opinion is that in order to carry out the task for such followers, he did  not take a great form consistent with his greatness of giving everything to others, but took the form of a dwarf (vāmana) for whom taking alms from others is the ordained duty.

**māvadivil maṇkoṇḍān** – he took a huge form \[after being granted his wish by mahābali\] capable of swallowing all the worlds, in order to measure the worlds \[and get back what his followers had lost\].

**kuṟaḷ uruvāy māvadivil** – Just as [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) mentioned in his tiruccanda viruttam 109 “*surukkuvāinṟiyē surungināy  surungiyum perukkuvārai inṟiyē perukkameydu peṟṟiyōy* ”, he dwarfed himself and grew to very large extent of his own volition, without anyone asking him to do so.

We shall take up the 100<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 100. Male nediyone

*mālē – ōh one vho is affectionate (tovards his follovers)! nediyōnē – ōh one vho cannot be estimated \[as tis muc\]! viṇṇavarkku mēlā – ōh one vho is te lord of nityasūris! viyan tuḻāyk kaṇṇiyanē – ōh one vho is adorning te tuḷasi garland! kaṇṇanē – ōh one vho vas born as kriṣṇa! mēnāḷ – once upon a time viḷavin kāy – vood apple fruit (inside vhic a demon had entered) kanṟināl – vit a calf (inside vhic anoter demon had entered) vīḻttavanē – ōh one vho felled it yān udaiya – tat ī have (tovards you) anbu – te affection entan aḷavanṟu – does not remain vitin me*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**mālē – one who is superior to everyone else! We can also construe this as one who has excessive affection towards his followers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “you have mentioned about how others approach me for other benefits. What is it that you want me to do for you?”. āḻvār says “shrink the affection that I have for you so that I can hold it”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: mālē nediyōnē kaṇṇanē  viṇṇavarkku mēlā viyan tuḻāyk kaṇṇiyanē  mēlāl \[mēnāḷ\] viḷavinkāy kanṟināl vīḻttavanē enṟan aḷavanṟāl yānudaiya anbu*

**nediyōnē** – one who cannot be estimated. This can also be construed as one who has boundless affection.

**kaṇṇanē** – the qualities mentioned earlier can be seen in kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa). One who is bound to his followers; one who controls everyone.

**viṇṇavarkku mēlā viyan tuḻāyk kaṇṇiyanē** – this mentions about the absence of limit to his wealth. One who is superior to nityasūris and who is donning the garland of tuḷasi as a manifestation of his wealth. It says how [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is donning the divine garland on his shoulders and is manifesting his beauty to nityasūris.

**mēlāl \[mēnāḷ\] viḷavin kāy kanṟināl vīḻttavanē** – earlier, he had plucked a wood-apple fruit using a calf. Demons \[sent by kamsan to kill kriṣṇa\] had entered the two objects, wood apple and calf. By throwing the calf at the fruit, kriṣṇa hit one against the other and destroyed both \[demons\] of them. This is mentioned to show that he will destroy our enemies too like this.

**enṟan aḷavanṟāl yānudaiya anbu** – the affection that I have towards you has grown too big and I am unable to enjoy you due to this. You have to shrink the size of this love so that I can contain it within myself. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is speaking like hanuman who said, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 40-16 “*snēhōmē paramō rājan tvayi nityam pratiśtita: bhaktiśca niyatā vīra bhāvō nānyatra gaccati* ” (the deep affection that I have towards you is firmly stabilised in me.  My devotion towards you is also permanent. My thoughts do not go elsewhere). The *māl* (affection) that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has towards [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is because of the affection that āḻvār has towards him. āḻvār is unable to contract that affection. emperumān showed himself to āḻvār. When emerpumān told āḻvār to carry out service to him with affection, āḻvār is trying to sustain himself by saying what he had said at the beginning \[of this prabandam\] “*anbē tagaḷiyā ārvamē neyyāga*”

Alternatively, once love becomes excessive, isn’t the next stage having direct vision (*sākśātkāram*)! Hence, we can construe that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) is having direct darśan of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) by saying “*tirukkaṇḍēn ponmēni kaṇḍēn*” in the next prabandam.

periya pirātti periya perumāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
bhūtattāḻvār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
emperumanār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
nampiḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
periyavāccān piḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
jīyar tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
appiḷḷār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
